# The v4.5 prompt bank: the evidence behind every family (2026-10-08)

Nine analyses, one agent each, over the export of 2026-10-08 (`web/scripts/export-annotation-review.ts`: 1,685 judgments of which 637 between the two pooled Gemini arms, 213 speaker corrections of model answers on train prompts with mechanical token diffs, 1,446 speaker-authored train answers, the 249 unjudged v4.4 train answers with surface metrics, per-prompt informativeness). No frozen gold left the database. Annotators are pseudonymised annotator_1..8 as the export assigned them. Speaker texts are quoted as evidence; Igala forms are data. Each finding names the prompts (promptId) and the speakers it rests on.

Companion files: `web/prisma/seed-prompt-bank-v45.ts` (the prompts, with every family's rationale on the row), `tasks/salem-lydia-writeup-2026-09-25-inventory.md` (the write-up's 82 rules), `tasks/prd-salem-writeup-ingest-2026-10-08.md`.

## The bank in one table

v4.4 has already removed what the speakers tagged most often on the bare and retrieval arms: Yoruba vocabulary, nobody's words, hyphenated prefixes, meta-sentences about the article and respelled proper names show zero or near-zero hits in its 249 train answers. What is left falls into three classes, and the v4.5 bank is built around them. First, structural habits the speakers correct in one token and v4.4 still has: a ki-form with no job or in the wrong shape (ki before plural ma, kì between chained verbs, ka kakini doubled, no linker where a noun is modified), the incompletive a written as a separate word, ef' and ñw' contractions that no speaker writes, wẹ as a subject, tone marks on particles only and the spelling Ígáláà, the formal na tẹnẹ frame in intimate speech, and six-line template prose where speakers write one chained sentence. Second, community formulas and words the model never learned and the speakers converge on with five to seven voices: good night, welcome back, take care, condolence, the reply to thanks, the completive V kpa mẹ, food, seventy, under, next week, money units in the market. Third, the rules Salem Ejeba and Lydia Wiernik named that the arena cannot yet measure because no prompt has ever asked for them as contrasts: the complementiser kí against the relativiser kì, one variety per answer with the variety named, the Ogwugwu r for central l, tone marked everywhere or nowhere, expanded forms beside contracted ones, and the restricted number agreement of sit and carry. Every family therefore takes one of two shapes: a one-sentence task where the target is a single visible token that a speaker can swap and name, or a minimal pair or ladder in one prompt where the model fails measurably if its two lines do not differ where the speaker's do. Every family carries controls where the rule must not fire, so over-correction is counted alongside under-correction. The shapes that produced ties and unexplained both_inadequate verdicts (note any variation, naturally with no scene, long genre pieces, say whether you changed anything, English exegesis beside the Igala, single-word tone requests) are retired. 108 prompts, 18 families, namespace ig_v45_<key>_NNN, train split, none held out.

| family | n | buckets | failure targeted | rule elicited |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| comp | 10 | grammar_tone | A ki-form emitted with no job or in the wrong shape, and the write-up's complementiser against relativiser contrast that gold cannot show because speakers write toneless ki in both slots. v4.4 writes a standalone ki-form in 113 of 249 train answers (4.41 per 100 words against 3.50 in gold) and the speakers' edits remove it 23 times and add it 18 times, so placement is the fault. Forms: annotator_7 cut 'Ù mọ̀ ọdọ kí ma bi mi n̄' to 'Ù mọ̀ ọdọ ku ma bi mi ñ' (ig_v42_auth_010); annotator_5 rewrote 'kí wẹ nọ́ t'[house]' as 'kẹ tọnọ t'[house]' with 'Amọma kẹ tọnọ not Amọma ki wẹ ma' (ig_v42_gram_027); annotator_3 replaced 'Lo t'òde kì tá jẹ́nì' with 'Lo kẹ nya ti jẹ́nì' (ig_v42_lex_013); annotator_5 cut 'ka kakini' to 'kakini' with 'Say that in igala is kakini' (ig_v42_gram_006); annotator_7 inserted a relativiser the model lacked, 'Idah chí [goat] ka ki de èfù Kogi State' (ig_v42_gram_036); on ig_bank_gram_030 four relativiser shapes appear in gold (ka annotator_6, kà annotator_3, kì ... lé annotator_8, ki annotators 4, 5, 7) and v4.4 writes 'Onokẹlẹ kì a lo t'[money] ñwu lẹ leku'; on ig_bank_gram_022 nine gold answers close no relative clause with lẹ while v4.4 writes 'Ujẹu ki [mother] mi hi lẹ ch'urinyọ'. Gold writes ki 461 times, kì 139, kí 22 (18 from annotator_8, who also puts kí on relatives, ig_v42_gram_036), so the tone contrast is unobservable without a designed pair. | The clause after say, tell, know, hear or 'the news' is opened by an overt linker (kakini / ka ki ni in community writing; kí in the write-up) and never dropped; a noun modified by a clause takes a relativiser (kì or ki, ku before the plural clitic ma, and the community's ka and ka ki variants to be counted); two actions of one subject chain with kẹ, not ki; a ki with no clause after it is an error; whether speakers hear two different words in the belief reading and the fact reading of 'glad that' and 'own up that'; whether lẹ closes a relative clause at all. |
| vari | 8 | dialectal_fidelity | Fabricated area attributions and mixed varieties, invisible to the arena because no gold names a variety. On the 15 'note any variation' prompts the pooled arms got 12 decisive of 56 judgments, 33 both_inadequate and zero explanations with Igala in them; the model named Dekina five times and Ankpa ten, speakers never name Dekina. v4.4 still attributes: 'Abọ Idah a ka kini Wa mọ, muda abọ Ankpa a ka kini Lia mọ' (ig_bank_dial_015) reverses annotator_8's 'Idah kakì lẹya kẽ jen̄wù, Ankpa kpaì ogugu kakì wá kẽ jen̄wù, Ibajì kakì lẹwa kẽ jamìn̄wu' (ig_bank_dial_007) and annotator_7's 'Abọ Igala okpakpa a ka ki ni liya ẹmi abo ọgugu a ka ki ni wẹ mi' (ig_bank_dial_004); it invents an Ibaji pronoun 'mi a lọ ti [money] (ig_bank_dial_007) and fills annotator_6's template 'Uchẹ ki bọ aka kini X abeki bọ aka kini Y' with the unattested 'abawe' (ig_bank_dial_005). v4.4 uses wa for come in six answers where four Idah speakers write lia ([child] mi lia kẹ jẹñwu' annotator_7, 'Amọma mi mẹ lia j'[food] mẹ' annotator_6, ig_bank_auth_014). The gold dialect field is unset on 688 of 1,446 rows and otherwise confounded with the annotator (ankpa = annotator_8 only, other = annotator_3 only). | One variety per answer; the variety is named when the prompt asks; never attribute a form to a town without a source, and 'I do not know the <town> word' is a correct answer. Markers to collect per speaker-named town: come (lia/liya against wa against lẹwa), eat, my sibling (omaye against omada), my mother ([mother] against iya), thank you (agba, awa, anya, nago), how are you, sit down. |
| erel | 4 | dialectal_fidelity, orthography | The Ogwugwu r for central l, which the arena has never collected: the gold dialect field says ogugu on 2 of 1,446 rows and no speaker has produced an l/r pair. What the data has is the reverse: r-forms leaking into central sentences and every editing annotator restoring l, 16 hunks on 11 prompts, 14 of them on the retrieval arm. annotator_5: 'Woman in Igala is onobulẹ not onobirẹ' (ig_v42_auth_010); annotator_3 'Ónobirẹ' to 'Ónobulẹ', 'Wrong spelling' (ig_v42_lex_005); annotator_7 'nokẹ́rẹ ... a chira' to 'onokẹ́lẹ ... ma chiya' (ig_v42_gram_029); annotator_5 'ch'ónokẹ́rẹ' to 'ch'ọnẹkẹlẹ' with 'Ọnẹkẹlẹ not ọnẹkẹrẹ' (ig_v42_reg_016); annotator_5 'Ẹnẹ ki a lẹ ñwu not ẹnẹ ki a rẹ ñwu' (ig_bank_auth_013), which is also what the served NEVER WRITE line does to 'ra' for buy, a dialect-blind ban. annotator_7 is the only speaker who names Ogugu with a form of its own, 'abo ọgugu a ka ki ni wẹ mi' (ig_bank_dial_004). | In the central variety the words for see, rat, roll, ladle, woman, young man and buy have l; an r in those words belongs to the Ogwugwu variety and must not appear inside a central sentence; whether any current annotator knows the Ogwugwu forms, and whether 'ra' for buy is Ogwugwu rather than Yoruba to them. |
| loan | 6 | lexicon_disambig, orthography | Loanword policy running against the speakers. Following its rules 9 and 10, v4.4 keeps English letters for common nouns (mathematics, social studies, biology, economics, password, camera) and calques others ([house] ogwu' for hospital, 'ẹnẹ [money] i che' for farmer, ig_v42_gram_023, ig_v42_gram_037), while the repair round deletes its own s-respellings. Speakers split three ways on the same word: annotator_5 respells with Igala letters ('I ch'ukọchẹ ẹkẹnọmiks', 'Ecnomics is English not igala', ig_v42_lex_004; 'Paswọdú is igala meaning your password', ig_v42_lex_008), annotator_6 substitutes an Igala word ('Ẹ kì du igede wẹ ñw'ẹnẹ du-n', ig_v42_lex_008), annotators 4 and 7 keep the English ('computer science', ig_v42_gram_005, where annotator_4 wrote 'komputa chienci'). Old loans keep their s in gold (asibiti, státì) while annotator_5 also wrote 'Hospital in igala is achíbítí' (ig_v42_lex_012); annotator_7: 'They is no s in Igala Alphabet' on 'sáyíkọ́lọ́jì' (ig_v42_lex_005). Names are solved: 'We just write it as Lagos' (annotator_4, ig_v42_orth_001), Zainab and Vivian kept by every editor. Decisive share on the loan prompts was 0.75 (ig_v42_lex_003, ig_v42_lex_004, ig_v42_lex_014). | s becomes ch in Igala words and in loans the community has adopted; a person, place or institution name keeps its letters; for each loan class (old everyday loan, new technical noun, school subject) the community verdict is keep, respell or replace; teacher, farmer, hospital, photograph and medicine have community words that are neither calques nor English. |
| tone | 8 | orthography | Partial tone marking and the spelling Ígáláà. v4.4 carries at least one tone mark on 172 of 249 answers but the median marked share is 0.09: five tokens copied from the grammar block (Ígáláà 89 times in 46 rows, kì 75, kà 32, á 25, yí 25) in otherwise unmarked prose ([child] mi, [god] kì lo kpai uwẹ. [god] kì ch'[hand] wẹ' against annotator_6 [frozen answer], ig_bank_auth_009). Speakers never write Ígáláà (0 of 636 gold; Igala 96 times; removed in 22 edits, 'Ígáláà' to 'Igala' annotator_6 ig_bank_auth_035, to 'Ígálá' annotator_5 ig_v42_reg_001). Speaker marking is bimodal: annotator_8 marks 89 percent of answers, annotator_3 32 percent, annotators 4, 5, 6, 7 between 0 and 16; when they mark they mark most words. Edits strip marks in 119 of 213 and add in 23, eight of those on numerals under an explicit request (annotator_7 'Ógwú Ógwú-ẹ́gwā Ọ̀gbọ̀méji' against annotator_5 unmarked 'Ogwu Ogwuẹgwa Ọgbọ mẹji' on the same ig_v42_orth_011). The tone_marks tag was applied to 112 outputs that HAD marks (annotator_6 'Some tonal mark were incorrect', ig_v42_auth_004; annotator_3 'The tone mark was not required', ig_v42_auth_001). Minimal pairs are the only shape where speakers mark deliberately and gloss (ig_bank_orth_028, ig_bank_orth_029 gold). | An answer is unmarked (community default) or marked on every word when the prompt asks; never particles only; the name is Igala, never Ígáláà; dotted vowels are letters and always written; when marks are asked for, which convention the speakers follow (acute high, grave low, mid unmarked, or a macron for mid) and whether the marks agree across annotators on the same sentence. |
| expd | 6 | orthography, grammar_tone | Contractions the community does not write, and the write-up's expanded form that nobody has asked for. v4.4 writes ef' 22 times (0 in 636 gold, efu written in full 34 times), ñw' 20 times (0 in gold; ñwu 107, ñwi 60), k'a 4 times where speakers fuse to ka ('Íchẹñwu k'a ma kakini' against annotator_6 'I chẹñwu ka ma kakini', annotator_5 'I'jeñwu kama kaki', ig_bank_gram_031), and 1.31 apostrophes per answer against 0.75 in gold. Speakers contract attested verb-noun joints ('Gba ọla' to 'Gbọ'la', 'Gbọ not gba', annotator_5, ig_bank_auth_008; 'kpa [hand] to 'gw'[hand]' three times, ig_v42_gram_030) and expand wrong ones ('Ubo k'u á chaj'u?' to 'Ugbo u neke chaji ñwu?', annotator_5, ig_v42_lex_006; 'ef'ádṣa' to 'efu [market], annotator_6, ig_v42_gram_004; 'ch'ọ́w'àwọ́tọ' to 'chí [hand] awọtọ', ig_v42_lex_018). The same junction is written three ways by seven speakers on 'if it rains' (k'[water], kì [water], Komi, ig_bank_gram_016). Edits run 30 expand against 29 contract, so contraction is not an error to them; the write-up's learner form has never been collected. | Elision at a vowel-vowel joint is licensed, not obligatory, and only at attested verb-plus-noun collocations; efu, ñwu and ñwi are written in full; ki plus a (we) is written ka; a clitic or a noun-initial vowel is never clipped; the learner form writes every word out, and the speaker can say which full words a contraction stands for (which tests the write-up's ambiguity claim about k'). |
| concd | 7 | grammar_tone | Restricted number agreement, never tested as a pair. Subject side: the project treated gwùgwú as unattested but the community uses it in 11 gold rows ('Ogìjo Agba, gwugwu anẹ' annotator_8, 'Gwugwu anẹ nago' annotator_6, ig_bank_reg_014; 'Gwugwu ane or gwu ane' annotator_8, ig_bank_dial_017), and one gold has the plural verb with the write-up's adverb ('ku mẹ wa jeju jọ emi yi', annotator_6, ig_bank_reg_031); 'here' is emi for three annotators (wa emi, liya ẹmi, Liy'emì, ig_bank_dial_004) while the model wrote mímí, efí, málí and v4.4 'ugboí', 'ibai'. Object side: 'Mẹ kó [yam] lẹ du t'ane [tree] corrected to 'Mẹ kó [yam] lẹ jọ ofe [tree] with 'Under the tree in igala is ofe [tree] not t'ane [tree] (annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_035); v4.4 learned jọ but writes 'jọ anẹ [tree] lẹ'. di appears in 8 edits where the model had du or gba ('dù òkìtì' to 'di òkpẹti' annotator_7 ig_v42_gram_034; 'gbà kẹkẹ' to 'di añya' ig_v42_gram_026; 'ch'ẹñwu unyẹdọ' to 'di unyẹdọ' annotators 4 and 5 ig_bank_auth_018). The only four-object plural in gold is annotator_8's 'ám'ẹnwu ku ma ko kì [child] wa' (ig_v42_lex_014). No singular/plural inanimate pair has ever been put through one verb, and the holdout ig_bank_gram_010 asks for 'correct agreement' with no contrast. | Sit and be located agree with the subject in number (one verb for one, another for many); here is emi; take or carry is du for one thing and kó for many, and the placing verb after it agrees (tẹ for one, jọ for many; tinyo/rinyo for throw away); di is give or hand over; no other verb changes for number and none for person. |
| negq | 6 | grammar_tone | The negator and the yes-no particle. v4.4 writes 'Ma á j'ẹñwu ñ' for 'They are not eating' where all seven speakers write the bare verb plus one nasal with no á (Ma jẹñwu-n annotator_6, Ma jẹñwu'ñ annotator_5, Mà jẹñwu ǹ annotator_3, Ma jẹnwu nọ annotator_7, Mà jẹnwu nóò annotator_8, Ma jẹ'ñwu no annotator_4, ig_bank_gram_019); it closes a yes-no question with a nasal ('Abọ [house] wẹ ń?', ig_bank_auth_011) where speakers close with a, ha, ba or fá ('Abọ akwora [house] wẹ a?' annotator_7, 'ch'ọlafiya ba?' annotator_6, 'Abọ [house] wẹ fá' annotator_8). The pooled arms' ǹ and n̄ were rewritten ñ ('i just changed the tone mark', annotator_7, ig_v42_lex_008) and idle nasals deleted ('U n̄ kọ ẹkpa [name] ñwu n̄' cut to 'U kpilọ [name] ñwu n̄', annotator_6, ig_v42_orth_006; 'kí n ka n?' to 'ku ka ba?', annotator_7, ig_v42_gram_028). Embedded negatives take ma before the verb and still a final nasal ('wẹ ma ch'íya kpa ma n?' annotator_5, 'wẹ ma chiya kpai ama ñ?' annotator_7, 'wà ma chìya ... n?' annotator_8, ig_v42_gram_029), which the served line 'ONLY a clause-final nasal' forbids. 31 edits change the count or spelling of a final nasal. | The negative is the bare verb plus one clause-final nasal, no preverbal á; the nasal's written shape (ñ, -n, 'ñ, nọ) is recorded per speaker, not legislated; a prohibition is (subject) ki plus verb plus the nasal; a negative inside a why- or that-clause takes ma before the verb and the nasal at the end; a yes-no question closes with a final particle (ba, a) and never with a nasal unless it is negative. |
| pron | 6 | grammar_tone, register_honorifics | Pronoun shape, person and addressee number. v4.4 puts the possessive or full wẹ before the verb in 13 rows ('Ugbo wẹ alo?' against seven gold 'Ugbo ẹlo', ig_bank_gram_021; 'Wẹ chẹ gbọ ọla ku ka lẹ?' against annotator_5 'Ẹ wú kú ka yí che nẹrejú?', ig_v42_gram_028), and the bare arm's 'Wẹ kpa [hand] wẹ' was rewritten 'Ẹ gw'[hand] wẹ' (ig_v42_gram_030); 1sg u for 3sg i: 'Í dẹ he was there Not U dẹ meaning i was there' (annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_013). Addressee number: 'Because wa is more than one person and wẹ s use for one person' (annotator_5, ig_bank_auth_001); 'ẹ wá jẹ [king] to 'mẹ wa jẹwñu' (annotator_4, ig_bank_auth_014); 'Mẹ wọla'ule' beat the singular 'Ẹ lálé o' 2 to 0 (ig_bank_auth_036). The 3sg possessive is served as -wn but five of six gold write a free word ('Ìmì [food] dù ñwì [mother] ñwù' annotator_3; [mother] ñwu annotators 4, 5, 7; [mother] un annotator_8; [mother] wñ annotator_6, ig_bank_gram_018). People plural am' corrected to abo ('Am'Ajaokuta' to 'Abo Ajaokuta', annotator_6, ig_v42_gram_006). A one-phrase answer fronted with a full pronoun ([water] chi ene ekegweji' against annotator_8 'Ẹnẹ ẹkegwèjì', ig_v42_gram_022). | Subject and object pronouns are short clitics (u, ẹ, i, a, mẹ, ma); the full forms appear only for emphasis, in coordination or as a one-word answer; to one person ẹ and wẹ, to several or to an elder mẹ, for us wa; the possessive after a noun is written as its own word (ñwu, with wñ and -wn as variants to be counted); the people-plural is abo plus noun. |
| aspec | 5 | grammar_tone | Tense and aspect frames the speakers converge on and v4.4 lacks. 'He has finished eating' is V kpa mẹ for all seven speakers ('Oñwù [food] jẹkpá mé' annotator_8, 'I jẹ kpa ma' annotator_6, 'Ì fùjẹñwù jẹ kpà mẹ' annotator_3, 'i fi [food] jẹkpa mẹ' annotator_5, 'E fu [food] jẹ kpa mẹ' annotator_7) while v4.4 writes 'I chẹ jẹñwu kpa' (ig_bank_gram_024). The incompletive a is fused to the verb 135 times in gold and separate 30 (annotator_6 46 to 1) while v4.4 separates it 168 times and opens 63 sentences with 'Ma a', a string that appears in no gold ('Ogijo lẹ a n'ọla am'[child] lẹ' against 'Ogijo lẹ agwa abimọtọ lẹ', ig_bank_gram_017). Future and intention are na with any subject ('a na loti okoñ' annotator_5 and 'a na lọtọ [money] ñ' annotator_4, ig_bank_gram_016; 'Mama na li' annotator_7, ig_bank_gram_036; 'Ná á lọ' annotator_8, ig_bank_gram_033) where v4.4 writes 'a lo t'[money] ñ'; the counterfactual is ma ('a ma dẹdẹ' four annotators, ig_bank_gram_031); the conditional keeps ki after chẹñwu for seven of seven ('Chẹñwu k'[water] lọ') while v4.4 drops it ('Ichẹñwu [water] a lọ'). | Bare verb is simple past; the incompletive a is written fused to the verb; na marks future or intention with any subject; ma marks the counterfactual; 'has finished V-ing' is V kpa mẹ; 'already' is chẹ ... mẹ; 'if' is chẹñwu ki; a habitual third-person statement is one chained sentence, not a list opening 'Ma a'. |
| formu | 10 | authenticity | Fixed speech acts composed instead of known, the largest both_inadequate block in the arena: 24 of 29 judgments on ig_bank_auth_002, 004, 008, 011 and 028 were both_inadequate, mostly 'Both are wrong' with no form, yet the gold on the same prompts was unanimous. Good night: six speakers write the till-morning phrase (Chọ'[morning] annotator_7, Ch'[morning] ke-oo annotator_6, Ch'[morning] annotator_3, Ch'[morning] annotator_4, 'Good night in Igala is Cho dudu' annotator_5) and v4.4 writes 'Wọla [name] from the greeting frame (ig_bank_auth_004). Welcome back: 'Wọla ulẹ' (annotators 4, 7; 'Welcome in igala is wọla ulẹ' annotator_5) against v4.4 'Nnọ, [road] lẹ' (ig_bank_auth_002). Take care: 'D'eju tọla wẹ' from five annotators against 'Nẹju kp'ọla wẹ' (ig_bank_auth_008). Condolence: [god] ki di añyi ẹdọ wẹ' (annotator_7), 'W'ọla akwuli' (annotator_6), 'Nago not Nno' (annotator_5) against v4.4 'Nnọ, [god] kì chẹ ñwẹ' (ig_bank_auth_018); 'Nnọ is igbo not igala' (annotator_5, ig_v42_reg_012). Reply to an elder's thanks: 'Àgbá [mother] ékìkwúra-n' (annotator_7) against 'Awa, [mother] (ig_v42_reg_013). Arrival hail: 'Agba not Agoo' (annotator_5, ig_v42_reg_015); the host's reply pair 'Wọla ulẹ / Agba nago' (annotator_7, ig_v42_cult_002). Blessings: speakers bless in one clause ([god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ' annotator_6, [frozen answer] annotator_3, ig_bank_auth_001) where the bare arm wrote five optative clauses and v4.4 four lines plus 'Àmí' (ig_bank_auth_033). Where an arm did know the formula it won 10 of 11 (ig_bank_auth_007, ig_bank_auth_023). | A greeting, farewell, wish, condolence or congratulation is one fixed phrase said once, not a sentence to build: good night is the till-morning phrase, welcome and welcome back use the wọla frame with its slot noun, take care has its own formula, sorry is nago and never the Igbo word, the arrival hail is Agba oo with a vocative and the host answers with the welcome or Agba nago, a blessing is one clause of the may-God frame whose pronoun matches the number of people blessed. |
| regis | 6 | register_honorifics | Register weight misplaced, the write-up's 'words too strong or too childish for the context'. v4.4 opens 12 answers with the formal announcement frame na tẹnẹ ku kà ... ka ki ni, to a mother listing school subjects ([mother], na tẹnẹ ku kà ñw'ẹ ka ki ni ma kọ [child] mi mathematics ...' against annotator_8's plain list, ig_v42_lex_002), to a family gathering ('Na tẹnẹ ku kà ñwu mẹ ka ki ni ọdọ onobulẹ ogijo ...' against [mother] kì chì ogìjo alu olókó ì chì Ógwúmẹ́lū', ig_v42_gram_015), to a friend in town ('My guy, na tẹnẹ ku kà ukọla ñwẹ today', ig_bank_dial_010); speakers use the frame in 2 of 636 gold, both on-air, and v4.4 omitted it on the one radio prompt where it belongs (ig_v42_auth_003, annotator_7 'Mẹ gba chakadu na tẹnẹ ku ka ñwu mẹ ka ki ni ...'). The bare arm piled titles and three blessings on a thank-you ('Agba kakini! ... [god] ki chẹwun nwẹ. [god] ki dabi fi kọ wẹ. [god] ki k'[hand] t'[child] wẹ mọ', cut to one blessing by annotator_5, ig_v42_reg_014) and used 'Ọdọfa' ('Ọdọfa is not igala', annotator_5, ig_v42_reg_013); 'You dont say agba wẹ you can say agba lẹ' (annotator_5, ig_v42_reg_011). The served ban on every alaafia shape conflicts with five annotators' reply to 'how are you' ('uchọla'fia na'go' annotator_7, 'Uch'ọlafiya nago' annotator_6, 'U chọla'fia nago abogijo' annotator_5, ig_bank_reg_013; 'chọ'lafía bá?' annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_027). | The announcement frame opens public announcements and formal requests to elders or a gathering; to a parent, spouse, friend or child the answer starts with the content and at most a vocative; one blessing per utterance; the reply hail takes no possessive (Agba o, Agba lẹ); the health formula in the reply slot is community Igala and the Yoruba ban must be narrowed to the question frame; the honorific plural mẹ to an elder or a group. |
| money | 6 | grammar_tone, lexicon_disambig | Prices, counting and contested numerals. Both pooled arms failed the market exchange and the trader's call (ig_bank_auth_013 five of six both_inadequate, ig_bank_auth_024 five of five). v4.4 renders 1,500 naira as 'Naira icham nyogwoko nyoke ọgbọ mẹ́lu ugbo mẹ́lu' where annotator_5 writes [market] ñwu chí naira Akpulu mébie ny'oje' (ig_v42_gram_019), and answers the thousand-naira question with 'ma á kà One thousand naira' where annotator_7 writes 'Nara íchámû nyú ọ̀gwáọ́kọ́' (ig_v42_lex_028). Speakers count money in unit words the rows lack: annotator_7 'Pam'ẹlu = 5, Pam'ogwu = 40, Pam'oje = 100, Akpulu ká / Ọgwọ́kọ́ = 200, Akpulu mélu = 1,000' and annotator_8 'kọbọ káà, jẹkà káà (200 naira), dẹlì káà (20,000)' with 'gbalukà [money] leẹ́' for count the money (ig_bank_dial_011). Numerals: 'Megwejo megwela' corrected to 'Ẹgwẹjọ ẹgwẹla' by annotators 5 and 7 (ig_v42_gram_021); '85 in igala is ọgbọmẹlẹ ñyọ mẹlú Not ogwẹlẽ kpai ẹlu' (annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_020); 70 split between 'ẹ̀tẹẹ̀gwa' (annotator_5) and 'ọ́gbọmẹta nyọ mẹgwā' (annotator_8, ig_v42_gram_018); 100 between 'Ógwúmẹ́lu' (annotator_5) and 'Ọgbọ mẹ́lu' kept by annotator_7 (ig_v42_orth_012); 40 between 'Ọ̀gbọ̀méji' and 'Ọgbọ' (ig_v42_orth_011). Numeric prompts were decisive (2 of 3 on ig_v42_gram_018, 020, 021). | Market prices are said in money units (the 200-naira unit and its multiples, the smaller pam' and kọbọ units) with the vigesimal numeral after the unit, not as an abstract numeral string; counting objects uses the vigesimal series; eleven to nineteen take no mẹ- prefix; 40, 70, 100 and 1,000 need three or more annotators to settle; how much is this and is that the last price have fixed buyer lines. |
| words | 7 | lexicon_disambig | Word-level items the speakers corrected with near-unanimity that v4.4 still misses, several in repaired rows. Food: 'I du ùjẹu lẹ ñw'[mother] wn' (v4.4) against 'I d'[food] lẹ ñwu [mother] wñ' (annotator_6, ig_bank_gram_018), 'Jẹñwu not jẹu' (annotator_5, ig_bank_auth_014), ùjẹu in five v4.4 rows. Seventy: v4.4 'etegwa' against '70 in igala is ẹ́tẹẹ̀gwa' (annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_018). Under the tree: v4.4 'jọ anẹ [tree] lẹ' against 'ofe [tree] (annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_035). Next week: v4.4 'aladi ojonwawn' where ojonwawn was removed in three edits and replaced by 'Aladi ki ya wa i' (annotator_7, ig_v42_lex_009). Bucket: 'Bọkiti is wrong Bọkẹti is right' (annotator_5, ig_v42_lex_014). Harmattan: v4.4 'Oye chi afufu ...' against 'Ekọ afu ọwẽ chi ochu ẹkẽgwaka' (annotator_7, ig_v42_auth_012). Age grade: v4.4 'abo otu' against 'abo [head]-ẹgọ' (annotator_6) and 'ami agbadufu' (annotator_7, ig_bank_cult_038). Yam: v4.4 'ọ́kà okilì' against [yam] (annotators 7 and 8, ig_bank_cult_002). Stranger: 'ilowa' against 'ọnọnọjọ' (annotator_7, ig_bank_cult_024). Wait: 'dẹ de mi' against 'agba dago d'eju mi' (annotator_6, ig_v42_auth_001). Late: 'gbéé' against 'gbanẹ' and 'kpọpẹ' (ig_bank_auth_027). Lives in: 'fodo do' against 'dodo' (annotators 3 and 6, ig_v42_auth_004). Death heading: 'Úkú' against 'Úkwu' (annotators 5 and 7, ig_v42_auth_011). Outside: t'òda / t'[name] / ọdoda from three annotators against 't'òde' (ig_v42_lex_013). Yes and no: three different pairs from three annotators and the model's 'Ẹn / Ẹñwẹñ' matching none (ig_bank_dial_006). | A short lexicon for the GRAMMAR block, each item attested by three or more speakers: food, seventy, under, next week, bucket, harmattan, age grade, yam, stranger, wait for me, be late, live at, death, outside, yes and no; and the blocklist extended with the forms speakers removed (ujẹu, etegwa, ojonwawn, Oye, otu, ọ́kà okilì, ilowa, t'òde). |
| locat | 4 | grammar_tone | Locative and determiner slots the rule names but the model still misses in new sentences. 'in' before a town: 'Musa dodo ef Idah' corrected to 'Musa dodo efẹwọ Idah' (annotator_7, ig_v42_gram_001), 'efu Abuja' to 'efẹwọ Abuja' by annotators 4 and 7 and 'efewo' by annotator_3 (ig_v42_gram_005), 'ma bí ma méjì lẹ efu Ankpa' to 'efẹwọ Ankpa' (ig_v42_gram_003); v4.4 still writes efu before a capitalised town in 9 answers and ef' before a consonant in 5. Destination: five annotators replaced the invented 'àwéhì tì Lokoja' with lo + ti spelled five ways (loti, leti, lotì, le tì, lo ti, ig_v42_gram_006); the 'toward' construction 'Ì lo tẹju [hand] ánẹ idah' is a single attestation (annotator_8, ig_v42_dial_001). Determiner: [name] lẹ' to 'yí' for this road (annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_025) and 'yí' to 'lẹ' for the town (ig_v42_lex_026). Clause linker 'oñ' rewritten 'ya' by three annotators and 'tákí' by annotator_5 ('Oñ is a wrong word', ig_v42_gram_011, ig_v42_gram_005); 'right now' needs preverbal ya ('Ì ya dẹ́ èf'[money]', annotator_5, ig_v42_gram_037). | efu before a state, a year or a common noun, efẹwọ before a town, ef' only before a vowel and never a bare ef; a destination takes ti after the motion verb and the fused spelling is to be collected not legislated; toward without arrival has its own construction to confirm; lẹ is the and yí is this; then and now are ya or taki, never oñ. |
| dirct | 3 | lexicon_disambig, dialectal_fidelity | Cardinal directions, where the speakers run two systems and the model invents. Bare arm [hand] anẹ Idah' corrected to [hand] apata Idah' with 'South' (annotator_5, ig_v42_lex_026) and Yoruba 'Áríwa ... Òkè' judged 'Both are not correct' (annotator_7, ig_v42_lex_015); retrieval arm 'Ọ̀wọ̀čo' deleted ('Ọ̀wọ̀čo is not igala', annotator_5, ig_v42_lex_016) and [hand] East Kogi State' rewritten [hand] apa baki [sun] [morning] Kogi State' (annotator_7, ig_v42_lex_021); v4.4 wrote 'ọ̀wọ̀cho' in a repaired row (ig_v42_gram_007) and 'Ọgẹbẹ' for north. Gold on ig_bank_lex_028 holds two systems: opata south, ojukpale/ukpale north, apa or apo east, ichi west (annotators 3, 5, 6, 7) against ọpata atẹ north, ọpata ọganẹ south, [sun] [morning] east, [sun] anẹ west (annotators 4 and 8); the four-at-once prompt produced five incompatible lists with no explanation. South has four-annotator convergence on opata/apata. | A direction is a side word plus a landmark and the words differ by area (two attested systems); each direction is collected one at a time from a speaker who names their town; south is the anchor (opata) and north, east and west are open. |
| provb | 4 | idioms_metaphor, cultural_values | Proverbs and fixed texts coined on the spot and attributed to the Igala. The retrieval arm's [hand] ki d'[money], onwu a j'ẹñwu' was rewritten [hand] ki ch'ukọklọ, onwu a j'ẹñwu' (annotator_7, ig_v42_idiom_002); the bare arm's [money] wá ra ẹ́nya wẹ ... ẹ́dọ́ wẹ a fù' corrected to 'l'ẹ́ñwu ... ẹ́dọ́ wẹ a bọ' with 'To buy in igala is lẹñwu' (annotator_5); 'Good thing in Igala is ẹñwu ẹnyọ not k'ẹñwu ñyonyo' (annotator_5, ig_v42_idiom_001). The 140 gold answers on the 39 ig_bank_idiom prompts converge on a stable repertoire (the stubborn fly, the snake in the hole, the child who washes his hands eats with elders: 'Imọtọ f'[hand] ñwu gwẹ fẹ ya b'ogijọ jẹñwu' annotator_6, ig_bank_idiom_008) that no Gemini arm has ever been scored against. Folktale opener: the retrieval arm's 'Ita mi de' was corrected to 'Alo mi de' with 'Alo is tale or story; Ita is proverbs' (annotator_3), and three annotators gave 'Alọ mi kpa kpa kpa' (ig_bank_auth_029); the lullaby was invented by both arms where three annotators recited the same song (ig_bank_auth_032). English exegesis in the same answer laundered coined proverbs and produced ties. | A proverb must be one the community says; alọ is a tale and ita a proverb; a folktale opens with the storyteller's call and the listeners' response; a fixed song is recited, not composed; the answer is Igala only with no English exegesis. |
| expos | 2 | cultural_values | Template expository prose. v4.4 opens cultural answers with 'X chi ẹñwu ọgbọgaga ọjanẹ Ígáláà' (ọgbọgaga 27 times against 4 in gold), writes five or six lines (2.37 sentences per answer against 1.58), adds 'Àmí' and bracketed English, and asserts content the speakers deny: 'Ọ́kà okilì chi ùjẹu ọgbọgaga ọjanẹ Ígáláà' against annotator_7 [yam] anẹ Igala chi ẹñwu ku ma jẹ i che ọfọfo' with the gloss that yam holds no special place for the Igala (ig_bank_cult_002); 47 words on pottery against annotator_7's eleven (ig_bank_cult_025); 'hospitality is a very good thing in Igala land' against annotator_7 opening with the scene 'Chẹñwu ki ọnọnọjọ lọ tu [house] ẹnẹ Igala, ma gbi ọnọnọjọ lẹ kpai [hand] mẹji' (ig_bank_cult_024). Speakers write one comma-chained sentence linked by ki, ku ma, kẹ and manyi. The open essays got 19 judgments in total and taught one register fact. | An expository answer is one or two sentences in the speaker's own voice, begins with what happens, carries no importance opener, no Amen and no bracketed English, and chains its clauses the way speakers do. |

### Rules the writers followed

- English prompt text is 25 to 420 characters, addressed to the speaker as 'you', one task per prompt. A pair, triplet or ladder counts as one task only when every line shares the same verb or scene and the prompt says how many lines to write.
- Ask, do not tell. A prompt never asserts an Igala form we have not verified; where speakers disagree (tone, negator spelling, 40 and 100, directions, loans) the prompt asks and the family metadata says plainly that we hold no rule there.
- Igala may be quoted in a prompt only from these sources, and every quote is cited in the family's igala_allowed_in_prompt: the served v4.4 prompt text; a grammar row marked community_verified, by row id; speaker gold or edits where three or more annotators converge, with promptId and annotators; the Ejeba and Wiernik write-up of 2026-09-25, as a question and never as a correct form. Never a model output, never a form from frozen (holdout) gold. Every prompt text passes checkStatic in web/src/lib/eval/leak-guard before seeding; a quoted form that trips the guard is replaced by its English gloss.
- Restate the write-up in English. No prompt reproduces a sentence or glossed example from it; its l/r pairs and the written form k'ọla are the only forms borrowed, and only inside questions.
- No em dash anywhere, in prompt text or metadata. Use a comma, a colon or parentheses. No emojis. No 'please'. No 'naturally', 'authentic' or 'as a native speaker would' as the only constraint.
- Every prompt fixes the scene: who is speaking, to whom, where and at what moment, or what fact the sentence must carry. A speech act names the addressee's number (one person, a group, an elder). A translation names a person and a place.
- Every prompt caps length ('one line', 'one sentence', 'at most two sentences', 'three lines') and, where a long genre is in play, asks for one named part of it.
- Answers are Igala only. Where a family needs an English note (which word differs, which syllable is high, kept or respelled), the prompt asks for it as one separate line after the Igala, never mixed into it and never as an explanation of the image or meaning.
- Never the clauses 'note any variation between areas', 'say whether you changed anything', 'if such a custom exists', 'say plainly if you are unsure', or 'explain in English what the words picture'. Variety is collected by asking the speaker to name their own town in brackets, not by asking what other towns say, except in the two labelled ignorance probes.
- Vary names and places across Igala land and beyond: Idah, Ankpa, Anyigba, Ayangba, Dekina, Ejule, Ibaji, Ogugu, Lokoja, Ajaokuta; Abuja, Kaduna, Lagos, Port Harcourt, Kano. Personal names drawn from the region (Ojochide, Onoja, Audu, [god], Amina, Zainab, Blessing, Esther Sule) and never the same name twice in one family. No name that appears in an existing bank prompt is reused in the same role.
- Every family carries at least one control prompt where the rule must not fire (a sequence with no embedding for the linker family, a verb that must not agree, a sentence with no vowel junction, a proper name that keeps its letters, a word that does not vary by area, a scene with no formula), and the metadata flags it as a control so over-correction is scored separately from under-correction.
- Solved problems get controls only: proper-name orthography, efu before a state, lo ti before a town, the Yoruba and nobody's-words lists, the ka kakini doubling in isolation. One regression token inside a larger sentence, never a prompt of its own.
- No near-duplicate of the 589 existing prompts: Jaccard over content words below 0.55 against every existing text and against every other new text; the writer checks this before seeding, as the v4.2 seed did.
- Namespace ig_v45_<key>_NNN with the family key in the id; split train, isHoldout false, create-only upsert with update: {}; bucket is the family's first listed bucket unless the metadata assigns a prompt to the second.
- Difficulty follows the family's stated mix and is recorded per prompt; a control is never the hardest prompt in its family.
- Routing is part of the design: every pair goes to at least one of annotator_5 or annotator_3 (the two who write 'X wrong, Y right' with forms) plus one other; tone-marked prompts also go to annotator_8 and annotator_3; variety-labelled prompts go to every annotator so the town brackets populate the dialect field; annotator_8's ties are read as gold-in-explanation, not as judgments.
- The 20 to 30 prompts in the tone, variety, formula and negation families whose gold is one short spoken line are tagged record-ready, so the audio feature the write-up asks for has its first targets when it is built.
- Every prompt is unseen: not a paraphrase of a training prompt, not a sentence whose gold v4.4 could retrieve, since 25 of 249 v4.4 answers were 60 percent copies of a speaker text on the same prompt.

### Informativeness rules (from the judgments themselves)

- Use: one-line fixed speech act with the scene fully specified (speaker, addressee, hour). Decisive 75 to 100 percent on ig_bank_auth_001, 005, 007, 009, 010, 015, 023; explanations carried the corrected form ([god] ki Dẹnyọ ñwu wẹ', 'Welcome in igala is wọla ulẹ'); gold unanimous across six or seven annotators on good night and welcome. Add a word-for-word gloss line so a composed sentence is exposed.
- Use: one-sentence translation with a named person and place and exactly one grammatical target. ig_v42_gram_001 3 of 4 decisive, ig_v42_gram_002 4 of 4, ig_v42_gram_006 3 of 6 plus five edits; the edit is one token with a stated reason ('Loti - gone to', 'Say that in igala is kakini').
- Use: numeric prompts with the number stated in the English (age, headcount, price, date, counting aloud). 2 of 3 decisive on ig_v42_gram_018, 020, 021 with exact-form corrections ('70 in igala is ẹ́tẹẹ̀gwa', '14th is ẹgwẹlẹ not ẹgwẹfa').
- Use: minimal pairs, triplets and ladders in one prompt (statement, negative, question; one object, many objects; is doing, did, has finished). ig_v42_gram_030 produced a five-segment glossed edit; ig_bank_gram_033 produced glossed paradigms from four annotators; ig_bank_gram_024 seven of seven agreeing gold. The model fails measurably when its lines do not differ where the speaker's do.
- Use: commands with a countable object and a spatial target. ig_v42_gram_035 and 034 drew the only rule-stating explanations on agreement ('Under the tree in igala is ofe [tree] not t'ane [tree], 'Outside in igala is ugbọda not [house]).
- Use: reported speech with the matrix verb varied and the embedded subject different from the matrix subject. ig_v42_gram_006 was the most edited prompt in the grammar bucket (5 edits, 6 judgments).
- Use: two-turn exchange (what they say, what you say back). Even where both arms failed, the gold came back as a labelled pair that teaches the whole formula (ig_v42_cult_002, ig_v42_reg_010).
- Use: the register ladder (one fact, five addressees as five prompts). Speakers used the formal frame on both radio prompts and on no intimate prompt (ig_v42_auth_003 against ig_v42_lex_002, ig_v42_gram_015), so the pattern is judgeable rung by rung.
- Use: a spoken sentence with one to three modern loan nouns and a separate English line on how each was written. ig_v42_lex_003, 004, 008, 014 were 0.75 decisive; the English line made both the model and the speaker commit ('They is no s in Igala Alphabet').
- Use: variety-labelled production (one concrete utterance, the speaker names their town in brackets). Where speakers named areas themselves the answer was usable (ig_bank_dial_014 'idah Igala: [child]-[mother] mì / Ankpa Igala: [child]-ìya mì'); the dialect field otherwise stays unset or follows the annotator.
- Use: a prompt whose two answers can differ in one dimension only. ig_bank_auth_012 (identical words, marks only) isolated the variable and showed word order mattered and marks did not.
- Avoid: 'give the word for X and note any variation between areas'. 0.13 decisive on 15 dialect prompts, 33 both_inadequate, zero Igala in explanations, three edits; the model fabricates attributions and the speaker rejects both with 'Both are wrong'.
- Avoid: 'write how an Igala speaker would naturally ...' with no scene. 27 of 39 authenticity bank prompts; 0.34 decisive, 53 of 88 both_inadequate; gold diverged because speakers imagined different scenes (ig_bank_auth_016, 020, 026). The same acts converge once the scene is fixed (ig_bank_auth_004 against ig_bank_auth_016).
- Avoid: long genre compositions (wedding speech, family prayer, market dialogue, consolation exchange, praise poem, folktale, lullaby, four-turn dialogue). 18 of 28 verdicts where both answers exceeded 100 characters were both_inadequate with verdict-only explanations; edits were wholesale rewrites at similarity 0.0 to 0.2 (ig_bank_reg_033, ig_bank_auth_005, 012, 017, 030, 039).
- Avoid: meta prompts ('say whether you changed anything from the English spelling', 'rewrite and note what changed', acronym expansion questions). The model answers with sentences about the article and the speaker deletes them (21 of 27 META hunks from six orth prompts); the lesson is one bit already in rule 9 (ig_v42_orth_001, 002, 006, 008, ig_bank_auth_037).
- Avoid: an English explanation of the image or meaning in the same answer as the Igala. It launders coined proverbs and absorbs the edit (ig_v42_idiom_001, 002; ig_v42_lex_027 where the speaker edited the English note instead of the Igala).
- Avoid: single common nouns 'with correct spelling and tone marks'. 13 of 15 gold for morning are unmarked and the two marked ones disagree; 35 such prompts exist; the comparison degrades to a dot check (ig_orth_001, ig_bank_orth_018, 020, 023).
- Avoid: abstract or modern topics in three or four sentences judged once (democracy, the internet, health insurance, what a computer is). Both_inadequate with 'Both are wrong', gold leans on loans and is itself unsure (ig_v42_auth_008, ig_v42_lex_007, ig_v42_lex_012).
- Avoid: open cultural essays ('describe the role of X in Igala culture'). Gold runs 11 to 198 words and disagrees on content; v4.4 answers with one template; 19 judgments on the whole bucket (ig_bank_cult_013, 014, 030, 032, 036).
- Avoid: hedge-permitting clauses ('if such a custom exists', 'say plainly if people use the English number'). The model takes the exit ('U m'ọla yí ñ', a digit) while the speaker answers the question, and the judgment is both_inadequate with no rule learned (ig_v42_dial_002, ig_v42_lex_028, ig_v42_cult_002). The two labelled ignorance probes in the variety family are the one deliberate exception, scored on the decline itself.
- Avoid: all four directions, all yam kinds or three confused words in one prompt. Gold lists were long and mutually incompatible with no explanation (ig_bank_lex_028, 031, 033).
- Avoid: routing a prompt only to annotator_8 or only to annotator_7. annotator_8 tied 29 of 29 grammar pairs and 16 of 16 lexicon pairs; annotator_7 gave 3 explanations in 42 grammar judgments (but 29 edits). Explanations with forms come from annotator_5 (29 'X not Y' rationales) and annotator_3.
- Scoring rule for both_inadequate: it counts as a finding only when the explanation or an edit names a form; the UI should ask verdict-only annotators (6, 7) for the form before accepting the verdict, since 70 of 99 both_inadequate explanations in the authenticity bucket contained no Igala.

## Authenticity (243 pool judgments, 58 edits, 220 gold)

Data read: Read in full with the Read tool after dumping each JSON row to text: review-split/comparisons-authenticity.json (243 rows, every prompt, both answers with arm, winner, confidence, explanation, failure tags, annotator, batch), review-split/edits-authenticity.json (58 rows with originalText, correctedText, rationale and segments), review-split/gold-authenticity.json (220 rows with dialect field and English gloss), review-split/prompts-authenticity.json (118 prompts with comparison and gold counts), review-split/prompt-informativeness.json filtered to bucket authenticity (53 prompts), scratchpad/prompt-v4-4.txt (served system prompt), scratchpad/grammar-rows-oct8.json (30 rows), scratchpad/writeup-oct8.txt (Salem Ejeba and Lydia Wiernik). Then ran two counting scripts (scratchpad/auth_counts.py, auth_counts2.py) over the same files for: per-arm occurrence of Nnọ, Ígáláà, é- prefixes, ṣ, alafia shapes, ọjà, [hand], ra, má, pẹlu, n̄, kí and kì; tone marks per word per arm and per gold annotator (acute, grave, macron only, dot-below excluded); edits that reduce or add tone marks; ki-family tokens per annotator in gold; apostrophe contractions per annotator; negation spellings in gold; verdicts by answer length; explanation style per annotator; both_inadequate per prompt with annotators.

### authenticity 1. Fixed-formula speech acts are composed instead of retrieved. For good night, welcome back, take care of yourself, asking after the family, goodbye and congratulations the community has one set phrase and the gold converges on it (unanimous on 002, 004, 008). Neither arm produced the phrase, so 24 of 29 judgments on 002, 004, 008, 011 and 028 were both_inadequate. Where the rag arm did retrieve the phrase (007 Wọla ulẹ, 023 Wọla [child] tito) it won 10 of 11.

- Rule a speaker would state: A greeting, farewell, wish or congratulation is a fixed phrase, not a sentence to build: good night = Ch'[morning] (literally till morning), welcome back = Wọla ulẹ, take care of yourself = D'eju tọla wẹ, how is your household = Abọ akwọra [house] wẹ a?, goodbye = Ch'ẹgbatugba or Ch'[god] omunẹ (till another day), congratulations on a child = Wọla ọjilẹ [child] ẹtitọ, please (before a request) = Agba. Say the phrase once; do not add clauses.
- Count: 41 judgments, 5 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly. The greetings row gives the (w)ọla + slot-noun frame (Wọla ulẹ, wọla ukọlọ), the farewell row gives the Ch'ugba t'ugba family and Ẹ lọ kpaì ọla fiya, the thanks row gives anya and Agba. NOT told: Ch'[morning] for good night (worse, the greetings row lists night among the wọla slot nouns, while every speaker said ch'[morning], so v4.4 may push a wrong form), D'eju tọla wẹ, Abọ akwọra [house] wẹ a, Wọla ọjilẹ for congratulations, Agba as please. For the told items the new prompt must test whether telling worked; for the untold ones it must collect the phrase.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_002 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Wọlẹ dẹ (rag); Nnọ. (bare)` -> speaker `Wọla ulẹ (annotator_4 G8, annotator_7 G11), W'ọla ulẹ (annotator_6 G10), Wọla ulẹ ooo, ulẹ a'ke (annotator_5 G9)`. "Welcome in igala is wọla ulẹ"
  - ig_bank_auth_004 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[name] ényọ̀ (rag); [name] kí d'ọ́la. (bare)` -> speaker `Ch'[morning] (annotator_4 G21), Ch'[morning] (annotator_3 G20), Chọ'[morning] (annotator_7 G25), Ch'[morning] ke-oo (annotator_6 G24), [father] cho dudu (annotator_5 G23)`. "Good night in Igala is Cho dudu"
  - ig_bank_auth_008 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Gba ọla wẹ nyọnyọ. (bare); Nẹju kp'ọla wẹ (rag)` -> speaker `D'eju tọla wẹ (annotator_3 G45), De'ju tọla wẹ (annotator_5 G47), D'eju tẹ ọla wẹ (annotator_6 G48), Dẹ'ẹju tọ'ọlawẹ (annotator_7 G50), Dẹju tóla wá nyọ nyọ (annotator_8 G51); edit E10 annotator_4: Nẹju kp'ọla -> dẹju t'ọla`. "D'eju tọla wẹ (the whole explanation is the corrected form)"
  - ig_bank_auth_011 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Aba abọ [house] wẹ? (rag); Àbá kẹ́ abúnyí wẹ? (bare)` -> speaker `Abọ akwora [house] wẹ a? (annotator_7 G68), Akwọra [house] wẹ a (annotator_3 G64), Abo akwọra [house] wẹ ch'ọlafiya ba? (annotator_6 G67), Abọ [house] wẹ fá (annotator_8 G70); edit E15 annotator_4: Agba abọ [house] wẹ ha?`. "Aba - wrong / Agba- right"
  - ig_bank_auth_028 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Agba ùjijì, lo kpai'ọlafia (rag); Agbá [god] wéwé. Nyánẹ́ ùwe. [god] kí d'[hand] wẹ, àwa a l'ọ́la. (bare)` -> speaker `chẹgbatugba, chẹ'ko kẹju de, ch'[god] munẹ, ch'ikọ munẹ (annotator_4 G157), Ch'[god] omunẹ (annotator_6 G158), Ch'ẹgbatugba ẹ lọ kpaì ọla fiya [god] kì dìde wẹ (annotator_8 G160)`. (bare)` -> speaker `Wọla ọjilẹ [child] ẹtitọ kẹ bi (annotator_7 G128), W'ọla ojile [child] titọ (annotator_6 G127), Wọla [child] etọ (annotator_3 G126)`. "Congratulations on your new born"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One-line speech act with the situation fully specified and the addressee count stated, and a second sentence asking for the literal meaning of the set phrase (so ch'[morning] = till morning becomes checkable and a composed sentence is exposed). Gold on this shape was unanimous across annotators, so each hit or miss is a clean signal; explanations on this shape carried the correct form (annotator_3, annotator_5).
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your neighbour is going to bed. Say good night to her in one short Igala phrase, the set phrase people actually use, then give its word-for-word meaning in English.
  - Your brother is leaving for Abuja tomorrow morning. Tell him, in one Igala phrase, to take care of himself.
  - You meet a friend you have not seen for a month. Ask after his household in one Igala question, the way it is said on the road.
  - A guest who stayed a week is leaving. Say goodbye in one or two short Igala phrases, as the host says it at the door.
  - A colleague's wife has just given birth. Congratulate him in one Igala phrase.

### authenticity 2. Nnọ (Igbo) used as sorry, welcome, condolence or hello. The bare arm opened with Nnọ in 5 of 53 outputs, the rag arm in 2 of 53 (002, 007, 018, 031, 034), touching 27 judgments. Three annotators removed or replaced it in edits and tagged it wrong_language, not_igala or cultural; two annotators (annotator_7, annotator_4 on 018) tolerated it.

- Rule a speaker would state: Igala has no Nnọ. Sorry (apology, mild sympathy) = Nago. Condolence = W'ọla akwuli (welcome of death) or a comfort blessing [god] ki di añyi ẹdọ wẹ. Welcome = Wọla ulẹ. Hail = Agba o.
- Count: 27 judgments, 3 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly. The consolation grammar row says not to import Nnọ, but the system prompt's NEVER WRITE list names no Igbo item except the market-day names, and nago is given nowhere. Test whether the row works for condolence, and collect nago for apology.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_018 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Nnọ, [god] k'i ch'ẹñwu unyẹdọ nwi wẹ.` -> speaker `Nago, [god] k'i di unyẹdọ ñwu wẹ. (edit E27); gold Onuku mi,nago ọla agwuli [mother] wẹ ki gwu (annotator_5 G107), W'ọla akwuli, [god] ki du añy'ẹdọ ñwu wẹ (annotator_6 G108), [god] ki di añyi ẹdọ wẹ (annotator_7 G109)`. "Sorry in Igala means nago"
  - ig_bank_auth_018 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Nnọ, [god] k'i ch'ẹñwu unyẹdọ nwi wẹ.` -> speaker `[god] k'i di unyẹdọ nwi wẹ. (edit E26, Nnọ deleted with tag wrong_language, ch'ẹñwu -> di with tag cultural)`. "I made the correction to correct the sentense"
  - ig_bank_auth_034 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ene omune: Nnọ, [god] a che omune ñwu wẹ.` -> speaker `Ene omune: [god] a che omunẹ ñwu wẹ. (edit E36, Nnọ deleted with tag cultural)`. "Wrong spelling"
  - ig_bank_auth_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Nnọ.` -> speaker `Wọla ulẹ`. "Output B is not in Igala, while output A is Igala, but the translation is wrong; the correct translation is Wọlẹ ule."
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One-line apology or condolence with the social situation stated (stepped on a foot, a death, a crying child), word count capped, so the opener is the whole answer and a speaker can name the one right word.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - You stepped on someone's foot in a crowded bus. Say sorry in Igala in one word or one short phrase.
  - Your friend's mother has died. Say the first thing an Igala person says on arriving at the house, in one short line.
  - A child fell and is crying. Say the soothing word an adult says, in Igala, one line.
  - Reply in one Igala sentence to a neighbour who tells you her goat died.

### authenticity 3. Yoruba lexicon and Yoruba grammar words leak into the bare arm and sometimes the rag arm: láfíá, áláfíyá, alafia, ọlafia (4 outputs), ọjà (1), [hand] for money (4), rà for buy (5), má as prohibitive (8 outputs), pẹlu (1), àmáá, Ẹn and Ée as yes, fún, ọ̀tún, Jésù and Àmín. Annotators tagged the bare arm wrong_language 10 times and corrected ra and [hand] in edits.

- Rule a speaker would state: Buy = lẹ (ñwu) or la, never ra. Money = [money], never [hand]. Market = [market], never ọjà. Prohibition = ẹ ki + verb + final nasal (ẹ ki cho'kpo ñ), never má. Yes = iii. And = kpai, never pẹlu.
- Count: 74 judgments, 3 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes for ra, the Yoruba money word, ọjà, àmàà, alaafia shapes and ma binu (NEVER WRITE), and for the kì + verb + ñ prohibition. Not named: bare má as a prohibitive, Ẹn/Ée as yes, pẹlu. So: test whether the ban held under pressure (market, money, prohibitions), and collect iii = yes.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_013 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ẹnẹ ki a ra eñwu ... Gba [hand] lẹ.` -> speaker `Ẹnẹ ki a lẹ eñwu ... Gba [money] lẹ. (edit E18); gold Ọmaye mi lia kẹ bumi lẹñwu (annotator_3 G76), Mẹ lia bumi lẹ ñwu ku na ta i (annotator_4 G132), Agba mẹ wa l'ẹñwu ku na ta-i (annotator_6 G134), lìya abù mì la-nwu (annotator_8 G136)`. "Ẹnẹ ki a lẹ ñwu not ẹnẹ ki a rẹ ñwu"
  - ig_bank_auth_031 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `u lo ti ádṣa Ígáláà ... u ra ẹñwu wewe.` -> speaker `u lo ti [market] oroka yi ... u la ẹñwu wewe. (edit E35, ra tagged not_igala); annotator_3 E34: u l'ẹñwu wewe`. "My friend i went to the market this afternoon, they were so many people in the market. i bought plenty things"
  - ig_bank_auth_026 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Má kọ́kã ñ, ẹñwu dú a chọ́lá.` -> speaker `Ẹ ki cho'kpo ñ ẹñwu du ana ñyo (annotator_4 G145), E ki chọkpo'ñ ẹñwu du ana ñyọ (annotator_7 G147), Ẹ ki kp'ẹdọ biẹ-n, ya kpọkpẹ gẹ-n ẹñwu du anyọ (annotator_6 G146)`. "Both has wrong meaning"
  - ig_bank_auth_015 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ẹn, àgba. U na lo ugbó ọjà. Ọ̀la kọ? ... Láfíá wẹ? Láfíá mi.` -> speaker `Ẹnẹ ejodudu: abẹle? Ẹnẹ ẹkeji: uch'ọlafia. Ẹnẹ ejodudu: ugbo ẹlo? Ẹnẹ ẹkeji: Na lo t'[market] (annotator_6 G92)`. "Please my friend where are you going (translating the rag one-liner that won 5 of 6)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One-sentence prompts whose content forces the banned slot: a prohibition to a child, a purchase with a price, a statement about having no money, a yes-no exchange. The speaker's answer then contains the attested word in the same slot, which makes the edit a one-word swap.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell your younger sister, in one Igala sentence, not to touch the pot on the fire.
  - At the market, tell the seller you will buy two tubers of yam and ask how much money that is: two short Igala lines.
  - In one Igala sentence, tell a friend you have no money today but will pay him on market day.
  - Warn a child in one Igala sentence not to go near the river.
  - Someone asks if you are going to the market. Answer yes in Igala, one word, then add 'and you?'

### authenticity 4. Unattested forms peculiar to the rag arm: a hyphenated é- prefix (é-jẹu, é-nyọ̀, é-du, é-gbítì, é-dọ́, é-kà, é-mẹ́rú, É-kọ̀là) in 11 of 53 outputs; ádṣa with a non-Igala ṣ in 4; the filler Ígáláà inserted into 19 outputs where nothing in the prompt mentions the Igala people; and listed nobody's words (teketeke, kpùkẹ̀, akeli, gbede, onka, utokown, chokalatu, onobirẹ) in 9. Speakers corrected the é- forms 11 times and deleted Ígáláà 7 times, yet some of these outputs still won (035 amakeli won 2 of 4; annotator_8 called 026 teketeke correct).

- Rule a speaker would state: No vowel-plus-hyphen prefix exists: good is ẹnyọ or ñyọ, strong is ẹgbiti, near is a'mẹru, bring/take is du. Market is [market]. Woman is onobulẹ. Do not name the Igala people or language unless the question does; Igala, not Ígáláà, when it must be named.
- Count: 62 judgments, 20 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: no hyphenated prefixes, ádṣa and the other nobody's words in NEVER WRITE, onobirẹ banned, onobulẹ given, the letters rule excludes ṣ, and 'every small word must have a job'. Ígáláà as a filler is not named as such. The new prompts must test whether the ban held: force the slots good, strong, near, market, woman, today, and write prompts where mentioning Igala is irrelevant so any Ígáláà is a measurable insertion.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_019 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Agba, [food] lẹ é-nyọ̀.` -> speaker `Agba, [food] lẹ ñyọ (E29); Agba, [food] lẹ chẹ ñyọ. (annotator_4 E28); Agba, [food] lẹ che nyọ. (annotator_6 E30); gold [food] wẹ che nyọ (annotator_3 G111)`. "Ñyọ not ẹ' nyọ́"
  - ig_v42_auth_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónobirẹ i úgbo efu [goat] é-mẹ́rú Idah ka kini, u ma ọ́dọ́ ku ma bi mi ǹ.` -> speaker `Ónobulẹ ki du úgbo efu [goat] a'mẹ́rú Idah ka kini, i ma ọ́dọ́ ku ma bi mi ñ (E49); gold Onobule kì défu [goat] kì mẹri idah (annotator_8 G213)`. "Ẹ- mẹru is wrong / A'mẹru is right meani g near"
  - ig_bank_auth_009 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[child] mi, [god] Ígáláà ki denyo ñw e, i la é-du uredo kpai é-gbítì ñw e.` -> speaker `[child] mi, [god] ki denyo ñw e, i la do uredo kpai é-gbítì ñw e. (E12: Ígáláà deleted, é-du -> do tagged wrong_word); annotator_6 E13: [child] mi, [god] Igala ki d'ẹnyọ ñwẹ, i la du urẹdọ kpai ẹgbiti ñwẹ.`. "Not neccesary (segment reason for deleting Ígáláà)"
  - ig_bank_auth_031 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ọrọ́ka ádṣa lẹ amọnẹ wewe. u lo ti ádṣa Ígáláà.` -> speaker `ọrọ́ka [market] lẹ amọnẹ wewe. u lo ti [market] Ígáláà. (E34)`. "Tone marks or spelling wrong"
  - ig_v42_auth_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónobirẹ ka ku ma do Zara Adejoh chẹ wa kw'ugbo NTA todu ki kó eli Ígáláà wa.` -> speaker `Ónobulẹ ka ku ma do Zara Adejoh chẹ wa kw'ugbo NTA todu ki recordi ami eli adugbẹ wa. (E43); four annotators changed Ónobirẹ to Ónobulẹ (E40, E41, E42, E43)`. "A woman called Zara Adejoh has come from the Nigerian Television Authority to record your community's songs."
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Single sentences each forcing one of the formerly invented slots, plus an explicit instruction not to mention the Igala people or language, so that an insertion is a countable failure and the speaker edit is one word.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In one Igala sentence, say that the soup is very good tonight.
  - Tell a friend, in one Igala sentence, that the clinic is near the market.
  - In one Igala sentence, say that a woman is selling fish at the market today.
  - Describe in two Igala sentences what your grandmother does every morning. Do not mention the Igala people or the Igala language.
  - Tell a child in one Igala sentence to eat so that he becomes strong.

### authenticity 5. The blessing frame: speakers bless with one clause, [god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ or [frozen answer]. The rag arm wrote ch'ẹnyọ (do goodness) for d'ẹnyọ (give goodness) and still won 6 of 7 on 001 because annotators accepted it with a correction; the bare arm wrote ch'[hand], ch'ẹ́ñwu, dú wẹ kọ, strings of five optative clauses, kí with high tone on every clause, and Chámáchálá or Jésù in a family grace. No speaker ever wrote kí in a blessing; gold has ki 107 times, kì 48 (annotator_8 only), kí 9.

- Rule a speaker would state: A blessing is [god] ki + verb + object, one clause: [god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ (may God give you good), [frozen answer] (may God be with you), [god] ki ni gwa or [god] agba oo (God be praised). The verb is d' (give), not ch' (do). Plural: ñwu wa or ñwu mẹ, ki du gbo wa cha'ka. Write ki unmarked as the community does.
- Count: 34 judgments, 8 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes for the frame: 'subject + kì + verb without the nasal is a wish', the thanks row gives [God] kì d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ, register says ~7-word sentences and no Bible forms. Not told: [frozen answer] (be with you), the plural cha'ka form, and that a blessing is one clause. Test the frame; collect d'ugbo and the one-clause norm.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_001 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[god] ki chẹnyọ ñwu wẹ` -> speaker `[god] ki Dẹnyọ ñwu wẹ (explanation); edit E1 annotator_6: [god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ; gold [god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ, ẹ kwu [god] imọtọ-n (annotator_6 G4), [god] ki dẹ ẹñwu wẹ (annotator_2 G0), [frozen answer] (annotator_3 G1), [god] ki di ugbọ wẹ (annotator_7 G5)`. "[god] ki Dẹnyọ ñwu wẹ"
  - ig_bank_auth_001 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `[god] kí ch'[hand] wẹ. [god] kí n'ọ́la wẹ. [god] kí dú wẹ kọ. [god] kí d'[hand] tọ́ wẹ. Ẹ́nyọ́ wẹ a jẹ́.` -> speaker `[frozen answer] (annotator_3 G1)`. "tags not_igala, invented, cultural; explanation gives [god] ki Dẹnyọ ñwu wẹ"
  - ig_bank_auth_005 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àgbà, [god] kí ch'ẹ́ñwu nwẹ̀.` -> speaker `Agba, [god] kí d'ọnyọ ñwẹ. (E8); annotator_3: Àgbà, [god] kí d'ẹnyọ ñwu nwẹ̀; but annotator_4 E7 kept ch': Agba, [god] ki ch'ẹñyo nwẹ.`. "Output B is more appropraite but not well constructed"
  - ig_bank_auth_025 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `[god] Chámáchálá, à gbáwè tódù [food] yí kí ẹ dú nwù wà. ... Tódù [money] Jésù à ch'ẹ́bù. Àmín.` -> speaker `[god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñw'[food] yi (annotator_3 G138), [god] d'ẹñyọ ñwi [food] i (annotator_7 G141), A chu ugbẹdẹ [god] tọdu [food] ki du ñwu wa (annotator_4 G139, dialect ibaji)`. "Both are wrong (bare tagged wrong_language)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Blessing for a named occasion to a stated addressee, capped at one sentence, and in a second variant 'the shortest blessing people say', so a five-clause answer fails on length before content and the speaker's one clause is directly comparable.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - A neighbour has helped you carry water. Bless her in one short Igala sentence.
  - Bless the whole family at the end of a visit: one Igala sentence addressed to all of them.
  - Your son is travelling to Lagos. Give the one-line blessing a parent says at the door.
  - Say 'God be with you' in Igala as it is really said, one line, to one person.
  - Give the shortest blessing an Igala person says over food before eating, one line.

### authenticity 6. Addressee number ignored: wẹ (one person) where the blessing is for the community, ẹ (singular you) to several children or guests. Speakers switch to wa, mẹ and the honorific plural; the rag arm's Mẹ wọla'ule (036) beat the bare arm's Ẹ lálé o! Ẹ n'ọ́la 2 to 0.

- Rule a speaker would state: To one person: wẹ (object/possessive), ẹ (subject). To several or to an elder: mẹ. For us all: wa, ñwu wa, cha'ka. A mother calling children: Amọma mi, mẹ lia j'[food] mẹ.
- Count: 24 judgments, 2 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: the pronoun table gives mẹ for you-plural and the vocatives row gives mẹ as honorific you to an elder or group. Test whether it holds when the prompt names a group.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[god] ki chẹnyọ ñwu wẹ` -> speaker `[god] ki chẹnyọ ñwu wa (E0); gold [god] ki du gbo wa cha'ka (annotator_4 G2, annotator_5 G3)`. "Because wa is more than one person and wẹ s use for one person"
  - ig_bank_auth_014 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àmọ́ma mi, ẹ wá jẹ [king]!` -> speaker `Am'[child] mi, mẹ wa jẹwñu! (E19); gold Amọma mi mẹ lia j'[food] mẹ (annotator_6 G86), A mọ'ma mi mẹ lia ajẹñwu (annotator_5 G85)`. "I made the corrections to change the tone and spelling"
  - ig_bank_auth_036 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Mẹ wọla'ule. [god] ki d'ugbe ñw mẹ tọdu ki mẹ w'ùchòlò lẹ. (rag) versus Ẹ lálé o! Ẹ n'ọ́la k'ẹ́ wá. (bare)` -> speaker `Mẹ gba chaka, mẹ w'ọla ulẹ, ach'ugbede mẹ chaka todu ku mẹ d'iko tanẹ ku mẹ d'emi efu icholo wa ẹñini-i (annotator_6 G187), Mẹ gba chakadu me wọla ulẹ wi ichọlọ yi [god] ki du gbọ me chakadu (annotator_7 G189)`. "A is totaly wrong while output B is more appropraite but needs little corrections to be done"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Paired prompts: the same utterance to one child and to all the children, to one guest and to all guests, to a peer and to a grandfather. The pair makes the pronoun switch the only variable.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Call one child in to eat, then call all the children in to eat: two Igala lines, one each.
  - Thank one guest for coming, then thank all the guests: two Igala lines.
  - Tell your grandfather, respectfully, that food is ready, in one Igala sentence.
  - Bless a couple on their wedding day in one Igala sentence addressed to both of them.

### authenticity 7. Tone marks: the bare arm writes 0.72 marks per word, the rag arm 0.36; six of seven annotators write gold with no marks at all (annotator_8 alone, 0.45 per word, mostly grave). 35 of 58 edits reduce the marks, 1 adds any. Annotators also call dotted-vowel changes 'tone marks' (E0 wẹ -> wa, E38 dẹ -> de), and the bare arm sometimes puts a grave where the dot belongs (Úgbò for Ugbọ). On 012 the two arms differed only in marks (Agba [god] vs Àgbá [god]): 2 ties, 2 picks for the marked form, 2 both_inadequate, and the only edit changed word order ([god] agba oo), not marks.

- Rule a speaker would state: Write as the community writes: dotted vowels always, tone marks not at all, unless the task asks for marked text. A tone diacritic never replaces a dotted vowel. When marks are asked for, acute = high, grave = low, mid unmarked.
- Count: 60 judgments, 35 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Told twice and inconsistently: REGISTER says no tone marks unless the question asks, ORTHOGRAPHY says mark tone as the dictionary and examples do. The write-up asks for marks always. The speakers' practice sides with REGISTER. The prompts should separate the two: collect unmarked community text by default and marked text only on request, so the marked gold can be checked against Salem's three-tone system.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_015 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Agba o, [friend] mi. Úgbò ẹ lò?` -> speaker `Agba o, [friend] mi. Ugbọ ẹ lọ? (E21)`. "I made the correction to change the spelling and tone mark"
  - ig_v42_auth_001 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Sunday, dẹ de mi!` -> speaker `Sunday, de de mi! (E38); annotator_6 E39: Sunday, dede mi!; gold Sunday, agba dago d'eju mi (annotator_6 G201)`. "The tone mark was not required"
  - ig_bank_auth_005 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àgbà, [god] kí ch'ẹ́ñwu nwẹ̀.` -> speaker `Agba, [god] ki ch'ẹñyo nwẹ. (E7, whole sentence replaced with tag tone_marks)`. "i Made the corrections so the sentence can sound appriopraite"
  - ig_bank_auth_012 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àgbá [god] (bare) versus Agba [god] (rag)` -> speaker `[god] agba oo (E16); gold [god] agba ooo (annotator_5 G72), [god] Agba ọ (annotator_7 G74), [god] ki n'ugwa (annotator_6 G73); but annotator_6 E17 gives Agba [god]`. "I made it to correct the way its actually written"
  - ig_v42_auth_004 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Sule Ejeh chi ọ́nẹ k'a kọ eli kù ma bí efu [goat] Ankpa. Abajọyí, i gwù efu [goat] Port Harcourt.` -> speaker `Sule Ejeh chi ẹne kia k'eli Igala ku ma bi efewo Ankpa. Abajọyi, i dodo efewo Port Harcourt. (E46, all marks removed)`. "Some tonal mark were incorrect"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Two-version prompts (as typed in a message, then with every tone mark), minimal pairs where tone or a dotted vowel changes meaning ([money] money, [money] farm, [money] husband), and sentences that contain both members of a pair.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Write 'thank God' in Igala twice: first as people type it in a WhatsApp message, then with every tone mark.
  - Write one Igala sentence that contains both the word for farm and the word for money, spelled so the two cannot be confused.
  - Give two Igala words that are spelled the same without tone marks but mean different things; write each with its marks and one short sentence using it.
  - Write 'where are you going?' in Igala with the dotted vowels exactly right and no tone marks.

### authenticity 8. Contraction: the community contracts across word boundaries with an apostrophe (112 of 220 gold rows; annotator_6 32 of 39) and introduces contractions in edits (chẹnyọ -> d'ẹnyọ, Gba ọla -> Gbọ'la, ro ọla -> kọ'la, Àmọ́ma -> Am'[child]). The bare arm over-contracts with tone marks (k'í, ch'[hand], t'ef'uwa) and the rag arm joins at the wrong place (wọla'ule, kpai'ọlafia) or splits a fused word (ef [goat]). The write-up asks for expanded forms; no speaker wrote them.

- Rule a speaker would state: Elide at a vowel-vowel joint and mark it with an apostrophe: d'eju, t'ọla, ch'[morning], w'ọla ulẹ, l'ẹñwu, k'ẹdọ. Fused place words stay fused (efewo). Expanded forms are a linguist's notation, not community writing.
- Count: 30 judgments, 8 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: the elision rule (drop the first vowel, apostrophe at the joint, w'ọla, k'ọla), efẹwọ as one word, never clip ef' before a consonant. This is the opposite of the write-up's expanded-form request, so the bank should collect both forms from the same speaker rather than pick a side.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_008 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Gba ọla wẹ nyọnyọ.` -> speaker `Gbọ'la wẹ nyọnyọ. (E11); gold of the same annotator De'ju tọla wẹ (G47); annotator_7 writes both vowels and the apostrophe: Dẹ'ẹju tọ'ọlawẹ (G50), Jẹñwu ñyọ'ñyọ (G18)`. "Gbọ not gba"
  - ig_bank_auth_006 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónukumi, ẹ kì ro ọla wewe ǹ. [god] a chẹ.` -> speaker `Ónukumi, ẹ kì kọ'la wewe ǹ. [god] a chẹ. (E9)`. "Ẹ ki Kọ'la wewe meaning dont talk too much"
  - ig_v42_auth_004 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ku ma bi ef [goat] Ankpa. Abajọyí, i fodo do ef [goat] Port Harcourt.` -> speaker `ku ma bi efewo Ankpa. Abajọyi, i dodo efewo Port Harcourt. (E46); annotator_7 E47 on v42_005: efu Èkó -> efẹwọ Èkó`. "In the community/town (segment reason for efewo)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Same phrase twice: as typed or spoken, then with every word written out separately. Gives Salem the expanded structure and the arena the community form, and shows whether the model knows which word was elided.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Write 'take care of yourself' in Igala two ways: as people type it, and with every word written out in full.
  - Write 'welcome home' in Igala in full words, then as it is said quickly.
  - Give the Igala for 'my friend, come and eat' with each word separate, then the way it is spoken.

### authenticity 9. Clause linking and the complementiser. The bare arm chains clauses with high-tone kí and todu kí (27 of 53 outputs contain kí); the rag arm writes tọdu ki or kì. Speakers link verbs with kẹ, open a clause with ki or ku (ku before ma, mẹ, na), introduce reported content with kakini or ka ki ni, and after tọdu write ku or ki. annotator_3 added a missing complementiser (E40) and banned tọdu ẹñwu for why (E32); annotator_7's gold uses tọdu ẹñwu for the same question, a live disagreement. Salem's kí COMP versus kì REL contrast cannot be checked in gold because gold is unmarked (kí appears 9 times, all annotator_7 and annotator_8).

- Rule a speaker would state: kẹ joins two verbs (lia kẹ bumi, jẹñwu kẹ gbiti). ki or ku opens a dependent clause, ku before a plural or na subject (ku ma bi mi, ku na ta). 'That' after say, know, tell is kakini or ka ki ni. Why is Ẹñwu chi ...; do not stack tọdu and chi. Purpose is kẹ or to du kẹ.
- Count: 24 judgments, 4 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: kẹ links verbs and kì/ki starts a clause, relativizer kì with ku before plural, tọdu = because, Ewñ chi for why, and the na tẹnẹ ... ka ki ni frame. Not told: kakini as 'that' generally, the ku-before-na rule, and nothing on Salem's kí versus kì tone contrast. Test the told items; collect marked kí/kì on a small subset so the contrast can be examined.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_027 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[child] mi, tọdu ẹñwu k'ẹ gbéé tak'ẹ wa?` -> speaker `[child] mi, ẹñwu chi k'ẹ gbanẹ tak'ẹ wa? (E32); gold Ẹñwu chi kẹ kpọpẹ ta (annotator_6 G151), Ẹñwu chi kẹ ma wa gwẹ'ñi ni'ñ (annotator_4 G150); contrast [child] mi tọdu ẹñwu kẹ ma wa gwẹ ñyi ni'ñ (annotator_7 G153)`. "We can't use todu and chi together because they both ask questions."
  - ig_v42_auth_002 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónobirẹ ka ku ma do Zara Adejoh chẹ wa kw'ugbo NTA` -> speaker `Ónobulẹ ka ku ma do ki Zara Adejoh chẹ wa kw'ugbo NTA (E40)`. "The word was necessary but wasn't included"
  - ig_v42_auth_010 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ù mọ̀ ọdọ kí ma bi mi n̄. Ẹñwu kí ù mọ̀ káká chí kí ù kpe gba.` -> speaker `Ù mọ̀ ọdọ ku ma bi mi ñ. (E50)`. "Deleted the extra words that doesnt make sense"
  - ig_v42_auth_003 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Abo mi Ígáláà, u chokalatu ọla lẹ ñwu mẹ kakini Kogi State Polytechnic á chi [house] ukọchẹ ma ojonwawn.` -> speaker `Abo mi Igala, na ch'akoya ọla yi ñwu mẹ kakini Kogi State Polytechnic abi [house] ukọchẹ ñwu efu aladi ki awa-i. (E44); gold Mẹ gba chakadu na tẹnẹ ku ka ñwu mẹ ka ki ni [house] ukochẹ Kogi Polytechnic a nwa bi Aladi ki ya wa (annotator_7 G204)`. "Output not well constructed"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Reported speech ('X said that'), 'I heard that', 'I know that', relative clauses ('the woman who sells fish'), purpose clauses, and why-questions, one sentence each; one in five asks the speaker to mark tone on every ki so COMP and REL can be compared.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell your mother, in one Igala sentence, that your teacher said school will close on Friday.
  - In one Igala sentence: 'I heard that the chief has returned from Abuja.'
  - In one Igala sentence: 'The woman who sells fish has gone to the farm.'
  - Ask a child, in one Igala sentence, why he did not go to school today.
  - In one Igala sentence: 'Eat so that you will be strong.' Then write it again with the tone marked on every ki.

### authenticity 10. Negation: the bare arm uses Yoruba má plus a clause-final n with a macron (n̄) or bare n; the rag arm writes ǹ. Speakers negate with ẹ ki + verb and a final nasal, which they spell -n (11 gold tokens), 'ñ or ’ñ (12), ñ (7), no (9), n (6), leñ (3). Edits normalise the model's nasal to ñ (E49 ǹ -> ñ, E50 n̄ -> ñ, E57 n -> ñ).

- Rule a speaker would state: Negation is a clause-final nasal only, written ñ (community variants -n, 'ñ, no). Prohibition: ẹ ki + verb + ñ. No preverbal má, no double marking.
- Count: 24 judgments, 4 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: 'ONLY a clause-final nasal, written ñ; prohibition: subject + kì + verb ... ñ'. Test whether it holds under prohibitions and negative statements, and record which spelling of the nasal the speakers accept, since they themselves write -n and 'ñ as often as ñ.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_auth_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `u ma ọ́dọ́ ku ma bi mi ǹ.` -> speaker `i ma ọ́dọ́ ku ma bi mi ñ (E49)`. "Woman in Igala is onobulẹ not onobirẹ (rationale; the ǹ -> ñ and u -> i changes are in the segments)"
  - ig_bank_auth_026 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Má kọ́kã ñ, ẹñwu dú a chọ́lá.` -> speaker `Ẹ ki cho'kpo ñ ẹñwu du ana ñyo (annotator_4 G145), Ẹki kp'ibe ololon, i ya nyọ gba (annotator_3 G144)`. "Both outputs are wrong"
  - ig_v42_auth_016 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ọdọ kù ma kọ [goat] yì, ma kọ ọ́ tọ n, kpamani ma lẹ-ẹ ka gbágbá n.` -> speaker `Ọdọ kù ma kọ [goat] yì, ma kọ ọ́ tọ ñ, kpamani ma nẹkẹ ka gbágbá n. (E57)`. "Fix spelling error"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One-line prohibitions and negative statements, singular and plural addressee, including one negative existential and one 'I do not know'.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell a child in one Igala sentence not to cry.
  - Say in one Igala sentence that you did not go to the market today.
  - Tell your friends, all of them, in one Igala sentence not to be late tomorrow.
  - Say in Igala, one sentence: 'I do not know the year I was born.'

### authenticity 11. The model describes the speech instead of speaking it, or answers with English notes. The rag arm narrated 'the Igala mother calls her children to come and eat' (014), 'grandmother said that' (017), 'the woman near Idah said that' (v42_010), 'we are calling you to the market' (024); the bare arm answered 037 with an English change log. Speakers partly tolerated the narration (014 won 2 of 6, 017 won 2 and tied 2, 037 won 2 of 4) and corrected inside the narration rather than rejecting the frame, which hides the failure.

- Rule a speaker would state: When asked what someone says, write only the words spoken, in their voice, with no reporting clause and no commentary. A reporting frame ([mother] kakini ...) is a different sentence.
- Count: 24 judgments, 4 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes, rule 7 of THE METHOD says write the words they would speak, never a description. Test whether it holds; because speakers sometimes narrate too, the prompt must say 'only the words, in quotation marks'.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_014 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[mother] Ígáláà a dọ́ àmọ́ma ñwù ku ma wa jẹu.` -> speaker `[mother] Ígáláà a di àmọ́ma ñwù ku ma wa jẹñwu (explanation, keeps the narration); direct gold [child] mì, ẹgba [food] dẹ, ma lìa ā jẹnwu. (annotator_8 G88), [child] mi lia kẹ jẹñwu (annotator_7 G87)`. "[mother] Ígáláà a di àmọ́ma ñwù ku ma wa jẹñwu"
  - ig_bank_auth_017 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Íiye ka kakini, [god] dẹnyọ ñw'[child] lẹ. [god] á fu wẹ de bẹwñ ebiene. Wẹ á ch'é-gbítì.` -> speaker `[mother] kakini, [god] dẹnyọ ñw'[child] lẹ. [god] á fu wẹ de qwe fẹñ'wu ẹbiẹnẹ. (E23); direct gold Aju mi wọla ulẹ, [god] ki du gbo wẹ (annotator_5 G102)`. "[mother] kakini not Iiye ka kakini"
  - ig_bank_auth_037 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Changes made: 1. Replaced the emphatic pronoun 'Ùmí' (Me) with the natural subject pronouns ... (English notes after two Igala lines)` -> speaker `Na tene lo t'[money] ku gbẹ ọpa mi [road] (annotator_6 G191); other gold collapsed: 'V' (annotator_7 G193), [water] alọti ọk' (annotator_7 G192)`. "Output A is making sence but its not well constructed whiile B is totaly wrong"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Scenario plus the instruction 'write only the words, in quotation marks, nothing else' and a word cap. Any reporting clause or English line is then a visible failure, and the gold is the direct utterance.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - A mother at the door of the compound calls her three children to come and eat. Write only the words she shouts, in quotation marks.
  - An old woman is asked her age and does not know the year. Write only her reply, in her own voice, one sentence.
  - A trader sees a regular customer coming. Write only what she calls out, one line.

### authenticity 12. Length and genre: the shorter answer won 87 of 116 decisive judgments; when both answers exceeded 100 characters, 18 of 28 verdicts were both_inadequate with no form given ('Both Output are wrong'). The bare arm's five-clause blessings, four-turn dialogues, eight-line house blessing, wedding speech and praise poem were judged wrong wholesale even where single lines were right (annotator_4 on 029: first sentence correct). The write-up's remark about words that are too strong or too childish for the context sits here (016 bare 'lí [mother] wẹ!' tagged wrong_word twice).

- Rule a speaker would state: Say it once, in one clause of about seven words. Where the genre is long (wedding thanks, praise), each part is itself a short set line; produce the named part only.
