Not available to me: the 16 comparisons of the holdout ig_dial_001 (they are not between the two pooled arms and are absent from the slice file), and ig_bank_dial_003, _015, _018, _019 have zero comparisons and zero gold (only a v4.4 answer exists). Write-up restated in my own words: Igala has no s sound (ch replaces it, except in names), the Ogwugwu variety says r where the centre says l, Ibaji allows closed syllables the centre does not (uñ for ẹñwu), tone should be marked throughout and full forms preferred to apostrophised ones, number agreement is real but limited to specific verbs (gwugwu/tẹ singular vs jọ plural; du one object vs ko many), kí introduces reported content while kì introduces a relative clause and the model over-uses the former, every answer should stay inside one variety, annotators should record audio, and word strength often mismatches the situation.

### dialectal_fidelity 1. Fabricated dialect geography. Whenever a prompt says 'note variation between areas', both arms invent a form and assign it to a named town. In the 36 distinct model answers the model names Ankpa 10 times, Idah 8, Dekina 5 and never Ibaji or Ogugu; the 62 speaker gold rows name Idah 8 times, Ogugu 6, Ankpa 5, Ibaji 3 and never Dekina. Every town-attributed form the model produced was rejected or unsupported; the alternations speakers themselves attest are different words entirely (come: lia/liya/lẹya Idah, wa Ankpa and Ogugu, wẹ mi Ogugu, lẹwa or lua Ibaji; eat: jẹñwu vs Ibaji jamìn̄wu; how are you: abẹle vs Ibaji ka bu ẹle; mother: [mother] Idah vs iya Ankpa; sibling: omaye Idah vs omada Ankpa/Ogugu; don't be angry: Ekiwedon Idah, Chakwuli Ankpa, Edọki'yẹ Ogugu; buttocks: ofe vs Ibaji ọkpu). v4.4 still attributes, and on dial_015 reverses the attested direction (Idah 'Wa mọ', Ankpa 'Lia mọ'), on dial_007 invents an Ibaji subject pronoun ('mi a lọ ti [money]), on dial_002 asserts an [water]/oho split that two of three speakers deny.

- Rule a speaker would state: Give only the form you can attest. If asked what another town says and no source gives it, say in Igala that you do not know; never assign a word to Idah, Ankpa, Dekina, Ibaji or Ogugu without an attested source, and never present a coined word as a town's variant. The speakers' own dialect map is Idah (general), Ankpa, Ogugu, Ibaji; Dekina is not a variety they name.
- Count: 26 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes, rule 8 ('Never assert which town or area uses a form unless your reference material says so - saying you do not know is correct'). Telling did not work: v4.4's own dial outputs still attribute (dial_002 [water]/oho split, dial_007 'abọ Ibaji a kà ki ni mi a lọ ti [money], dial_015 Idah/Ankpa reversed, dial_017 'Ibaji aka ka kini ókà'). Retrieval of annotator_6's 'Uchẹ ki bọ aka kini X abeki bọ aka kini Y' frame now supplies the sentence shape, and the model fills the slots with guesses. New prompts must test whether it declines to attribute when no source exists.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_004 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Wá mímí (Idah) / Wá málí (Ankpa)` -> speaker `Wa, lia / Uchẹ ki bọ aka kini "wa" abeki bọ aka kini "lia" (gold); also annotator_7 gold: Abọ Igala okpakpa a ka ki ni 'liya ẹmi' abo ọgugu a ka ki ni 'wẹ mi'`. "Both Output are wrong (wrong_word on both arms)"
  - ig_bank_dial_004 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Wa efí = come here (Ankpa area)` -> speaker `Liy'emì (gold annotator_8); Ankpa kpaì ogugu kakì wá k�“ jen̄wù (annotator_8 gold on dial_007: Ankpa and Ogugu say wá)`. "Both output are not correct (cultural tag on the bare arm)"
  - ig_bank_dial_005 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `'Lafia we' efu Ígáláà, abọ Ankpa a ka 'Daago', abọ Dekina a ka 'Awa'` -> speaker `"Abu wele", or "Agbebo" (gold); annotator_6 gold: "abẹ le" ama IBAJI aka ka kini "ka bu ẹle"; annotator_7 gold: abu'wẹ gbọ manyi ayẹ gbọ`. "In Igala the word for an Igala way of saying 'how are you' ... is "Abu wele", or "Agbebo""
  - ig_bank_dial_005 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Amí Ídá à dọ́ kí "Ọla àgbo?". Amí Ankpa à dọ́ kí "Àgbo?". Amí Dekina à dọ́ kí "Àgbo wé?"` -> speaker `abu'wẹ gbọ manyi ayẹ gbọ (gold annotator_7)`. "both are inadequate (wrong_word, grammar on both)"
  - ig_bank_dial_007 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Amí Idah a ka kí, "Ù dọmọ lo tí ọja." Amí Ankpa kpaí Dekina a ka kí, "Ù a lo tí ọja."` -> speaker `Idah kakì lẹya kẹ jen̄wù, Ankpa kpaì ogugu kakì wá kẹ jen̄wù, Ibajì kakì lẹwa kẹ jamìn̄wu (gold annotator_8); annotator_7 gold: Abo ogugu a ka ki ni 'La ka jẹñwu' abo Idah a ka ki ni lia ka jẹñwu`. "The both output are not correct (tone_marks on the bare arm)"
  - ig_bank_dial_017 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `"U" (I) ... efu [goat] Idah, Dekina; "N" (I) ... efu [goat] Ankpa: "N tẹ́ñwu ki jẹ"` -> speaker `Ekiwedon - Idah / Chakwuli - Ankpa / Edọki'yẹ - Ogugu (gold annotator_7); annotator_8 gold pairs: Agba or awa, Gwugwu ane or gwu ane, Olodudu or [frozen answer], [sun] ukọlọ or wola ukọlọ, Alọ or nalo; annotator_6 gold: Ofe - ọgbọ Igala adọ abalẹ / Ọkpu - Abo Ibaji adọ abalẹ`. "Both are inaccurate (cultural on both arms)"
  - ig_bank_dial_017 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Abọ Idah a ka 'ẹñwu', oñ abọ Ankpa a ka 'ọñwu'` -> speaker `no speaker attests ọñwu; annotator_8's variant list above`. "Both output is inadequate (grammar on both)"
  - ig_bank_dial_014 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `[mother]-àgbà ... amí éfù-[goat] Ígálá àbọ́jí a dọ́ kí "[mother]-káká" abikí "[mother]-ógwú"` -> speaker `Idah - Omaye / Ankpa, Ogugu - Omada / Okwọ, omẹnyi (gold annotator_7); annotator_8 gold: idah Igala "[child]-[mother] mì", Ankpa Igala "[child]-ìya mì"`. "both are wrong (wrong_word, invented on the bare arm)"
  - ig_bank_dial_011 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Amọ́nẹ Ankpa kpaí Dekina a dọ kí "ukalọ [money]" abí "ka ukọ" ... amí ugbó omunẹ a dọ kí "ukọ"` -> speaker `gbalukà [money] leẹ́ = count that money (gold annotator_8); [road] ka ku ma kaluka [money] efu a ja (gold annotator_6)`. "Both are inaccurate (wrong_word on both)"
  - ig_bank_dial_001 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Efu [goat] Ígáláà omune, ma ka kakini Nnọ` -> speaker `[frozen answer] kpai ọlọdudu (gold annotator_7, gloss: spoken across all Igala); annotator_8 gold: Àgbà, nágo or Áwà / Ọla [morning] or [frozen answer]`. "Output is nearly correct (invented tag on the retrieval arm)"
  - ig_bank_dial_015 [annotator_8 (gold on dial_007 contradicts it)] (v4.4 (v44-vs-gold.json)): model `Abọ Idah a ka kini "Wa mọ", muda abọ Ankpa a ka kini "Lia mọ"` -> speaker `Idah kakì lẹya kẹ jen̄wù, Ankpa kpaì ogugu kakì wá kẹ jen̄wù (annotator_8): Idah has lia/lẹya, Ankpa has wá, the opposite of v4.4's claim`. "no judgment yet; v4.4 output only"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Two complementary shapes. (a) Fix the variety in the prompt and forbid comparison: 'as people in Idah say it, one utterance, Idah form only', and ask the speaker to write their own town in brackets, so gold comes with a declared variety instead of the uniform general_idah field. (b) Ignorance probes: name a town and a word for which no reference exists (Ogugu 'don't be angry', Ankpa 'my sibling', Dekina anything) and score the model on whether it says it does not know; the speaker's gold tells us the real answer if they have it. Both shapes produce one short utterance that can be edited word by word, which is what made ig_v42_dial_001 and dial_010 decisive.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - A child is running off with your cooking pot. Call her back the way people in Idah shout 'come here', one short utterance, Idah form only. If you are not from Idah, write your own town's form and put the town's name in brackets after it.
  - Your younger sister has put food on the table. Tell your brother 'come and eat' exactly as people in your own town say it, then write the town's name in brackets. Do not give a second town's form.
  - Someone asks you what people in Ogugu say for 'do not be angry'. If you know the Ogugu form, write it alone. If you do not, write one Igala sentence saying you do not know the Ogugu word.
  - Write 'my mother's child' (my sibling) the way an Ankpa speaker says it, one phrase. If you are not sure what Ankpa speakers say, say so in Igala rather than guessing.
  - A market woman in Ibaji greets you with her town's 'how are you'. Write only her greeting in the Ibaji form, then your reply in your own town's form, two lines, and name both towns.

### dialectal_fidelity 2. Coined words survive the blocklist. Both arms fill lexical gaps for 'here', 'how are you', 'yes/no', 'now/today' with invented forms (mímí, málí, efí, dọmọ, ọ̀nálẹ́, aha, Hẹn, Ẹ̀n-ẹ́n, ádṣa, kpùkẹ̀, ojoji, éfodṣa, é-gbítì, kpegwa, gbede, chokalatu, íčí) and the retrieval arm adds a hyphenated prefix é-du. v4.4 drops every listed word but coins new ones in the same slots: 'Wa ugboí', 'wa ibai' (dial_004), 'Abawe, abawe de' (dial_005, after the repair round), 'Ẹñwẹñ' (dial_006). The speakers' word for 'here' is emi/ẹmi in all three annotators who wrote it.

- Rule a speaker would state: 'Here' is emi (wa emi, liya ẹmi, liy'emì); a word the dictionary and examples do not contain is not Igala and must not be coined, whatever the blocklist says; when the slot is unknown, use a plain attested paraphrase or say the gap exists.
- Count: 37 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes for the specific words (ádṣa, kpùkẹ̀, ojoji, mímí, efí, kpegwa, chokalatu, éfodṣa, gbede are all on NEVER WRITE; hyphenated prefixes are banned) and v4.4 no longer writes them. No for the general behaviour: the prompt does not give emi for 'here' or any yes/no pair, so v4.4 coins ugboí, ibai, abawe, Ẹñwẹñ. The next prompts test generalisation of 'never coin', not the list.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_004 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Wa mímí / Wa efí` -> speaker `liya ẹmi (gold annotator_7); wa emi, lia emi (gold annotator_6); Liy'emì (gold annotator_8)`. "Both are inaccurate"
  - ig_bank_dial_016 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ẹ a lo t'ádṣa? ... u a lo t'ádṣa now, todu Ígáláà mi chẹ rusty` -> speaker `I dey go house ẹ lọ kpo'mì for this sun (gold annotator_8)`. "They are made up of word that does not exist (invented on both arms)"
  - ig_bank_dial_012 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ọ̀là Ígáláà kpùkẹ̀ chi ádṣa` -> speaker `Na ñya kp'ẹjẹ / Alukate ma k'ọla yi (gold annotator_6)`. "Both Output does not pass the message (wrong_word on rag, invented on bare)"
  - ig_bank_dial_013 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Eyi chi é-du ñà alu ku ma ka kakini é-du ẹñwu ñwu mi efu Ígáláà. Efu Ígáláà gbagba: Du [water] ñwu mi.` -> speaker `Elu'eju Ígáláà gbagba: Du [water] ñwu mi. (edit: whole preamble deleted, Efu replaced by Elu'eju, reason 'Igala way')`. "I made this so we can get a perfect sentence"
  - ig_bank_dial_010 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ẹnẹ Ígáláà abíne ki a k'ọla ẹfi éfodṣa é-gbítì kpegwa Ígáláà kpai ọla Enefu ẹfi ọla òókáà` -> speaker `Guy, u n'álo ti school abajọyi, but u ni transport ñ, a ni ukọla later (edit of the bare arm, annotator_7)`. "wrong_word, grammar on the retrieval arm"
  - ig_bank_dial_006 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `'yes' chi ọ̀nálẹ́, abẹki ee ... 'no' chi aha, abẹki ññ` -> speaker `Iii (yes), Idabẹlẹ-n (no) (gold annotator_6)`. "They are both incorrect"
  - ig_bank_dial_004 [none yet] (v4.4 (v44-vs-gold.json)): model `Wa ugboí / Uchẹ ka a kà ka ki ni wa ibai` -> speaker `wa emi (annotator_6), liya ẹmi (annotator_7), Liy'emì (annotator_8)`. "v4.4 output only; ugboí and ibai appear in no speaker text"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Concrete scene that forces the gap word (here, there, now, yes, no, how are you as a reply) inside one short utterance, no 'note variation' clause. Speakers then edit the one coined word (the dial_010 edit shows they will) instead of writing 'both wrong'. Score: does the model's word appear in any gold or edit; v4.4 vs pooled arms on the same slot.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - You are at your door and your friend is at the gate. Tell him to come here, one sentence, as you would shout it.
  - Tell the child carrying your bag: 'put the bag here, not there'. One sentence.
  - Someone asks if the pot is here in the kitchen. Answer 'it is here' in one short sentence.
  - A neighbour greets you in the morning. Write her greeting and your reply, two lines, as spoken in your town.
  - Tell your mother you are leaving now and will be back this evening. One sentence.

### dialectal_fidelity 3. The sentence that states a variation is wrong Igala even when the quoted word is right. The model's metalanguage is 'Amí Idah a dọ kí', 'Amí [goat] kímí a lò X nwu Y', 'Àmáá', 'abí', 'tàbí', 'oñ', 'kpaa', 'dabí', 'Ma a lò ọla-Igala yí efu [goat]; speakers have a fixed frame: 'Uchẹ ki bọ a ka ki ni X abẹki bọ a ka ki ni Y' (some say X, others say Y), 'Abọ Idah a ka ki ni X, abọ Ogugu a ka ki ni Y' for named towns, 'X ma dọ ojanẹ Igala du' (it is called X all over Igala land) for no variation, 'X - ọgbọ Igala adọ abalẹ / Y - Abo Ibaji adọ abalẹ'. The one edit here changed exactly the metalanguage word: kpaa to gba ('all').

- Rule a speaker would state: To say 'some people say X and others say Y': Uchẹ ki bọ a ka ki ni X, abẹki bọ a ka ki ni Y. To name towns: Abọ Idah a ka ki ni X, abọ Ankpa a ka ki ni Y. To deny variation: X ma dọ ojanẹ Igala du. 'All' is gba; 'or' is abẹki or manyi; 'people of' is abọ/abo, never amí; 'use' is never lò.
- Count: 26 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly. tàbí, àmàà and oñ are banned; abí, lò (Yoruba 'use'), amí (for 'people of') and dabí are not. The 'some say X, others say Y' frame is not in the prompt; v4.4 reproduces it only because retrieval returns annotator_6's gold verbatim ('Uchẹ ki bọ aka kini ... abeki bọ aka kini ...' on dial_002, 004, 005, 006, 017). New prompts must require the frame on a topic whose retrieval hits are not dial gold.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_002 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `amí Ígálá kpaa a dọ́ kí [water]` -> speaker `amí Ígálá gba a dọ́ kí [water] (edit, rationale 'Wrong Spelling'); annotator_6 gold: [water] ma dọ ojanẹ Igala du`. "Wrong Spelling"
  - ig_bank_dial_002 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `[water]. Ọ́la yí kpa nyán̄ efu amí [goat] Ígálá wéwé n̄; amí Ígálá kpaa a dọ́ kí [water].` -> speaker `[water] / [water] ma dọ ojanẹ Igala du (gold annotator_6)`. "Output B got the correct word but the explanation does not make sence"
  - ig_bank_dial_006 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Amí [goat] kímí a lò *Hẹn* nwu "Yes"` -> speaker `Ẹẹ manyi ññ (gold annotator_7)`. "Both are slighlty wrong"
  - ig_bank_dial_001 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àmáá, efu [goat] Ankpa, amáa kọla [hand] ọmúnẹ, amáa dọ "Nnaa" àbíkọ "Ọdọda"` -> speaker `Uchẹ ki bọ aka kini "wa" abeki bọ aka kini "lia" (annotator_6 frame, gold dial_004)`. "Output is nearly correct (grammar on the bare arm)"
  - ig_bank_dial_017 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ma a lò ọla-Igala yí efu [goat] Idah, Dekina, kpaí [goat] Igala wéwé` -> speaker `In idah and Ankpa has common regional variant (annotator_8 gloss); Abọ Igala okpakpa a ka ki ni ... abo ọgugu a ka ki ni ... (annotator_7)`. "Both put all slight correct (grammar on the bare arm)"
  - ig_bank_dial_005 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ma a ka 'Lafia we' efu Ígáláà, amáà abọ Ankpa a ka 'Daago', oñ abọ Dekina a ka 'Awa'` -> speaker `Uchẹ ka aka kanini "abẹ le" ama IBAJI aka ka kini "ka bu ẹle" (gold annotator_6)`. "both are inadequate (wrong_word, grammar)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One-sentence statements about words, not about dialects, so the retrieved dial gold cannot be copied: 'tell a learner that some people call X one thing and others another', 'say that everybody calls it X', 'answer whether [father] and [father] mean the same'. The frame words (uchẹ, abẹki/manyi, gba, ma dọ, ojanẹ Igala du) are then measurable and the speaker's version gives the frame in new vocabulary.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell a visitor, in one Igala sentence, that some people call the morning meal one thing and others call it another, using two real words you know.
  - Say in Igala, one sentence: 'Everyone in Igala land calls it [water].'
  - A learner asks whether [father] and [father] mean the same thing. Answer in two short Igala sentences.
  - Write one Igala sentence telling a learner that people in your town say one word for 'thank you' and people in another town say another. Use only forms you have heard yourself and name the towns.
  - Tell your friend, in Igala, that the word for 'water' does not change anywhere in Igala land. One sentence.

### dialectal_fidelity 4. Yoruba lexis inside everyday requests and variation answers. The bare arm's 'fully Igala' request is Yoruba (Ẹjọwọ ... ra íjẹ lú mi), 'market' is Yoruba ọja, 'or' is tàbí or abí, 'how are you' is the alaafia shape Lafia we, 'use' is lò. Speakers' forms: please = agba (sentence-final or initial), give = ch'/chi, help me = bu mi, market = [market] (efu [market], efu a ja), or = abẹki/manyi.

- Rule a speaker would state: Please is agba (Ch'[water] emó mì, agba), help me is bu mi, give me is ch'[water]/du ... ñwu mi, market is [market], or is abẹki or manyi. Yoruba ẹjọwọ, ra, lú, ọja, tàbí, abí, lò, lafia are not Igala.
- Count: 22 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: tàbí, ọja, ra, any alaafia shape and àmàà are on NEVER WRITE; ẹjọwọ (only ejoo is listed), abí, lú and lò are not. v4.4's dial_013 output ('Du [water] ñwu mi k'u mọ / Du water ñwu mi make I drink') is clean, so the listed items appear fixed; the unlisted ones are untested.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_013 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Igala gbáá: Ẹjọwọ, dá mi [hand] ra íjẹ lú mi. Igala kpaí Én̄gílísì: Ẹjọwọ, help mi ra food lú mi.` -> speaker `Ch'[water] emó mì, agba / Abeg, ch'[water] mi emó mì make idrink. (gold annotator_8); Agba bu mi ch'ukọlọ yi ta / Please, bu mi ch'ukọlọ yi ta (gold annotator_6)`. "Give me water (wrong_language tag on the bare arm; retrieval arm chosen)"
  - ig_bank_dial_013 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ẹjọwọ, dá mi [hand] ra íjẹ lú mi` -> speaker `Ch'[water] emó mì, agba (gold annotator_8)`. "Output A not correct, Output B is correct"
  - ig_bank_dial_007 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ù dọmọ lo tí ọja` -> speaker `efu a ja = in the market (gold annotator_6, dial_011); Ekalọ [money] efu [market] is the model's own later form of 'market'`. "They are both wrong (wrong_word on both)"
  - ig_bank_dial_005 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `"Àgbo?" tàbí "Ọla àgbo?"` -> speaker `Ibo ka cha ka kini "abẹle" / Ibo omunẹ chi "aidẹ" (gold annotator_3)`. "Wrong words (grammar on both)"
  - ig_bank_dial_005 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ma a ka 'Lafia we' efu Ígáláà` -> speaker `abẹ le (gold annotator_6)`. "They are both incorrect (wrong_word)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Everyday requests with 'please', 'buy', 'market', 'or', 'help me' in a concrete scene, one sentence each, no variety question. A Yoruba word then stands out as a single editable token and the speaker's agba/bu mi/[market]/abẹki forms accumulate.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Ask your neighbour politely to help you carry the firewood. One sentence.
  - Tell your son to go to the market and buy palm oil or salt, whichever he finds. One sentence.
  - Ask a stranger politely for drinking water, the way you would really say it.
  - Tell your friend: 'I will come either today or tomorrow.' One sentence.
  - A trader asks what you want. Say you are buying pepper, not onions. One sentence.

### dialectal_fidelity 5. Yes and no. Neither arm produces a pair any speaker accepts (bare: Ee / Ẹn, Ẹ̀n-ẹ́n / N̄-n̄, Hẹn; retrieval: ọ̀nálẹ́ / ee, aha / ññ; v4.4: Ẹn, Ee / Ẹñwẹñ, Nn). The three speakers who answered gave three different pairs, so there is no verified row to tell the model.

- Rule a speaker would state: Yes is Iii/Ììí (annotators 6, 8) or Ẹẹ (annotator_7); no is ññ (annotator_7), Oda'a (annotator_8) or Idabẹlẹ-n (annotator_6, literally 'it is not so'). Which of these is variety, register or spelling is unknown and must be collected before a rule is written.
- Count: 4 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: No. There is no yes/no row in the 30 grammar rows and nothing in the prompt; v4.4 coins Ẹñwẹñ. This is evidence we lack, not a rule to test.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_006 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `*Ee* abí *Ẹn* (Yes) / *Ẹ̀n-ẹ́n* abí *N̄-n̄* (No) / Hẹn` -> speaker `Iii, yes / Idabẹlẹ-n (no) (gold annotator_6)`. "They are both incorrect"
  - ig_bank_dial_006 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `'yes' chi ọ̀nálẹ́, abẹki ee / 'no' chi aha, abẹki ññ` -> speaker `"Oda'a" (no) / "Ììí" (yes) (gold annotator_8)`. "Both output are not correct (tone_marks on the retrieval arm)"
  - ig_bank_dial_006 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `aha, abẹki ññ` -> speaker `Ẹẹ manyi ññ (gold annotator_7: ññ accepted for no, ọ̀nálẹ́/aha not)`. "Both are slighlty wrong"
  - ig_bank_dial_006 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ee / Ẹn` -> speaker `no gold from annotator_3; chose the bare arm, tone_marks on retrieval`. "(no explanation)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Yes/no as a one-word reply inside a two-line exchange with a stated addressee (grandmother, friend, child) so register and variety are visible and the speaker's own town is requested; never 'give the word for yes and no and note variation'.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your grandmother asks whether you have swept the compound. You have. Write her question and your one-word yes, as a respectful child, and name your town.
  - A friend asks if you are coming to the farm tomorrow. You are not. Write his question and your one-word no.
  - Someone knocks and asks 'is your father at home?'. Answer no, then add 'he went to the farm'. Two short sentences.
  - Write the shortest way you would say 'no' to a child, to a friend and to an elder, one per line, in your own town's speech.

### dialectal_fidelity 6. Market money is counted in named units the model does not have. Both arms answer with an abstract phrase (Ekalọ [money] efu [market]; Eka [money] ẹfi ádṣa) and v4.4 answers with the grammar row's bare numbers (Ogbo meji, oje, ogumelu). Speakers count money in unit words: kọbọ, jẹkà (jẹkà káà = 200 naira), dẹlì (dẹlì káà = 20,000) from annotator_8; pam' (Pam'ẹlu 5, Pam'ogwu 40, Pam'oje 100), akpulu (Akpulu ká / Ọgwọ́kọ́ 200, Akpulu mélu 1,000) from annotator_7; and the verb for 'count money' is gbaluka/kaluka (annotators 6, 8), 'how much is this' is [money] [king] dé.

- Rule a speaker would state: In the market money is named in units, not in abstract numerals: 'count the money' is gbaluka [money], 'how much is this' is [money] [king] dé, and amounts use jẹka/dẹli (or pam'/akpulu/ọgwọkọ) with the vigesimal numeral after the unit. Which unit system belongs to which area or generation is unknown.
- Count: 3 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: the vigesimal numeral row (ogwu 20, ọgbọ meji 40, ooje 50, ọgwọkọ 200) is there; no money unit, no 'count money' verb, no 'how much' question. Evidence we lack.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_011 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ekalọ [money] efu [market]. ... Amọ́nẹ Idah a dọ kí "ekalọ [money]" abí "ka [money]"` -> speaker `1: [money] [king] dé 2: gbalukà [money] leẹ́ 3: kọbọ káà 4: kọbọ mẹgwa 5: jẹkà káà (200 naira) 7: dẹlì káà (20,000) (gold annotator_8)`. "Both output are not correct"
  - ig_bank_dial_011 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Eka [money] ẹfi ádṣa chi ẹñwu ki ami Ígáláà a chẹ` -> speaker `Ojanẹ igala, [road] ka ku ma kaluka [money] efu a ja che "ijẹka" ma du gbaluka (gold annotator_6)`. "Both output do not adequate pass a constructive message (cultural on rag, grammar on bare)"
  - ig_bank_dial_011 [annotator_7 (gold)] (v4.4 (v44-vs-gold.json)): model `Ogbo meji (forty), oje (fifty) kpai ogumelu (one hundred). U ma uchẹ ki ya kọ [money] [hand] omunẹ ñ.` -> speaker `Pam'ẹlu = 5, Pam'ẹgwa = 10, Pam'ogwu = 40, Pam'oje / Ọgbọmélu = 100, Akpulu ká / Ọgwọ́kọ́ = 200, Akpulu mélu = 1,000 (gold annotator_7)`. "v4.4 output only; no unit word appears"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Priced transactions with the naira amount given in the prompt, one utterance each (trader states price, buyer counter-offers, mother says count it), so the unit word must appear and the speaker edits it; each prompt pins one amount so annotator_7's and annotator_8's systems can be compared on the same number.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - A yam seller tells you one tuber costs 200 naira. Write exactly what she says, one sentence.
  - You offer 150 naira for the tomatoes instead of 200. Write your one-sentence counter-offer.
  - Ask the trader how much the whole basket costs, then write her answer: 1,000 naira. Two lines.
  - Your mother hands you 50 naira and tells you to count it before you go. Write her instruction, one sentence.
  - Tell a child that twenty thousand naira is a lot of money, one sentence, with the amount in words.

### dialectal_fidelity 7. Direction versus destination. 'Went to Idah' with ti is the one dialect prompt the bare arm wins outright (3 wins, 1 tie), but the speaker's gold on the same prompt uses a different construction for 'toward' (lo tẹju [hand] ánẹ idah, face the side of the land of Idah) that no arm and no grammar row has; and the direction-word exchange prompt collapsed into both_inadequate with every tag set.

- Rule a speaker would state: Destination: lo ti X. Direction without arrival: lo tẹju [hand] anẹ X (toward X). Left and right are [hand] awọtọ and [hand] awọhi. Only the first is verified; the second is one annotator's form and needs confirmation.
- Count: 5 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Destination ti: yes, and it works (v4.4 'I lo t'Ankpa'). Left/right hand words: yes (grammar row). 'Toward / on the side of': no. v4.4's second sentence on dial_001 ([name] [goat] u du chukọlọ') is opaque and untested.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_dial_001 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Í lo t'Ídah. Àwa a kà [name] [goat].` -> speaker `Ì lo tẹju [hand] ánẹ idah (gold annotator_8, gloss: He/She went toward Idah)`. "(no explanation; tone_marks on the retrieval arm 'Ene le lo Idah. A du [name] kà.')"
  - ig_v42_dial_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ene le lo Idah. A du [name] kà.` -> speaker `Í lo t'Ídah chosen by annotators 7, 5, 3; annotator_8 tie`. "(no explanation; grammar on the retrieval arm, which lacks ti)"
  - ig_v42_dial_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ù kpa gba lẹ́yọ̀ yí n̄. Ù kpa gba n̄yọ̀ kpaí ọ́nẹ́ kí wá uwo [goat] [money] kí a dọ́ ọ́la [hand] [road] [money] n̄.` -> speaker `[hand] awọtọ kpai [hand] awọhi (gold annotator_7, left and right)`. "Both are inaccurate (tone_marks, grammar, cultural on both arms)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Scenes that contrast arriving with heading (toward Idah but only as far as the river; the goat ran toward the farm, not into it) and landmark directions (left after the church, on the Ankpa side of town), one sentence each, no request to explain.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your brother set off toward Idah this morning but he is only going as far as the river. Tell a neighbour where he went, one sentence.
  - A stranger asks the way to the market. It is on the left after the church. Give the direction in one sentence.
  - Tell a child: 'the goat ran toward the farm, not into the farm.' One sentence.
  - Say that the school lies on the Ankpa side of the town. One sentence.

### dialectal_fidelity 8. Code-switching: the retrieval arm describes mixing instead of doing it, the bare arm mixes but its Igala half is wrong, and both use textbook English or Hausa where speakers switch into Nigerian Pidgin. Speakers accept the sentence that actually switches (dial_010: bare wins 3 of 4) and then correct the Igala: ch'álo to n'álo ti (destination needs ti), nẹ to ni (have), final n to ñ, nọ k'ọla to na'kọla or ni ukọla. Speaker switches are 'Abeg', 'make I drink', 'I dey go house', 'my guy', 'Ọya', 'how are you', Hausa 'Nyarinya'.

- Rule a speaker would state: When asked how town speakers mix, write the mixed utterance itself; the switched-in words are Pidgin/English items (Abeg, make I, I dey, my guy, Ọya) and the Igala clause keeps full grammar: destination ti, have = ni, negative ñ, 'we will talk' = a na'kọla / a ni ukọla.
- Count: 14 judgments, 2 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Rule 7 (write the words, never a sentence about them): yes, and v4.4 obeys on dial_010/013/016. Pidgin as the switching register: no, but retrieval carried it in ('make I drink', 'Abeg' in v4.4's dial_013 and dial_016). ti after motion and ni = have: ti yes, ni not stated as 'have'.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_010 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ẹnẹ Ígáláà abíne ki a k'ọla ẹfi éfodṣa é-gbítì kpegwa Ígáláà kpai ọla Enefu ẹfi ọla òókáà` -> speaker `Guy [water] alọ tì school ugbata (gold annotator_8); Uche like afẹ wẹ (gold annotator_6); Lia gwanẹ [head] chair (gold annotator_5)`. "Output B is not the answer because is completely Igala and out put A is correct because is mix with English"
  - ig_bank_dial_010 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Guy, ù ch'álo school abajọyí, but ù nẹ transport n, a nọ k'ọ́la later.` -> speaker `Guy, u n'álo ti school abajọyi, but u ni transport ñ, a ni ukọla later. (edit)`. "Guy i am going to school now, but I don't have transport; we both have gist later"
  - ig_bank_dial_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `a nọ k'ọ́la later` -> speaker `a na'kọla later`. "Output A - a na'kọla later not a nọ'kọla later"
  - ig_bank_dial_013 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Efu Ígáláà kpai English: Du water ñwu mi.` -> speaker `Abeg, ch'[water] mi emó mì make idrink. (gold annotator_8)`. "Output A not correct, Output B is correct"
  - ig_bank_dial_008 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `"Ọda kọ, lafiya lau?" "Naa gba, mutumina."` -> speaker `Fine girl abu'wẹ gbọ, Nya'rinya abu'wẹ gbó (gold annotator_7); Nyarinya a'bẹle (gold annotator_3); Ameriya ọl'[morning] (gold annotator_6)`. "Both Output are wrong (grammar on bare, cultural on rag)"
  - ig_bank_dial_016 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `A: [English] Guy, how far? [Igala] Àbọ́ kíá? B: [Igala] Ólá wéwé. ... ù kọ́kọ́ nẹ́ ẹ́kpá ñ.` -> speaker `A: mọ jọ, my guy dó ló? B: I dey go house ẹ lọ kpo'mì for this sun. Ọya make we gbéré lọ together (gold annotator_8)`. "not accurate (invented on rag, grammar on bare)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Single-utterance scenes with a named interlocutor and the English/Pidgin item given in the prompt ('use the word transport', 'the stop is called Market Junction'), so the Igala clause around the switch is the only thing judged and the speaker's edit lands on Igala grammar, not on the choice of English.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell your friend you have no transport money today, using the English word 'transport' inside an Igala sentence.
  - Text a classmate: 'I'm going to school now, we'll talk later.' Mix in whatever English a young person in Anyigba really would.
  - A bus conductor asks where you are dropping. Answer him in Igala with the stop's English name 'Market Junction'.
  - Greet a Hausa trader in Igala and ask the price of onions, mixing in one Hausa word a speaker in your town would really use.
  - Your friend's phone is off. Tell her later, in one sentence, that her line was not reachable, as a young person in town would say it.

### dialectal_fidelity 9. Tone marks are penalised, not rewarded, by most of this pool. tone_marks was tagged 8 times (6 on the retrieval arm, whose answers carry heavy marks like Ígáláà, Ọ̀là), and the one edit that touched tone removed it (ù to u, abajọyí to abajọyi, tagged tone_marks). In gold, annotator_8 marks tone in 18 of 22 rows, annotator_7 in 3 of 16, annotators 6, 3 and 5 in 0 of 24. This is the opposite of the write-up's instruction and v4.4's 'no tone marks unless asked' sits between the two.

- Rule a speaker would state: Unresolved: speakers 6, 3, 5 write no tone, 7 strips it, 8 marks it fully. A rule cannot be written until the pool is asked for both versions of the same sentence.
- Count: 8 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: v4.4 says 'no tone marks unless the question asks for them' in REGISTER and 'Mark tone as the dictionary and examples do' in ORTHOGRAPHY; the two lines conflict and neither matches the write-up's 'always'.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_010 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `ù ch'álo ... abajọyí ... ù nẹ` -> speaker `u n'álo ti ... abajọyi ... u ni (edit; every segment tagged tone_marks)`. "I made these corrections because of wrong spelling"
  - ig_v42_dial_003 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Òfò ẹ̀jọ òfò ẹ̀tà, ẹ̀lẹ̀ èjì ọ̀kà, ẹ̀lú ẹ̀lú ẹ̀fà ẹ̀fà (fully toned, chosen)` -> speaker `Ofo ẹjọ ofo e'ta èji élèla éjì élèle éjì é'ta éjì (gold annotator_8, partly toned; note the gold reads a different number, 08032924238)`. "(no explanation; tone_marks tagged on the lightly toned retrieval arm instead)"
  - ig_v42_dial_001 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ene le lo Idah. A du [name] kà.` -> speaker `Ì lo tẹju [hand] ánẹ idah (gold annotator_8)`. "(no explanation; tone_marks tag)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Ask for the same short sentence twice: once as the speaker would text it, once with full tone marks. This collects marked gold from the four speakers who never mark, measures whether the model's marks match theirs, and stops tone from contaminating the win/loss signal on dialect prompts.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Write 'come and eat' as you would text it to your brother, then write it again on a second line with every tone mark.
  - Write 'she went to Idah' twice: first as you normally write, then with full tone marks.
  - Write 'how are you' in your town's form twice, plain and then fully tone-marked, and name the town.

**Shapes that produced decisive, explained judgments**

- One concrete utterance in a stated scene, with the variety or the interlocutor fixed and a short 'say which you used' note at most: Produces a single editable sentence; speakers pick a winner and correct words (ti, ni, ñ, na'kọla) instead of writing 'both wrong'. ig_v42_dial_001 was decisive 3 of 4, ig_bank_dial_010 3 of 4, ig_v42_dial_003 2 of 3, ig_bank_dial_013 2 of 3, and these four prompts account for 2 of the 3 edits in the bucket. (ig_v42_dial_001, ig_bank_dial_010, ig_v42_dial_003, ig_bank_dial_013)
- Read a given number or amount aloud: Digit-by-digit output is checkable form by form (ofo/òfò for zero, ẹlẹ four, ẹla nine, ẹfa six) and exposes the model's choice between abstract numerals and unit words; the pooled arms got 2 decisive of 3 on the phone number, and the money prompt shows the gap (jẹka, dẹli, pam', akpulu) that abstract numbers miss. (ig_v42_dial_003, ig_bank_dial_011)
- Ignorance probe: ask for a specific town's form where no source exists and accept 'I do not know' as the correct answer: Directly measures rule 8, which v4.4 still breaks (dial_002, 007, 015, 017 attributions). ig_v42_dial_002 already carried the 'say so plainly' clause and v4.4 answered 'U m'ọla yí ñ'; the pooled arms fabricated instead and were both_inadequate. (ig_v42_dial_002)

**Shapes that produced ties and "both inadequate"**

- 'Give X and note whether it varies between areas / communities / localities': Invites the model to fabricate a town attribution and the speaker to reject both outputs with a one-line verdict; 0 decisive judgments on dial_001, 004, 005, 011, 017, and 1 of 4 on dial_006, 007, 014. Explanations are generic ('Both are wrong', 'Both are inaccurate') and no edits were made because the speaker had to rewrite the whole thing as gold instead. (ig_bank_dial_001, ig_bank_dial_004, ig_bank_dial_005, ig_bank_dial_011, ig_bank_dial_017, ig_bank_dial_014)
- 'Mark any word whose pronunciation differs across areas': Speakers do not produce pronunciation variants: the three gold answers are an unmarked sentence (kọ́ [yam]), a sentence with no variant (Ebi akpa mi) and 'it is pronounced the same everywhere' (Alukate ma k'ọla yi). No s/ch or r/l evidence came out of it, and the model answered with coined words (kpùkẹ̀ chi ádṣa). (ig_bank_dial_012)
- 'Show how a speaker might mix in English or Hausa' or 'mark the switch points' (meta-description of code-switching): The model writes about mixing or produces textbook Hausa (lafiya lau, mutumina), and the 'switch points' instruction makes speakers tie (dial_008: 2 ties, 2 both_inadequate; dial_016: 1 tie, 2 both_inadequate). The same content as a single switched utterance (dial_010) was decisive. (ig_bank_dial_008, ig_bank_dial_016)
- 'Identify a word a neighbouring language shares': Produces ties and both_inadequate (dial_009: 2 ties, 2 both_inadequate, 1 win) because the judgment is about the metalinguistic claim, not the Igala; the speaker gold is a bare phrase plus an English note (Ebi akpa mi, [hand] awọ mi) that teaches nothing the model lacks. (ig_bank_dial_009)

**What the speakers' own writing shows**

- tone marks: Split by annotator, not by rule: annotator_8 marks tone in 18 of 22 gold rows (Lìya k'ego, Àgbà, [morning], jen̄wù, [money] [king] dé), annotator_7 in 3 of 16 and removed the model's marks in the dial_010 edit while tagging tone_marks, annotators 6, 3 and 5 never mark (0 of 24). The tone_marks tag is used loosely for spelling as well (annotator_3 edit rationale 'Wrong Spelling' on kpaa/gba carries it). Heavy marking on the retrieval arm (Ígáláà, Ọ̀là) drew 6 tone_marks tags.
- contractions: Apostrophised elision is normal speaker writing: annotator_8 in 10 of 22 rows (ch'[water], Liy'emì, lẹwa'gọ, Pam'ẹlu, kpo'mì), annotator_7 in 9 of 16 (abu'wẹ, Nya'rinya, Elu'eju, Edọki'yẹ, nyọ'ñ), annotator_6 in 4 of 19 (k'ọla, kp'ẹjẹ, ọl'[morning], ch'ukọlọ), annotator_5 na'kọla. The one counter-example is annotator_7 expanding the model's k'ọ́la to ukọla (a ni ukọla later) while annotator_5 kept the contraction (a na'kọla later). The write-up's 'expanded forms' preference is not current speaker practice.
- ki usage: The dominant complementiser is the quotative string 'ka ki ni' / 'kakini' / 'aka kini' (say that: Abọ Igala okpakpa a ka ki ni 'liya ẹmi', annotator_7; Uchẹ ki bọ aka kini, annotator_6; kakì, annotator_8). Relative/determiner ki appears as 'Uchẹ ki bọ' (those who), [road] ka ku ma kaluka' (annotator_6 uses ku before plural ma, matching the v4.4 rule), 'todu ẹn̄wù ki chí lẹ dẹ' (annotator_7). No speaker marks the kí/kì tone contrast in this bucket except annotator_8's low-toned kakì and abekì; the COMP vs REL distinction the write-up describes is not visible in speaker writing here.
- dialect: 59 of 62 gold rows are general_idah and 3 are 'other' (annotator_3); nobody declares Ankpa, Ibaji or Ogugu. Everything speakers say about other areas is second-hand and framed from Idah: 'Abọ Igala okpakpa' (general Igala people, annotator_7), 'ọgbọ Igala' (annotator_6), 'ojanẹ Igala du' (all Igala land). The areas they name are Idah (8 rows), Ogugu (6), Ankpa (5), Ibaji (3); never Dekina, which the model names 5 times. Attested alternations: come = lia/liya/lẹya (Idah), wa (Ankpa, Ogugu per annotator_8), wẹ mi / la (Ogugu per annotator_7), lẹwa / lua (Ibaji); eat = jẹñwu vs Ibaji jamìn̄wu; how are you = abẹle, abu wele, abu'wẹ gbọ, agbebo, aidẹ, Ibaji ka bu ẹle; mother = [mother] Idah vs iya Ankpa; sibling = omaye Idah vs omada Ankpa/Ogugu; don't be angry = Ekiwedon Idah, Chakwuli Ankpa, Edọki'yẹ Ogugu; buttocks = ofe vs Ibaji ọkpu; thank you = agba / awa / nágo; sit = gwugwu ane / gwu ane; good morning = ọla [morning] / [frozen answer] / ọlọdudu; well done = [sun] ukọlọ / wola ukọlọ; let's go = alọ / nalo; one = oka / enye. No speaker in this bucket produced an s/ch or r/l pair.
- other: 'Or' is abẹki/abeki (annotators 6, 8) or manyi (annotator_7), 'but/while' is ama, never abí or tàbí. 'Please' is agba (sentence-initial or final). 'Give me' is ch'[water] / du ... ñwu mi; 'help me' is bu mi. First person is u (annotator_7 edit), na for 'I will' (Na ñya kp'ẹjẹ, annotator_6), full [water] for emphasis (Guy [water] alọ tì school, annotator_8). Destination takes ti (n'álo ti school, alọ tì school). Code-switching is into Nigerian Pidgin (Abeg, make I drink, I dey go house, my guy, Ọya) and Hausa address terms (Nyarinya, Ameriya). Market money is counted in units (kọbọ, jẹkà, dẹlì; pam', akpulu, ọgwọkọ) with gbaluka 'count'. Toward is 'lo tẹju [hand] anẹ X' (annotator_8). Tag copula appears ([child] onobulẹ yi chi friend i che, annotator_6).

**Open questions (speakers disagree)**

- Yes and no: annotator_6 Iii / Idabẹlẹ-n, annotator_7 Ẹẹ / ññ, annotator_8 Ììí / Oda'a. Is Iii vs Ẹẹ a variety, a register or a spelling difference, and is ññ (which the retrieval arm also wrote) acceptable to annotators 6 and 8?
- Water: annotators 6 and 7 say [water] only ([water] ma dọ ojanẹ Igala du'; [water]), annotator_8 writes [water] abekì [water] oho'. Is oho a variant for water or a modifier (still water), and does the [water]/oho split v4.4 now asserts exist anywhere? Also [water] (annotator_7, dotted) vs [water] (annotators 6, 8).
- Come here by area: annotator_8 puts wá in Ankpa and Ogugu and lẹwa in Ibaji; annotator_7 puts wẹ mi and la in Ogugu; annotator_6's gloss gives lua for Ibaji. v4.4 (dial_015) puts wa in Idah and lia in Ankpa, the reverse of annotator_8. Who is right about Ankpa?
- How are you: abẹle (annotators 6, 3), abu wele and agbebo (annotator_8), abu'wẹ gbọ and ayẹ gbọ (annotator_7), aidẹ (annotator_3), Ibaji ka bu ẹle (annotator_6). v4.4's repaired answer 'abawe' matches none; which of these are the same word differently spelled (abẹle / abu wele / abu'wẹ gbọ) and which are distinct?
- Money units: annotator_8 counts kọbọ, jẹkà (jẹkà káà = 200 naira), dẹlì (dẹlì káà = 20,000); annotator_7 counts pam' (Pam'oje = 100), akpulu (Akpulu ká / Ọgwọ́kọ́ = 200, Akpulu mélu = 1,000, Akpulu meji = 4,000, probably a slip for 2,000). Are these two generations of currency naming (kobo vs pound), two areas, or both current? Neither matches the v4.4 numeral row.
- Tone marks: annotator_8 marks nearly everything, annotator_7 removes the model's marks as errors, annotators 6, 3, 5 never mark. The write-up says always mark; v4.4 says no marks unless asked. Which convention should gold follow, and should tone be judged at all on dialect prompts?
- Contraction: annotator_5 'a na'kọla later' versus annotator_7 'a ni ukọla later' for 'we will talk later' on the same model sentence; is na'kọla a contraction of ni ukọla or a different verb (na = will)?
- Kinship: 'father' is [father] (annotator_3: 'the main igala word is [father]; annotator_6: [father]); annotator_3 adds [father]. Sibling omaye Idah vs omada Ankpa/Ogugu (annotator_7) and mother [mother] Idah vs iya Ankpa (annotator_8) are single-annotator claims that need a second voice.
- The model's claimed Ankpa first-person 'N' (dial_017) and Ankpa 'ọñwu' for ẹñwu: no speaker confirms either; the write-up gives Ibaji uñ for ẹñwu. Is there any pronoun variation by area at all?
- Toward: annotator_8's 'Ì lo tẹju [hand] ánẹ idah' is the only attestation of a direction construction distinct from lo ti X; confirm with another speaker before it becomes a row.
- Which annotators actually come from Ankpa, Ogugu or Ibaji? All 59 dialect fields say general_idah, yet annotators 6, 7 and 8 make confident claims about those areas. The next bank should ask for the speaker's home town on every variety prompt.
- No s/ch or r/l pair was produced by any speaker in this bucket, so the write-up's Ogwugwu r/l rule and the s-to-ch rule have no arena evidence yet; prompts that force li/ri 'see', ikelekwu 'rat', kpali 'roll' in a fixed variety are needed to collect it.

## Register, cultural values, idioms (40 + 19 + 6 judgments; 266 + 76 + 140 gold)

Data read: All 12 review-split files for the three buckets (comparisons, edits, gold, prompts), prompt-informativeness.json filtered to the 23 prompts of my slice, prompt-v4-4.txt (served system prompt), grammar-rows-oct8.json (30 rows), writeup-oct8.txt. Gold was dumped per prompt to /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-halim-Documents-wikitongues--claude-worktrees-fervent-jemison-b6bb05/4e7b27ad-9966-41d9-a2d6-dec139f0636b/scratchpad/gold-reg-dump.txt, gold-cult-dump.txt, gold-idiom-dump.txt and read in full.

### register_cultural_idioms 1. Yoruba doorstep hail Agó o / Agóó used as the arrival call at a house, a compound or a house of mourning. Bare arm does it every time an arrival is staged; speakers tag it not_igala or wrong_language and the rag answer with Agba oo wins.

- Rule a speaker would state: The arrival hail is Agba oo (with the kin or house vocative: Agba oo, abọ [house]) or the host's wọla ulẹ; a shut door is knocked (gbam gbam) and asked to be opened (fi [road] bi mi ta). Agó/Agoo is not Igala.
- Count: 3 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: 'Agó o' is in the NEVER WRITE Yoruba list. New prompts must test whether telling worked, and must also test the positive form (Agba oo + vocative), which the prompt does not give.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_015 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Agóó! Ẹ̀gwá oh.` -> speaker `Agba oo, abo [house] (winning rag answer); gold annotator_7: Ma wola ì yahì ukwu ì`. "Agba not Agoo"
  - ig_v42_reg_006 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Agó o!` -> speaker `gbam gbam fi [road] bi mi ta (gold annotator_7)`. "Both are wrong (failureTagsA: not_igala)"
  - ig_v42_cult_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ọ̀nẹ kí wá: Agó ó!` -> speaker `Uwẹ: Wọla ulẹ / Ọnọnọjọ - Agba nago / Uwe: Gwanẹ (gold annotator_7)`. "Both are inaccurate (tags not_igala, tone_marks, grammar)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Staged arrival with a named addressee and a required second turn (what the host answers). Two turns force the hail plus the reply (Agba nago / wọla ulẹ), so a Yoruba hail is caught and the speaker's dialogue gold teaches the pair. Ask for the words only, no description.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - You arrive at your aunt's compound at midday and nobody is in sight. Give in Igala the words you call out, and what she calls back from inside.
  - A stranger from Ankpa knocks at your door at dusk. Give in Igala the first thing he says from outside and the first thing you say as you open.
  - You enter a house where an old man died yesterday. Give in Igala exactly what you say as you step in, and what the eldest son answers.
  - Your friend's door is shut and you know she is inside. Give in Igala what you call out, then what you say when she opens, in two lines.

### register_cultural_idioms 2. Igbo Nnọ (welcome) imported into homecoming and apology replies, by both arms. Where a speaker caught it the judgment was both_inadequate; where annotator_8 judged, both answers carried Nnọ and the result was a silent tie.

- Rule a speaker would state: Welcome and homecoming use the (w)ọla frame: wọla ulẹ (welcome), wọla [money] (welcome from the farm), reply Agba nago. Nnọ never appears in 482 gold answers.
- Count: 3 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Not in the served prompt: the Igbo line of NEVER WRITE lists only Orie and Nkwọ. Grammar row 5 says 'do not import Nnọ' but only inside the consolation rule. Add Nnọ to the Igbo list and test it.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_012 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Nnọ, onya mi. Ẹ̀kpe gwa, má d'éwù.` -> speaker `[god] mi! Nago ẹki wẹdọñ, múdú báyá mí nago (gold annotator_5)`. "Nnọ is igbo not igala"
  - ig_v42_reg_010 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[mother] mi kà ùbì kakini Nnọ, agba [money] (bare arm: Nnọ́ [money])` -> speaker `Àbò mẹ wola òrọka / [mother] [child] mì àgbá è wolá [money] (gold annotator_8)`. "(tie, empty explanation; both arms used Nnọ)"
  - ig_bank_reg_005 [annotator_6] (gold only): model `n/a` -> speaker `wọla ulẹ / W'ọla ulẹ / Mẹ wọ'lalẹ / wọ́la-ùlẹ̀ / Agba ooo wọla ulẹ (annotators 7, 6, 3, 8, 5)`. "W'ọla ulẹ"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Homecoming and welcome exchanges with a named relation and the hour, two lines each, so the (w)ọla + occasion noun must appear and Nnọ is a visible intrusion. Also an edit-friendly single sentence.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your son comes back from the farm at dusk. Give in Igala what he says at the door and what you, his mother, say back.
  - Your sister returns from three months in Lagos. Give in Igala the first two sentences you say to her at the motor park.
  - A trader you buy from arrives at your house for the first time. Give in Igala what you say as she steps in and what she answers.
  - Give in Igala the one word or phrase a host says to welcome a guest, then the one word the guest answers.

### register_cultural_idioms 3. The ọlafia health formula. The v4.4 prompt bans 'any alaafia shape (lafia, ọlafia)' as Yoruba, yet all five gold annotators write it for 'I am fine' and 'safe journey'. The bare arm's Yoruba-shaped 'Láfíyá wẹ n̄?' was tagged grammar by two speakers, so the fault is the Yoruba frame, not the noun.

- Rule a speaker would state: 'I am fine' is u ch'ọlafia (uchọla'fia nago, Uch'ọlafiya nago); 'safe journey' is lo kpai ọlafia / [god] ki dẹ tanẹ ọlafiya. The question 'how are you' is abẹle / A'idẹ ke, never Láfíyá wẹ?
- Count: 2 judgments, 2 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Told the opposite: v4.4 forbids every alaafia shape. The speakers' gold contradicts the ban for ọlafia/ọlafiya. The new prompts must settle this before v4.4 trains the model out of a form the community uses.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_007 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àgbà ọ̄. Láfíyá wẹ n̄? Ọjà wẹ n̄?` -> speaker `Onuku ábale, ab'[market] det (gold annotator_8); A'idẹ ónobulẹ i (edit annotator_7)`. "Weldone this woman (tag grammar on A)"
  - ig_bank_reg_013 [annotator_5] (gold only): model `n/a` -> speaker `uchọla'fia na'go (annotator_7); Uch'ọlafiya nago (annotator_6); U chọla'fia nago abogijo (annotator_5); U'chọla fia anya (annotator_3)`. "U chọla'fia nago abogijo - to an elder"
  - ig_bank_reg_028 [annotator_6] (gold only): model `n/a` -> speaker `lo kpai'ọlafia (annotator_7); [god] ki dẹ tanẹ ọlafiya (annotator_6); Bọgìjo ulẹ kì cholafìa (annotator_8); [god] ki dẹ tanẹ kpo lafia (annotator_5)`. "[god] ki dẹ tanẹ ọlafiya"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Reply slots only: give the elder's greeting and ask for the reply, or give a traveller and ask for the parting blessing. Ask speakers to write both an answer and what they would NOT accept, so a model answer without ọlafia gets corrected if it is wrong.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - An elder greets you [frozen answer], abẹle?' Give in Igala exactly what you answer, word for word.
  - Your uncle is boarding a bus to Lokoja. Give in Igala the one sentence you say as he leaves.
  - A friend asks how your mother is doing after her illness. Give in Igala your one-sentence answer.
  - Give in Igala the short reply a child gives when a visitor asks how the house is.

### register_cultural_idioms 4. r for l and the wrong woman word: onobirẹ, onokẹrẹ, ọnẹkẹrẹ in Central-variety sentences (the Ogwugwu r/l alternation leaking in), plus the Yoruba market word ọja for [market].

- Rule a speaker would state: woman = onobulẹ; young man / male = onokẹlẹ or ọnẹkẹlẹ; market = [market]. r-forms belong to the Ogwugwu variety and never sit in an Idah or Ankpa sentence.
- Count: 4 judgments, 4 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: onobirẹ and onokẹrẹ are in 'nobody's words', ọja in the Yoruba list, and grammar row 29 gives onobulẹ / onokẹlẹ. Prompts must test whether telling worked, and should also test r/l in words the prompt does not list (rat, see, ladle, roll).
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_016 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `todu wẹ ch'ónokẹ́rẹ` -> speaker `todu wẹ ch'ọnẹkẹlẹ (edit annotator_5)`. "Ọnẹkẹlẹ not ọnẹkẹrẹ"
  - ig_v42_reg_007 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `A'idẹ ónobirẹ agbọ́kọ` -> speaker `A'idẹ ónobulẹ i (annotator_7); A'idẹ [market] eyíní á (annotator_5)`. "I fixed spelling error and deleted what wasn't appropriate"
  - ig_v42_reg_004 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `kpaí ọ́ja efu anẹ lẹ` -> speaker `kpaí [market] efu anẹ lẹ (edit annotator_3)`. "Wrong spelling"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Short sentences that force the woman / young man / market nouns and a second lexical r/l trap, with the dialect named in the prompt so a mixed answer is a visible fault.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In the Igala of Idah, say in one sentence: the woman at the market sells pepper and her son carries the basket.
  - Say in Igala: I saw a rat under the ladle. Then say which Igala area would say it with r instead of l, if any.
  - A young man and a woman are coming back from the market. Give the Igala sentence, then say the same sentence as an Ogwugwu speaker would.
  - Give the Igala words for woman, man, young man and old woman, each in a four-word sentence.

### register_cultural_idioms 5. The acknowledging hail built wrongly: 'agba wẹ' (hail + your), and the tone/vowel of the tag (Agba ọ for Agba o).

- Rule a speaker would state: The reply hail is Agba o / Agba oo or Agba lẹ; agba never takes the possessive wẹ. Shouted replies from the farm: Agba o, Awa.
- Count: 4 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: grammar row 9 gives 'Agba oo' as the general hail and row 22 as a hail of appreciation, but nothing says agba takes no possessive and the lẹ variant is unrecorded.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_011 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ńń, àgba wẹ o!` -> speaker `Agba o (edit annotator_3 of rag 'Agba ọ'); Awa, ágóò (gold annotator_8)`. "You dont say agba wẹ you can say agba lẹ"
  - ig_v42_reg_011 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Agba ọ` -> speaker `Agba o`. "Tone mark wrong"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Call-and-response at a distance (farm, road, river) where the reply must be two or three words; a wrong particle is the whole answer and the speaker's correction is one word.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Someone shouts a greeting to you across the river while you are washing. Give in Igala the two or three words you shout back.
  - A boy greets you 'Agba o' as he passes your stall. Give in Igala what you answer him.
  - You are pounding yam and your neighbour calls 'Wọla ukọlọ'. Give in Igala exactly what you reply without stopping.
  - Give three different two-word Igala replies to a passer-by's greeting, and say who uses each.

### register_cultural_idioms 6. Dotted vowels dropped or doubled where meaning changes: [road] for [road] (tomorrow), nyo for ñyọ, and the rag arm's retrieval spelling Ígáláà for Ígálá, amon'Ígáláà for abo igala.

- Rule a speaker would state: Tomorrow is [road]; good is ñyọ; the people are Ígálá / abo igala with a single final vowel. A missing dot or an extra à is a different word, not a tone slip.
- Count: 5 judgments, 4 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: The seven-vowel rule is told; Ígáláà is not named and the rag arm keeps producing it from its retrieval corpus. Rows for [road] / ñyọ do not exist.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_009 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónuku mi, [road] ki nyo. / Agba, [road] ki nyo.` -> speaker `Ónuku mi, í ch'[road]. / Agba, [road] ki ñyọ. (edit annotator_5); Nāgo chi iko omune (gold annotator_8)`. "Tomorrow in igala is [road] not [road]"
  - ig_v42_reg_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Idah chi [goat] Ígáláà ki dẹ efu Kogi State.` -> speaker `Idah chi [goat] Ígálá ki dẹ efu Kogi State. (edit annotator_5); Idah chì lọcál gomenti kì dì ojane státì kogì (gold annotator_8)`. "I corrected the spelling"
  - ig_v42_idiom_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ígáláà a kà kakini` -> speaker `Ígálá a kà kakini (edit annotator_5)`. "Because of wrong words. Sentence, spellings and tone mark"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Minimal-pair sentences where the undotted spelling is a real other word, asked with the instruction to mark tones, so the speaker's correction isolates one character.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Say in Igala, with tone marks: I will come tomorrow, not today.
  - Say in Igala: the road is good, and the market is far. Mark the tones.
  - Write the Igala name of the Igala people and of their land in one sentence, with tone marks.
  - Say in Igala: tomorrow the women will go to the river. Mark the tones.

### register_cultural_idioms 7. Proverbs and blessings coined on the spot. The model wraps an English image in Igala words ([hand] ki d'[money] 'the hand that holds money'; ẹ́dọ́ wẹ a fù 'cool heart'; Ẹ́nya wẹ a tà) and attributes them to 'the Igala'. No speaker gave any of these; speakers quote a stable repertoire.

- Rule a speaker would state: A proverb must be one the community says. The stable repertoire in gold: [hand] ki gwura ọnwu ejọ alọ (4 gold, 2 annotators); Achichi ki ma gbọla-n a lẹbọ ọkwu tẹfura (6 gold, 4 annotators); Ẹñwu kẹ gbẹ ọ ñwu ẹja / ẹñwu ki ọnẹ gbẹ ya ja (7 gold, 4 annotators); Alu ma mujọñ ya fifon; Ẹñwu ki ogijo dachi anẹ li, imọtọ tẹgwu [head] [tree] ki gbọgba li-n; Imọtọ f'[hand] ñwu gwẹ fẹ ya b'ogijọ jẹñwu; Enwu ke bo' maneke'chen ogwu nekechen no'o; Ẹfi akpa gbẹ ... ẹ ni ukpahiu ki ya chẹ ñ. Work is ch'ukọlọ, buy is l'ẹñwu, a calm heart is ẹdọ bọ.
- Count: 6 judgments, 3 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Not told. v4.4 says never coin a word and bans 'ra' for buy, but says nothing about proverbs needing attestation, and the GRAMMAR block has no proverb or blessing formulas. The 39 ig_bank_idiom prompts with 140 gold were never run against the Gemini arms.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_idiom_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ígáláà a kọ̀là: [hand] ki d'[money], onwu a j'ẹñwu.` -> speaker `Ígáláà a kọ̀là ka ki ni: [hand] ki ch'ukọklọ, onwu a j'ẹñwu. (edit annotator_7)`. "I restructured the sentence to be appropriate"
  - ig_v42_idiom_002 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `[money] wá ra ẹ́nya wẹ ... [god] kí k'ọ́dọ́ wẹ, ẹ́dọ́ wẹ a fù.` -> speaker `[money] wá l'ẹ́ñwu wẹ ... [god] kí dẹdọ ẹbọ wẹ, ẹ́dọ́ wẹ a bọ (edit annotator_5)`. "To buy in igala is lẹñwu"
  - ig_v42_idiom_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `[child] kí kpa [hand]-ñwú fẹ́, í a jẹ́ñwú kpaí am'ógbó.` -> speaker `Imọtọ f'[hand] ñwu gwẹ fẹ ya b'ogijọ jẹñwu (gold annotator_6, ig_bank_idiom_008); Ìmòtọ kì fì [hand] nwu gwẹ nyo nyo ajeñwu kpì ìbogìjo (gold annotator_8)`. "Good thing in Igala is ẹñwu ẹnyọ not k'ẹñwu ñyonyo"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: A concrete situation, then 'give the proverb an elder would quote, in Igala only'. No English exegesis in the same answer (it hid the fault and produced ties). Score against the gold repertoire: an attested proverb wins, a coined one is both_inadequate with the real one written in.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - A man who set a trap for his neighbour has fallen into trouble himself. Give in Igala only the proverb people would say about him.
  - A boy keeps ignoring his grandmother's warnings. Give in Igala the one proverb she would quote to him, nothing else.
  - Two sisters refuse to speak to each other and the household work is failing. Give in Igala the proverb an aunt would say to them.
  - A farmer planted well but the rains did not come. Give in Igala the proverb that fits, and in one English sentence say when it is used.
  - A young man has been given a seat among the elders because of his conduct. Give in Igala the proverb that explains why.

### register_cultural_idioms 8. Register strength incongruity: the bare arm answers a two-word speech act with a pile of blessings, titles and exclamations (Agba kakini!, Ochu mi, [king] mi!, [god] ki ... three times), and inserts non-Igala fillers (Ọ̀dọ̀fa, Dọọ). Speakers prefer the short formula and cut the excess.

- Rule a speaker would state: Thanks to a child: Na'go, [child] mi or [child] mì àgbakocho. Reply to an elder's thanks: Àgbá [mother], ékìkwúra-n. Gratitude for a big favour: Awa, omaye mi + one blessing ([god] k'ajiọgbọ nwu wẹ). One blessing per utterance; kakini is 'said that', not an exclamation.
- Count: 7 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: '~7-word sentences' and 'give the answer only'. Nothing says one blessing per utterance or names the short thanks formulas (na'go, awa, àgbá + vocative). The write-up names this as word strength out of place.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Agba kakini! U wẹ agba nwi [money] ukọchẹ [child] mi ki wẹ san. ... [god] ki chẹwun nwẹ. [god] ki dabi fi kọ wẹ. [god] ki k'[hand] t'[child] wẹ mọ.` -> speaker `Agba todu [money] ukọchẹ [child] mi kú wẹ rá ... [god] ki chẹwun nwẹ. [god] ki du [money] wẹ dabí wẹ. (edit annotator_5); Awa, omaye mi. Ẹnyọ kẹ chẹ nwu [child] mi lẹ, afu chọ mi ẹfọwọ. [god] k'ajiọgbọ nwu wẹ (gold annotator_7)`. "I correted the word, spelling and sentence"
  - ig_v42_reg_013 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Awa, [mother]. Ọ̀dọ̀fa n.` -> speaker `Awa [mother] (rag, won); Àgbá [mother] ékìkwúra-n (gold annotator_7)`. "Ọdọfa is not igala"
  - ig_v42_reg_008 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àgbà, [child] mi. [god] kí ch'ùbìọ́jọ́ nwẹ. Úwẹ ch'[child] ẹ́nyọ́.` -> speaker `Na'go, [child] mi (rag, won); [child] mì àgbakocho (gold annotator_8)`. "(a wins; tag tone_marks on B)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Everyday speech acts with a word budget stated in the prompt ('in at most five words'), so excess is measurable and the speaker's gold is a formula, not a paragraph. Pair child and elder addressee for the same act.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your small son hands you your hoe. Thank him in Igala in at most four words.
  - An old woman thanks you for fetching her water. Answer her in Igala in at most five words.
  - A neighbour returned your lost goat. Give in Igala what you say, in one sentence with at most one blessing.
  - Say 'thank you' in Igala first to a child, then to your grandmother, then to a chief, each in its shortest form.

### register_cultural_idioms 9. Yoruba and invented vocabulary in expository cultural prose from the bare arm (ewn for ẹñwu, Amaa, abeki, todudu, okokolobia, ẹgbẹ, ọla-[child], pin, álúfíá, Óñwu, Ámí Gáágá), and in the rag arm (Utokown, ádṣa, onka, ojoji, taku). Speakers tagged wrong_word, wrong_language, invented, and deleted ẹgbẹ.

- Rule a speaker would state: Family is akwọra [house]; youths are ami agbadufu / abo okolo; thing is ẹñwu; and is kpai / ka ki; but is amá/muda; the eldest son is [child] ọgba; inheritance is ẹñwu ogwu. No age-grade word ẹgbẹ.
- Count: 12 judgments, 2 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Mostly told: ẹgbẹ, àmàà, abẽki/abẹki, alaafia, Utokown, ádṣa, onka, ojoji are in the ban lists and oñ is marked Bible register. Not told: the share verb kpe ñwu, the joiner ka ki, the tag copula i chẹ (grammar row 21 only), [child] ọgba for eldest son.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_cult_038 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Akwọra [house] kpai ẹgbẹ ami agbadufu a ch'úkọ́lọ́ ọgbọgaga ọjanẹ Ígáláà.` -> speaker `Ẹfu Akwọra [house], ami agbadufu a ch'úkọ́lọ́ ọgbọgaga ọjanẹ. (edit annotator_7)`. "kpai ẹgbẹ is not supposed to be there"
  - ig_bank_cult_037 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `anẹ kpai uku chi ewn ki nẹ [money] nwi ọla-[child] ... Amaa, ma dọmọ a nẹ uwe ki kọ ọla ma, abeki ma du anẹ nwi ma` -> speaker `Alẹju ọgwuchẹkpọ, chẹ ñwu ku ma tẹnẹ kpa anẹ'ogwu ami [child] ọnẹkẹkẹ katẹtẹ ma kpẹ ñwu (gold annotator_7)`. "(b wins; tags on A: wrong_word, grammar)"
  - ig_bank_cult_037 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `abọ ọgijọ akwọra [house] a kanya ógwu kpai ane ... oñ ma a du ógwu wewe` -> speaker `abọ ọgijọ akwọra [house] a kpe ñwu ógwu kpai ane ... ka ki ma kpẹ ẹñwu ọgwu ki bọ (edit annotator_4)`. "to make the sentence make sense"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Expository prompts only with a sentence cap (three sentences) and a required fact list, and only where gold already converges (inheritance: five annotators agree). Ask speakers for a sentence-by-sentence gloss in the explanation, which annotator_7 did on cult_038 and which was the most informative explanation in the slice.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In three Igala sentences: who inherits a father's land, who shares it out, and what a daughter receives instead.
  - In two Igala sentences, say what the extended family does when a member dies and what the youths do for the town.
  - In three Igala sentences, name the [father], the Achadu and the Igalamela and say in one clause what each does.
  - In two Igala sentences, say what a first son is expected to do in his father's house.

### register_cultural_idioms 10. The biography line: the rag arm makes a dummy subject 'ene le' (the person) and writes efu before a town; the bare arm writes bíi and ọ̀dọ̀. Speakers disagree on efẹwọ versus efu [goat], and the preferred object is the full pronoun onwu.

- Rule a speaker would state: Ma bí onwu efẹwọ Ankpa efu ọdọ 1952 (he or she was born in Ankpa in 1952). Town takes efẹwọ (one word, annotator_7) or ẹfu [goat] (two words, annotator_8); state and year take efu.
- Count: 3 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Told: 'before a town name it is efẹwọ' and grammar row 1 says never two words. annotator_8's gold writes it as two words, so the rule itself needs the test.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ma bi ene le efu Ankpa efu odo 1952.` -> speaker `Ma bi ene le efẹwọ Ankpa efu odo 1952. (edit annotator_7); Ma bí onwu / Ẹfu [goat] Ankpa / Efu ọdọ 1952 (gold annotator_8)`. "Just made a spelling error change"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One-line factual sentences with a town, a state and a year, varying which comes first, so the preposition choice is isolated and the edit is one token.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Write one Igala sentence: she was born in Idah in 1960 and now lives in Abuja.
  - Write one Igala sentence: the school was built in Ankpa in Kogi State in 1975.
  - Write one Igala sentence: he works in Lokoja and his wife trades in Dekina.
  - Write one Igala sentence: the meeting is in Ayangba on the fourth day of the second month.

### register_cultural_idioms 11. High-tone kí stamped on every linking slot by the bare arm: relative clauses ([goat] kí dẹ́), optative blessings ([god] kí ch'[hand] wẹ), and quoted sayings. Speakers' gold writes kì or unmarked ki on relatives, 'ka ki ni' as 'that' after say or inform, and 'ku ma' before a plural subject. annotator_5 also wrote kú before wẹ.

- Rule a speaker would state: kí with high tone is the complementiser (that, after verbs of saying) and ka ki ni is the formal 'that'; kì or ki introduces a relative clause and the optative ([god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ); ku precedes the plural clitic ma (ku ma di [name]) and, per one annotator, 2sg wẹ.
- Count: 6 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: v4.4 gives 'Relativizer kì (singular), ku before plural ma/me' and the optative 'subject + kì + verb', but does not say that kí is the complementiser, so the write-up's COMP/REL contrast is not in the prompt. Nothing covers kú before wẹ.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_reg_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Idah chí [goat] kí dẹ́ efu Sítétì Kogi` -> speaker `Idah chì lọcál gomenti kì dì ojane státì kogì (gold annotator_8); Idah chi [goat] Ígálá ki dẹ efu Kogi State (edit annotator_5)`. "(b wins; tag tone_marks on A)"
  - ig_v42_reg_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `[money] ukọchẹ [child] mi ki wẹ san. Ẹñwu ki wẹ chẹ yi` -> speaker `[money] ukọchẹ [child] mi kú wẹ rá. Ẹñwu kú wẹ chẹ yi (edit annotator_5)`. "(segments tagged tone_marks: ki to kú)"
  - ig_bank_cult_001 [annotator_7] (gold only): model `n/a` -> speaker `Ichọlọ [name] ẹdu nwọ [child] chi [god] ku ma di [name] nwi [child] ẹtito i chẹ (gold annotator_7)`. "[god] ku ma di [name]"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Paired sentences, one with 'that' after a verb of saying and one with 'who/which', same lexical content, with tones required; and a may-God blessing. The speaker marks three particles and the contrast is readable.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - With tone marks, say in Igala: (a) I heard that the chief came; (b) the chief who came is my uncle.
  - With tone marks, say in Igala: (a) she said that the yam is ready; (b) the yam which she cooked is ready.
  - Give in Igala, with tone marks, the blessing 'may God give you children' and the statement 'God gave her children'.
  - Say in Igala: the women who went to the river have come back; mark the tone on the linking word.

**Shapes that produced decisive, explained judgments**

- Staged speech act with a fixed social frame (who, where, hour) and a two- to five-word expected answer: Decisive judgments (3 of 4 on reg_005, reg_007, reg_011) with one-word corrections and 'X not Y' explanations; the fault is the whole answer so even a terse annotator names it. (ig_v42_reg_005, ig_v42_reg_011, ig_v42_reg_015, ig_v42_reg_016)
- Two-turn dialogue (what they say, what you say) in a set scene: Even when both models failed, the gold came back as a labelled exchange (Wọla ulẹ / Agba nago / Gwanẹ / gbọ [water] kẹ mọ) that teaches the full formula pair and exposes Igbo or Yoruba hails. (ig_v42_cult_002, ig_v42_reg_010, ig_v42_reg_009)
- Lexical trap sentence where the likely Yoruba or Igbo form is a different word: Produced the five explicit explanations in the slice: Nnọ is igbo, Agba not Agoo, Ọdọfa is not igala, Ọnẹkẹlẹ not ọnẹkẹrẹ, To buy is lẹñwu. (ig_v42_reg_012, ig_v42_reg_013, ig_v42_idiom_002)
- Situation then 'give the proverb an elder would quote' (gold evidence, never yet compared on Gemini): 140 gold answers converge on a stable repertoire quoted by two to four annotators each (snake in the hole, stubborn fly, sow and reap, lips and whistle), giving a scoring key for coined proverbs. (ig_bank_idiom_008, ig_bank_idiom_012, ig_bank_idiom_018, ig_bank_idiom_034)
- Expository prompt with a sentence cap where annotators gloss sentence by sentence: annotator_7's cult_038 explanation glossed each rag sentence and named the intruder (kpai ẹgbẹ); annotator_4's edit on cult_037 replaced kanya, oñ and added the tag copula i chẹ. Only works when the gold already converges (inheritance). (ig_bank_cult_038, ig_bank_cult_037)

**Shapes that produced ties and "both inadequate"**

- Encyclopaedic register one-liners ('as a reference book would'): Two of three judgments tie with empty explanations on reg_001 and reg_002; annotators have no shared criterion for Wikipedia register and the only edits were spelling (Ígáláà, efẹwọ). (ig_v42_reg_001, ig_v42_reg_002, ig_v42_reg_003)
- Long 'describe the titles / the system' exposition with both arms writing paragraphs: Judgments say 'Both output are correct', 'Output be is more appropraite', 'Nothing is wrong'; one annotator pasted naira counting as the explanation. No form is named. (ig_bank_cult_036)
- 'Give the Igala words and explain in English the picture inside them': The English exegesis dominates the answer and launders coined proverbs; annotator_8 tied both; mixed-language text cannot be edited cleanly and a wrong proverb with a fluent explanation reads as right. (ig_v42_idiom_001, ig_v42_idiom_002)
- Any pair judged by annotator_8 alone: 9 of 11 register judgments by annotator_8 are ties with empty explanation, including pairs where both arms wrote Nnọ; a prompt routed only to this annotator returns nothing measurable. (ig_v42_reg_004, ig_v42_reg_010, ig_v42_reg_017)
- Open hypotheticals where the model can refuse or hedge ('if such a custom exists', 'say plainly where you are unsure'): The hedge invites meta-commentary; cult_002 rag added 'Akwọra [house] Igala omune a che ojoji u ma gba-n' with nobody's words, and the judgment was both_inadequate with no form named. (ig_v42_cult_002, ig_bank_cult_019)

**What the speakers' own writing shows**

- tone marks: Mostly unmarked. annotator_8 marks often but inconsistently (Àgbá onayì, Ọla-odúúdú, kì, chì, dì, 'Ẹ́nẹ̀'); annotators 7, 6, 5, 3 write bare vowels with occasional marks (Àgbá [mother] ékìkwúra-n, Amíye). The write-up's 'always mark tone' is not current practice: roughly one gold in five carries any mark, and marks are also used to flag vowel quality (ẹ/ọ) rather than pitch.
- contractions: Heavy apostrophe elision is the norm, against the write-up's expanded-form rule: Ọl'[morning], W'ọla ulẹ, Ch'ẹgbatugba, uchọla'fia, Ai'dẹ, Na'go, M'ẹgba, ch'ukọlọ, k'amonẹ, mu'du baya mi, aj'ẹñwu. annotator_6 contracts most; annotator_5 and 7 mix ([frozen answer] / wọla'ulẹ). Negation is written five ways: ñ, 'ñ, -n, ǹ, -ǹ (jẹñwu ñ, gẹ-n, che-n, chẹ ǹ, kwane-nwu).
- ki usage: Unmarked ki for relative and optative ([god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu, ẹnẹ ki chi imọtọ); annotator_8 writes kì (abo kì chẹ ewewe, Idah chì ... kì dì); 'that' after say or inform is ka ki ni / ka ki (na tẹnẹ ka ñwu mẹ ka ki ni); before plural ma the form is ku (ku ma di [name], ku ma jẹ dama, ku ma lotoko); annotator_5 wrote kú before 2sg wẹ in an edit. High-tone kí appears in gold only once (annotator_8, ábó kí dé gbaí). The polite volitive na tẹnẹ / na tene opens requests in 4 of 5 annotators.
- dialect: Self-declared: register gold 56 general_idah, 51 ankpa, 27 other, 132 unlabelled; cultural 46 idah, 26 ankpa; idiom 46 idah, 53 ankpa, 22 other. Ankpa (annotator_8): Onàyì 'elder', bọgìjo, kòchò 'please', ab'[market] 'how is market', Ọla-odúúdú, àgbá onayì. Idah (annotators 5, 7): abogijo / abọ ọgijọ, [father], [mother], nago, A'idẹ ke, giyọ [god] or todu [god] 'please'. annotator_3 (other): Atai 'sir', Ko'cho, Ọmaye a'bẹle, [father] anya 'thank you'. annotator_6: Abẹle 'how are you', Ọl'[morning], Dọga for a chief, Nago. Loanwords are kept with their letters: Imam, pastor, churchi, weeki, festivalu, lọcál gomenti, státì, récọdu, Wikipedia.
- other: Honorific plural mẹ to elders and groups (Mẹ wọla, Mẹ gba chaka, mẹ mudu ba'ya); kneeling is described (danye'kwu, dupẹ tẹkpẹ baki dañyẹkwu) rather than lexicalised. Court address Gabaidu / Agabaidu (4 annotators); chief greeted Ajọfẹ or by title. Yes-no particle ba or á (dachi nyoyo ba?, eyíní á). Farewell Ch'ugba t'ugba (5 annotators) or í ch'[road]. Blessing frame [god] ki + verb ([god] ki d'ẹnyọ ñwu [child] mi, [god] ki du gbọ mẹ). Data hygiene: annotator_8 submitted annotator_7's text verbatim as own gold on at least 9 cultural prompts (cult_001, 006, 008, 009, 010, 013, 014, 015, 016) and reg_006's 'Ẹlolu nyọ nyọ nwọjọ ba' is identical across annotators 8, 3, 5, so attestation counts overstate independent agreement.

**Open questions (speakers disagree)**

- ọlafia / ọlafiya: the v4.4 NEVER WRITE list bans every alaafia shape, but annotator_7 (uchọla'fia), annotator_6 (Uch'ọlafiya), annotator_5 (U chọla'fia nago abogijo, kpo lafia), annotator_3 (U'chọla fia anya) and annotator_8 (ulẹ kì cholafìa) all use it for 'I am fine' and 'safe journey'. Either the ban must be narrowed to the Yoruba frame (Láfíyá wẹ?) or the community is using a loan it accepts. Needs Salem's ruling and a dedicated prompt.
- ágóò as a reply: annotator_8 gold reg_011 'Awa, ágóò' versus annotator_5 'Agba not Agoo' (reg_015) and annotator_7 not_igala on 'Agó o!' (reg_006). Is ágóò an Igala response particle distinct from the Yoruba doorstep hail?
- efẹwọ Ankpa (annotator_7 edit, grammar row 1) versus Ẹfu [goat] Ankpa written as two words (annotator_8 gold reg_002).
- Relativizer ku before 2sg: annotator_5 wrote kú wẹ rá and kú wẹ chẹ (reg_014 edit); v4.4 says ku only before plural ma/mẹ.
- Peer greeting at the market: A'idẹ ónobulẹ i (annotator_7) versus A'idẹ [market] eyíní á (annotator_5) versus Onuku ábale, ab'[market] det (annotator_8). Three formulas, two share A'idẹ; which is Idah, which Ankpa?
- Plain thanks: grammar row 22 says anya, but nago / na'go is what annotators 5, 6, 7 write (reg_003, 013, 029) and annotator_8 writes àgbá; anya appears only in annotator_3. The row under-represents nago.
- Please: nago (5, 6), ko'cho / kòchò (3, 8), todu [god] / giyọ [god] (7), agba (6, 8). Is kòchò Ankpa-only?
- 'State' in a Wikipedia line: Kogi State (rag, won), Sítétì Kogi (bare, tagged tone_marks), státì kogì (gold annotator_8 with s). The name-letters rule and the no-s rule collide here.
- Negation spelling: ñ versus -n versus ǹ; v4.4 mandates ñ but annotator_6 writes gẹ-n, che-n and annotator_8 writes ǹ and -nwu. Should the arena normalise or record variants?
- Igbo market-day names: gold cult_007 and cult_027 give the four Igala market days as Ede, Eke, Afor, [market] (annotators 7, 8), while v4.4 bans Orie and Nkwọ as Igbo. Afor and Eke are Igbo names too; check with Salem whether the Igala names are these loans or something else before training on either.

## Every speaker correction as a token diff (213 edits)

Data read: review-split/edits-diffs.json (all 213 edits, all 474 hunks read in full), prompt-v4-4.txt (served v4.4 system prompt), grammar-rows-oct8.json (30 rows, topics and content), writeup-oct8.txt (Salem/Lydia write-up, full), review-split/comparisons-*.json (637 comparisons, only failure-tag totals and winner split used: grammar tagged on 280 comparisons, tone_marks 102, wrong_word 85, cultural 39, wrong_language 27, invented 23, not_igala 18, english_mixed 2; winners b 170 / both_inadequate 168 / tie 157 / a 142), review-split/v44-vs-gold.json (249 prompts: v4.4 answer text, metrics, and gold texts with annotator and self-declared dialect; used for regression grep and per-annotator tone and apostrophe rates in gold).  Primary-category totals over 474 hunks: WORD_SUBST 72, INS_WORD 28, INVENTED_DEL 28, FOREIGN_WORD 27, DEL_PAD 27, META_EN 27, KI_FAMILY 23, DOT_VOWEL 18, RESPELL_OTHER 17, E_PREFIX 16, NUMERAL 16, PRONOUN_NUMBER 15, VERB_CHOICE 13, TONE_STRIP 13, NASAL_N 13, EFU 11, REWRITE 10, IGALAA 10, LINKER 10, NAME 9, R_L 9, LOAN_TO_IGALA 8, LOAN_NATIVIZE 8, NA_TENE 8, CONTRACT 7, TI_MOTION 6, WORD_ORDER 5, LE_YI 5, EXPAND 3, COPULA 3, TONE_ADD 3, PUNCT_CASE 2, Q_PARTICLE 2, S_CH 1, LOAN_PREFERRED 1. Any-tag totals (hunks / distinct edits / distinct prompts): INVENTED_DEL 43/36/22, KI_FAMILY 42/36/28, FOREIGN_WORD 40/31/24, TONE_STRIP 35/29/25, DEL_PAD 35/30/26, NASAL_N 33/31/25, DOT_VOWEL 33/26/22, INS_WORD 33/25/22, E_PREFIX 31/26/18, PRONOUN_NUMBER 31/25/20, META_EN 29/18/10, CONTRACT 28/24/22, IGALAA 22/20/16, VERB_CHOICE 21/17/15, NUMERAL 20/16/10, EFU 19/18/14, EXPAND 18/15/15, LOAN_NATIVIZE 17/15/11, R_L 16/15/11, COPULA 14/14/12, S_CH 13/12/7, NAME 13/10/6, LINKER 12/11/8, NA_TENE 11/9/7, LOAN_TO_IGALA 11/11/10, TONE_ADD 11/8/7, TI_MOTION 10/10/6, LE_YI 10/10/10, Q_PARTICLE 5/5/5.  Arm split worth knowing: E_PREFIX 28 of 31 hunks, IGALAA 22 of 22, INVENTED_DEL 40 of 43, NUMERAL 19 of 20, DOT_VOWEL 27 of 33, R_L 14 of 16 are on the retrieval arm (rag-v3): these are corpus-contamination failures that retrieval introduces. KI_FAMILY (25 vs 16), FOREIGN_WORD (22 vs 18) and PRONOUN_NUMBER (15 vs 16) are shared with the bare model.  v4.4 regression grep over its 249 answers: Ígáláà 89 hits in 46 prompts (still failing); 'efu' + capitalised town 9 prompts and ef' before a consonant 5 prompts (rule present, still failing); 'kì ma' / 'ki ma' 6 hits in 5 prompts vs 'ku ma' 80 (mostly fixed); word-final -wñ 3; é- prefix 0; Ida without h 0 (Idah 14); Ónobirẹ/onokẹrẹ 0; banned Yoruba words 0; meta 'U kà' 0; oñ 0; ǹ/n̄ 0; ojonwawn 2. Apostrophes per answer 1.31 (v4.4) vs 0.75 (gold); toned word share 0.12 vs 0.18; words per answer 21.8 vs 17.75.
