### edit-diffs 1. The language and people name is written Ígáláà by the retrieval arm (and by v4.4 in 46 of 249 answers); speakers never keep that form. They write Igala (annotator_6 x4, annotator_4 deletes it, annotator_7 replaces it) or Ígálá (annotator_5 x2). 22 hunks, 20 edits, 16 prompts, all on gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3. This is the single most frequent form v4.4 still emits that no speaker accepts.

- Rule a speaker would state: The name of the language and the people is Igala (toned: Ígálá). Never Ígáláà, never a doubled final vowel. The plural 'the Igala people' is abo Igala / abọ Igala (annotator_5 wrote 'abo igala' for the model's amon'Ígáláà). Treat this as a retrieval artifact: the corpus spelling must not leak into answers.
- Count: 0 judgments, 20 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: No. The v4.4 prompt never mentions the spelling of Igala itself; its orthography rule 'mark tone as the dictionary and examples do' licenses the corpus form. Regression: v4.4 writes Ígáláà 89 times across 46 answers.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_035 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ígáláà` -> speaker `Igala`. "Ene nyo -> Ẹnẹ ẹnyọ; Ígáláà -> Igala; ómù -> omu (edit with no rationale)"
  - ig_v42_reg_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ígáláà` -> speaker `Ígálá`. "I corrected the spelling"
  - ig_v42_reg_016 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `amon'Ígáláà` -> speaker `abo igala`. "I corred the words and spelling"
  - ig_v42_auth_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `kó eli Ígáláà wa` -> speaker `recordi ami eli adugbẹ wa`. "A woman called Zara Adejoh has come from the Nigerian Television Authority to record your community's songs."
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Any one-sentence prompt whose answer must name the language or the people. Measurable by a single string match; the speaker correction is a one-token diff whose direction (Igala vs Ígálá) also records the speaker's tone habit.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In one Igala sentence, tell a visitor that the people of Idah speak Igala and that you yourself are Igala.
  - Write the opening sentence of an Igala Wikipedia article about the Igala language: what it is called and where it is spoken.
  - Your cousin asks whether your children speak Igala at home. Answer her in one Igala sentence.
  - Say in one Igala sentence that Igala people and Hausa people both live in Lokoja.

### edit-diffs 2. ki-family errors. (a) ki/kí/kì before the plural pronoun ma where speakers write ku: 11 hunks, 10 edits, 8 prompts, 4 annotators (ki -> ku, kí -> ku, ki -> kú, 'tọdu ki' -> 'to du ku ma'). (b) ki or k'ẹ used where two verbs chain and speakers write kẹ: 5 hunks (lex_013 'kì tá' -> 'kẹ nya ti' and 'kẹ fú'; gram_032 'k'ẹ' -> 'kẹ'; gram_027 'kí wẹ nọ́' -> 'kẹ tọnọ'). (c) ki inserted by speakers as the clause opener of an announcement ('ka ki ni', 'ka ki de', 'do ki Zara'): 4 hunks, 4 annotators. (d) idle kí deleted (gram_017). No speaker ever changed kí to kì or kì to kí: when they touch a toned kí they strip the tone (auth_003 'nyọ́nyọ́ kí' -> 'nyọnyọ ki'; auth_005 'kí ch'ẹ́ñwu' -> 'ki ch'ẹñyo'). 42 hunks any-tag, 36 edits, 28 prompts; 25 on the bare arm, 16 on rag-v3.

- Rule a speaker would state: Relativizer agrees in number with its head: kì/ki with a singular head, ku before plural ma or me. kẹ, not ki, joins two verbs of one subject (go and switch on; carry and put). An announcement runs 'na tẹnẹ ku kà ... ka ki ni ...'. The COMP kí vs REL kì tone contrast in the write-up is not something the annotators mark in edits; they write toneless ki in both positions.
- Count: 280 judgments, 36 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes for (a) and (b): 'Relativizer kì (singular), ku before plural ma/me' and 'kẹ links verbs; kì/ki starts a new clause - never swap them'. Regression: v4.4 still writes kì ma / ki ma in 5 of 249 answers against 80 ku ma, so (a) is mostly but not fully fixed. (c) is in the grammar rows (na tẹnẹ row) but not in the static prompt. The write-up's kí/kì tone contrast is not in v4.4 and the annotators do not enforce it.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_lex_009 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `todù kí ma chẹ allura` -> speaker `todù ku ma di ogwu`. "Di ogwu ñwi ami ọmọtọ - igala Meaning to give drug to children"
  - ig_v42_reg_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `ki ... ki` -> speaker `kú ... kú`. "I correted the word, spelling and sentence (two separate ki -> kú hunks in one edit)"
  - ig_v42_lex_013 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `t'òde kì tá` -> speaker `kẹ nya ti`. "Wrong word"
  - ig_v42_lex_013 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `t'òde kì tá jẹ́nì` -> speaker `t'òda kẹ fú jẹní lẹ tájo`. "Lo’tọda not lo’tode Meaning go outside"
  - ig_v42_gram_032 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `k'ẹ ch'ónokẹ́rẹ imoto` -> speaker `kẹ chokolobia`. "I  corrected the words and spelling"
  - ig_v42_auth_002 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ka ku ma do Zara Adejoh` -> speaker `ka ku ma do ki Zara Adejoh`. "Ónobulẹ ka ku ma do ki Zara Adejoh chẹ wa kw'ugbo NTA todu ki kó eli Ígáláà wa."
  - ig_v42_gram_036 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `(absent)` -> speaker `ka ki de`. "I made that correction to complete the sentence"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One-sentence translations with a plural head noun plus relative clause (forces ku ma), a singular head (forces kì), two chained actions by one subject (forces kẹ), and a 'X said that Y' complement (forces the COMP slot). The speaker's edit is then a one-token diff on exactly the particle.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In one Igala sentence, say that the men who fish on the river have all come back.
  - In one Igala sentence, say that the woman who sells pepper has gone home.
  - Tell your sister in one Igala sentence to go to the kitchen and bring the pot.
  - The chief said that the meeting is tomorrow. Write that in one Igala sentence.
  - Write two lines in Igala: a blessing over a traveller (may God keep you), then the warning 'do not travel at night'.

### edit-diffs 3. Speakers strip tone marks far more than they add them. TONE_STRIP: 35 hunks any-tag, 29 edits, 25 prompts (annotator_6 16 strips / 0 adds; annotator_4 8 / 0; annotator_3 3 / 0; annotator_7 6 strips / 4 adds; annotator_5 2 strips / 7 adds). TONE_ADD: 11 hunks, 8 edits, 7 prompts, and 8 of the 11 are numerals in prompts that asked for 'full tone marks' (orth_011, orth_012) or counting (gram_021). On the same prompt that asked for full tone marks (orth_011) annotator_7 added tones ('Ógwú Ógwú-ẹ́gwā Ọ̀gbọ̀méji') and annotator_5 did not ('Ogwu Ogwuẹgwa Ọgbọ mẹji'). Gold toned-word share per annotator: annotator_6 0.00, annotator_4 0.00, annotator_5 0.05, annotator_7 0.05, annotator_3 0.25, annotator_8 0.39.

- Rule a speaker would state: Default register is toneless (the v4.4 REGISTER line matches the majority of speakers). Tone marks are a per-request feature, and even then only some annotators comply. The write-up's 'tone marks always present' is the practice of two annotators (3 and 8), not of the five who edit. A prompt that wants tone must ask for it and should present a minimal pair so the mark is load-bearing.
- Count: 102 judgments, 29 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes and contradictory: REGISTER says 'no tone marks unless the question asks for them', ORTHOGRAPHY says 'Mark tone as the dictionary and examples do'. The speakers side with the REGISTER line. v4.4 output tones 12% of words vs 18% in gold (gold is pulled up by annotators 3 and 8).
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_orth_002 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Añkpa` -> speaker `Ankpa`. "Just a normal n without tone marks"
  - ig_v42_auth_001 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `dẹ` -> speaker `de`. "The tone mark was not required"
  - ig_bank_auth_012 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Àgbá [god]` -> speaker `Agba [god]`. "(edit with no rationale; whole answer reduced to the toneless form)"
  - ig_v42_orth_011 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ogwu Oguegwa Ogwu mẹji` -> speaker `Ógwú Ógwú-ẹ́gwā Ọ̀gbọ̀méji`. "I made spelling corrections"
  - ig_v42_orth_011 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Oguegwa Ogwu` -> speaker `Ogwuẹgwa Ọgbọ`. "I corrected the spelling"
  - ig_v42_gram_013 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `U de` -> speaker `Í dẹ`. "Í dẹ he was there  Not U dẹ meaning i was there"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Two shapes. (1) Minimal-pair prompts: name two words that share letters and differ by tone, use each in a sentence, mark tone. The edit then shows whether the speaker marks tone when it carries meaning. (2) 'Write with full tone marks' on a closed list (numbers, days, body parts): measurable (toned-word share) and reveals which annotators comply.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Give two Igala words that are spelled with the same letters but differ only in tone. Use each in a short sentence and write the tone marks on both.
  - Write the Igala numbers from one to ten with full tone marks, one per line.
  - Write the Igala words for 'farm' and 'money' in one sentence each, with tone marks, so a reader cannot confuse them.
  - Write the days of the Igala market week with full tone marks.

### edit-diffs 4. Contraction runs both ways, and the direction is diagnostic. Speakers CONTRACT frequent verb-plus-object idioms (28 hunks any-tag, 24 edits, 22 prompts): kọ’la, Gbọ’la, gw’[hand] (x3), l’ẹñwu (x2), dọ’tákàdá, dọ́wọ́, k'eli, Am'[child], k'ùbí, di’wọ, ta’kpa, chọ’lafía. They EXPAND when the model's apostrophe hides a wrong word or clips a clitic (18 hunks, 15 edits, 15 prompts): k'u á -> u neke, ch'ọ́w'àwọ́tọ -> chí [hand] awọtọ, nw'ẹ -> ñwẹ, k'ma -> ku ma, amon'Ígáláà -> abo igala, k'ọ́la -> ukọla (initial u restored), ef'ádṣa -> efu [market]. Per annotator (contract/expand): annotator_5 12/8, annotator_6 6/5, annotator_3 5/1, annotator_7 3/4, annotator_4 2/0. v4.4 writes 1.31 apostrophes per answer vs 0.75 in gold; gold apostrophes per word: annotator_6 0.135, annotator_3 0.087, annotator_4 0.079, annotator_7 0.068, annotator_5 0.044, annotator_8 0.04.

- Rule a speaker would state: Contract only attested verb-plus-noun collocations where the noun's first vowel drops (kọ'la, gbọ'la, gw'[hand], l'ẹñwu, k'eli). Never contract a pronoun or particle onto a verb (k'u, k'ma, k'ẹ, nw'ẹ), never clip a noun-initial vowel (ukọla, ọtakada, onokẹlẹ, uyí stay whole), and never contract across two apostrophes in one word (ch'ọ́w'àwéhì). The write-up's preference for expanded forms is not the annotators' default; if the bank wants expanded forms it must ask for both forms side by side.
- Count: 0 judgments, 24 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly. v4.4 gives the elision rule (drop the first vowel, apostrophe at the joint) and bans stripping a word-initial vowel, but tells the model to contract ('apostrophized elision') with no list of licit collocations, so it over-contracts (1.31 vs 0.75 apostrophes per answer) and contracts clitics (k'u, k'ma). The write-up asks the opposite (expanded forms).
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_006 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ro ọla` -> speaker `kọ’la`. "Ẹ ki Kọ’la wewe meaning dont talk too much"
  - ig_bank_auth_008 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Gba ọla` -> speaker `Gbọ’la`. "Gbọ not gba"
  - ig_v42_gram_030 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Wẹ kpa [hand]` -> speaker `Ẹ gw’[hand]`. "I corrected the words, spelling and sentence (kpa [hand] -> gw’[hand] three times in the edit)"
  - ig_v42_lex_018 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ch'ọ́w'àwọ́tọ` -> speaker `chí [hand] awọtọ`. "Awehi is not igala"
  - ig_bank_dial_010 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `nọ k'ọ́la` -> speaker `ni ukọla`. "I made these corrections because of wrong spelling"
  - ig_v42_lex_009 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `k'ma du ogwu nw'àmì [child]` -> speaker `ku ma du ogwu ñwu abimọtọ`. "It makes sense but need a little corrections to be well constructed"
  - ig_v42_lex_011 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `du tákàdá ... dù [hand]` -> speaker `dọ’tákàdá ... dọ́wọ́`. "Some weren’t needed"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Ask for the same sentence twice: once with every word written in full and no apostrophe, once as spoken with elisions. The pair makes the contraction rule explicit in the gold and lets the edit show which joints speakers actually contract. Use idioms the speakers contracted (talk, take care, wash hands, buy things).
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Write 'take care of yourself' in Igala twice: once with every word in full and no apostrophe, once as you say it aloud with the elisions.
  - Translate 'I bought things at Idah market this morning' into Igala twice: first with every word written out, then as spoken.
  - Tell a child to wash his hands before eating, in Igala, written in full and then as spoken.
  - Write 'the child of the woman' in Igala in full and then contracted.

### edit-diffs 5. Incompletive marker written as a hyphenated or standalone prefix (é-du, é-nyọ̀, é-gbítì, é-kà, é-ténè, É-kọ̀là, á fu, á chi, A á kọ). 31 hunks any-tag, 26 edits, 18 prompts; 28 of 31 on the retrieval arm. Speakers drop it (é-du -> du, é-nyọ̀ -> ñyọ / enyọ / che nyọ) or fuse it onto the verb (á fu -> afu, á chi -> abi, A á kọ -> Akọ, á ch' -> aná chí, é-mẹ́rú -> a’mẹ́rú).

- Rule a speaker would state: No hyphenated prefixes. When the incompletive is needed the speakers write it fused to the following verb (afu, abi, akọ, ach'ẹnẹ) or as a' with an apostrophe (a’mẹ́rú), not as a standalone á; most of the model's é- tokens were simply superfluous and were deleted.
- Count: 0 judgments, 26 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: 'Igala has no hyphenated prefixes - never é- ... The incompletive is the standalone word á'. Regression: 0 hyphenated prefixes in 249 v4.4 answers, so the ban works. But the second half ('standalone word á') is contradicted by the speakers' fused forms (afu, abi, akọ); nobody checked whether v4.4's standalone á gets accepted.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_009 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `é-du uredo ... é-gbítì ñw e` -> speaker `du urẹdọ ... ẹgbiti ñwẹ`. "(edit with no rationale; [child] -> [child], Ígáláà -> Igala in the same edit)"
  - ig_bank_auth_019 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `é-nyọ̀` -> speaker `ñyọ`. "Ñyọ not ẹ’ nyọ́"
  - ig_bank_auth_017 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `á fu` -> speaker `afu`. "(edit with no rationale; also Wẹ á ch'é-gbítì -> Uwẹ ach'ẹnẹ ẹgbiti)"
  - ig_v42_auth_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `é-mẹ́rú` -> speaker `a’mẹ́rú`. "Woman in Igala is onobulẹ not onobirẹ"
  - ig_v42_auth_003 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `á chi` -> speaker `abi`. "Output not well constructed"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Tense triplets: the same event as happening now, done yesterday, and to be done tomorrow, one line each. Measurable (count of á / fused a-forms) and the speaker's edit shows how the incompletive is actually written.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your mother asks where your brother is. Answer in three short Igala sentences: he is eating now; he ate this morning; he will eat again tonight.
  - Write three Igala lines: the rain is falling; the rain fell yesterday; it will rain tomorrow.
  - Say in Igala that the women are cooking now and will serve the food when the men come back.

### edit-diffs 6. Nasal spelling. The negation nasal written n, ǹ or n̄ by the model is rewritten ñ by annotators 5 and 7 (n -> ñ, ǹ -> ñ, 'ma n' -> 'ama ñ', 'nwi mi n' -> 'wu mí ñ'); annotator_6 instead writes a hyphenated -n (duu ǹ -> du-n; alu Igala-n; ojoji-n; gold imọtọ-n). Inside words the model writes ny / nw / u where speakers write the ñy / ñw digraphs ([house] -> [house], jẹu -> jẹñwu, nwi -> ñwu, nwẹ̀ -> ñwẹ, ómù -> óñwu, nyo -> ñyọ, [goat] -> ẹñwu). 33 hunks any-tag, 31 edits, 25 prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: Negation is a clause-final ñ (two annotators) or -n (annotator_6): the bank should record both, not pick. Word-internal nasals are the digraphs ñw and ñy: ẹñwu, ñyọ, [house], jẹñwu, ñwu, ñwẹ; nw / ny spellings are the Yoruba-looking error. The word-final -wñ the model writes (ch'ewñ, bẹwñ) is rejected (ch'ewñ -> che).
- Count: 0 judgments, 31 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes for the negation ñ ('negative nasal written ñ'; 0 ǹ/n̄ left in v4.4 output) and for 'never end a word in -wñ' (3 -wñ still in v4.4). Only implicitly for the ñw / ñy digraphs (listed in the letter inventory, no example). Annotator_6's -n convention is not represented anywhere.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `jẹu` -> speaker `jẹñwu`. "Jẹñwu  not jẹu"
  - ig_v42_lex_008 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ǹ` -> speaker `ñ`. "i just changed the tone mark"
  - ig_v42_lex_008 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `duu ǹ` -> speaker `du-n`. "Because it was not exactly correct"
  - ig_bank_auth_011 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[house]` -> speaker `[house]`. "I made corrections to the spelling and and the tone"
  - ig_v42_gram_042 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `la ch'ewñ` -> speaker `che`. "Deleted the words that are not approprate  and made corrections"
  - ig_v42_gram_029 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ma n` -> speaker `ama ñ`. "Adjusted the spelling"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Negative sentences (two negations in one answer so the mark appears twice) and sentences built from ñw/ñy words (thing, good, house, eat, to/for). Measurable by regex; the edit is a one-character diff that also records which negation convention the speaker uses.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In two Igala sentences, say that you do not eat fish and that your brother does not drink palm wine.
  - Tell a child in Igala: eat the good thing in the house, do not eat the thing on the ground.
  - Say in Igala that the woman did not come and the children have not eaten.

### edit-diffs 7. Dotted vowels and the copula vowel. The model drops or adds the dot (koche -> kọchẹ, odo -> ọdọ, Abobule -> Abobulẹ, omune -> omunẹ, egba -> ẹgba, imoto -> imọtọ, [money] -> [money] where 'farm' not 'money' was meant, lò -> lọ, nyate -> nyatẹ). The copula vowel is unsettled across annotators: annotators 3, 5 and 7 write chi (che -> chi, chẹ -> chí, le che -> lẹ chi), annotator_6 writes che (chẹ -> che, chi akọchẹ -> che ak'onẹ ẹñwu i che). DOT_VOWEL 33 hunks any-tag, 26 edits, 22 prompts (27 of 33 on the retrieval arm); COPULA 14 hunks, 14 edits, 12 prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: ẹ and ọ are separate letters and change the word ([money] farm vs [money] money; ọdọ year vs [name] name; de vs dẹ). The equative copula is chi for most annotators and che for annotator_6; chẹ is accepted by neither. Record the speaker's variety with each copula correction.
- Count: 0 judgments, 26 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes (rule 6 'a dotted vowel makes a different word'; 'Copulas: chi/chẹ = is'). The dot errors persist on the retrieval arm because retrieved examples carry undotted spellings. The chi/che/chẹ split is not addressed and chẹ, the form the prompt offers, is the one speakers replace.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_034 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[money]` -> speaker `[money]`. "Wrong spelling"
  - ig_v42_auth_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `le che` -> speaker `lẹ chi`. "I correted the spelling"
  - ig_v42_auth_014 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `che` -> speaker `chi`. "Wrong spelling"
  - ig_v42_auth_002 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `chẹ` -> speaker `che`. "Because it was not rightly constructed"
  - ig_v42_gram_005 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `koche` -> speaker `kọchẹ`. "The output was not well constructed"
  - ig_v42_gram_041 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Abobule` -> speaker `Abobulẹ`. "Wrong tone marks"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Sentences that force a dotted/undotted minimal pair in the same answer (farm and money; year and name) plus two equative copulas, so the diff isolates the vowel and the copula. Ask the speaker to state their variety.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In one Igala sentence, say that your father is a farmer and your mother is a trader.
  - Write one Igala sentence that uses both the word for farm and the word for money.
  - Say in Igala: the name of the town and the year it was founded are not known.
  - In one sentence, say that Musa is a teacher and his wife is a nurse, and say which Igala variety you write.

'Next week' is aladi ki ya wa / aladi ki awa-i; 'south' is opata/apata; 'market' is [market]; 'left hand' is [hand] awohi, 'right hand' [hand] awọtọ.
- Count: 41 judgments, 36 corrections

- Prompt shape that elicits it: Short everyday sentences that need 'market', 'next week', 'south', 'left/right hand', 'what', 'all', 'outside', so a non-word is a single detectable token and the speaker's replacement is the attested word.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Say in Igala that your neighbour's son has gone south to Lokoja and will come back next week.
  - Which is your left hand and which is your right? Say both in Igala, one line each, and say whether you use the same words to direct a driver.

### edit-diffs 9. r for l in core words (the write-up's Ogwugwu r vs central l): Ónobirẹ 'woman' -> Ónobulẹ (8 hunks, 4 annotators; annotator_5 once wrote Ónobilẹ), onokẹ́rẹ 'man' -> onokẹ́lẹ / ọnẹkẹlẹ / Ónokẹlẹ (5 hunks, annotators 5 and 7), a chira -> ma chiya, rọ̀ -> lọ̀, ra -> la. 16 hunks any-tag, 15 edits, 11 prompts; 14 on the retrieval arm. No annotator ever wrote an r-form; one (annotator_5) wrote rá for 'pay'.

- Rule a speaker would state: In the Central/Idah variety every annotator writes, these words have l: onobulẹ, onokẹlẹ, la (buy), lọ. r-forms belong to the Ogwugwu variety and must not appear in a Central answer (one variety per answer).
- Count: 0 judgments, 15 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: As word bans only ('onobirẹ, onokẹrẹ' are in the nobody's-words list; 0 hits in v4.4 output). The r/l rule itself and the one-variety rule are not stated, so the next r-word will not be caught.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_auth_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónobirẹ i` -> speaker `Ónobulẹ ki du`. "Woman in Igala is onobulẹ not onobirẹ"
  - ig_v42_auth_002 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónobirẹ` -> speaker `Ónobilẹ`. "Ónobulẹ not ónoburẹ  Because the spelling is wrong"
  - ig_v42_lex_005 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ónobirẹ` -> speaker `Ónobulẹ`. "Wrong spelling"
  - ig_v42_gram_029 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `nokẹ́rẹ ... a chira` -> speaker `onokẹ́lẹ ... ma chiya`. "Adjusted the spelling"
  - ig_v42_reg_016 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ch'ónokẹ́rẹ` -> speaker `ch'ọnẹkẹlẹ`. "I corred the words and spelling"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Ask the speaker to name their variety, then give the write-up's own l/r test list (see, rat, roll, ladle) plus woman and man in short sentences. The answer measures variety consistency and the edit records the l-forms per annotator.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Say which Igala variety you speak. Then write in that variety, one short sentence each: I see the rat; roll the mat; bring the ladle; the woman is cooking.
  - Write 'a man and a woman came to the house' in Igala, and say which variety you wrote.
  - In Igala, tell a child to look at the woman selling oranges.

### edit-diffs 10. 'In' before a town: the model writes efu Abuja, ef Idah, Ef (clipped before a consonant); speakers write efẹwọ / efewo before a town (annotators 4, 5, 6, 7) and restore efu before a consonant (Ef -> Efu, annotators 5 and 7). 19 hunks any-tag, 18 edits, 14 prompts; 16 on the retrieval arm. Four annotators converged on gram_005 (efu Abuja -> efẹwọ Abuja by 7 and 4; efewo by 3; annotator_6 kept efu).

- Rule a speaker would state: efu before a state, a year or a common noun (efu Kogi, efu ọdọ, efu [goat] yí); efẹwọ (also spelled efewo) before a town name; ef' only before a vowel; never a bare Ef/ef.
- Count: 0 judgments, 18 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes, exactly ('in is efu, clipped to ef' only before a vowel ... before a town name it is efẹwọ'). Regression: v4.4 still writes efu before a capitalised name in 9 answers and ef' before a consonant in 5, so the rule is stated but not followed; the bank should keep this as a measurable regression item.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_005 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `ṣ'uṣe efu` -> speaker `chukọlọ efewo`. "Appropriate word is chukọlọ efewo"
  - ig_v42_gram_001 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ef` -> speaker `efẹwọ`. "I corrected the spelling"
  - ig_v42_auth_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ef` -> speaker `Efu`. "I correted the spelling"
  - ig_v42_auth_005 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `efu` -> speaker `efẹwọ`. "I heard your voice; I will come and see you in Eko after I finish harvesting in the farm"
  - ig_v42_gram_032 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `efewo lẹ` -> speaker `efu [goat] yí`. "I  corrected the words and spelling"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One sentence that needs 'in' before a town, a state, a year and a common noun at once, so one answer tests all four slots and the diff isolates each.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In one Igala sentence: Musa was born in Ankpa, went to school in Kogi State, and in 2019 he moved to Abuja.
  - Say in Igala that your aunt sells cloth in the market in Idah and lives in a house near the river.
  - Write one Igala sentence saying the festival is held in the town every year in December.

### edit-diffs 11. Destination after a motion verb: the model wrote 'àwéhì tì Lokoja' (invented) and bare lọ Lokoja; five annotators on gram_006 converged on lo + ti but spelled it five ways (loti, leti, lotì, le tì, lo ti); elsewhere lọ -> lotí (annotator_5), lọ -> lọti (annotator_5), lo -> loti ulẹ, and ti inserted (lex_020). 10 hunks any-tag, 10 edits, 6 prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: A destination takes ti after the motion verb (lo ti Lokoja); before a vowel it elides (t'Idah). The fused spelling (loti / lotí / lọti) and the le/lo vowel are unsettled among speakers and should be collected, not legislated.
- Count: 0 judgments, 10 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes ('A destination follows a motion verb with ti: lo ti Idah'); v4.4 writes lo ti 18 times. Fixed as a rule; keep two regression prompts and use them to settle the fused spelling.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_006 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `àwéhì tì` -> speaker `loti`. "The people of Ajaokuta said that Ibrahim had already left for Lokoja'"
  - ig_v42_gram_006 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `àwéhì tì` -> speaker `leti`. "The people of Ajaokuta said that Ibrahim had already left for Lokoja"
  - ig_v42_gram_006 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `lọ Lokoja kpa` -> speaker `lo ti Lokoja`. "The output was not well constructed"
  - ig_v42_orth_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `lọ` -> speaker `lotí`. "I corrected the words and spelling"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One sentence with two or three destinations (left for X, will go to Y, come back to Z) and a request to write the fused and spaced spelling.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Say in Igala: my brother has left for Ankpa; tomorrow he will go to Lokoja and come back to Idah on Friday.
  - Tell a driver in Igala that you are going to Anyigba and your friend is going to Ejule.

### edit-diffs 12. Numerals and dates (NUMERAL 20 hunks any-tag, 16 edits, 10 prompts; 19 on the retrieval arm). Where two annotators corrected the same number they agree: 18 and 19 drop the mẹ- prefix (Megwejo megwela -> Egwejo Egwela / Ẹgwẹjọ ẹgwẹla, annotators 7 and 5), 85 is ọgbọmẹlẹ ñyọ mẹlu (both; the model's 'ogwẹlẹ kpai ẹlu lewa' used kpai), 200 is Ọ̀gwọ́kọ́ (both), 30 rejects Oguegwa (Ógwú-ẹ́gwā / Ogwuẹgwa), 'three years' needs mẹ- (odo eta -> ọdọ meta/mẹta, both), 70 is ẹ̀tẹẹ̀gwa, four is mẹ̀lẹ̀, 14 is ẹgwẹlẹ not ẹgwẹfa. They disagree on 40 (annotator_7 Ọ̀gbọ̀méji; annotator_5 Ọgbọ) and 100 (annotator_5 Ógwúmẹ́lu; annotator_7 kept Ọgbọ mẹ́lu). Date 14 September 2018: both wrote month-ordinal first, 'nọlu mi', day cardinal, then ọdọ 2018 (annotator_7 'ọchu ẹkẹla nọlu mi [god] mẹgwẹlẹ ọdọ 2018'; annotator_5 'ochu ẹkẹ’la nolu mi ẹgwẹlẹ efu ọdọ 2018').

- Rule a speaker would state: 2 to 10 take mẹ- (mẹji, mẹta, mẹlẹ, mẹlu); 11 to 19 are ẹgwẹ-forms without mẹ- (ẹgwẹjọ 18, ẹgwẹla 19, ẹgwẹlẹ 14); 20 ogwu, 30 ogwu ẹgwa, 70 ẹtẹẹgwa, 80 ọgbọmẹlẹ, 85 ọgbọmẹlẹ ñyọ mẹlu (linker ñyọ, never kpai), 200 ọgwọkọ; 40 and 100 need a ruling. Dates: month ordinal, nọlu mi, day cardinal, ọdọ + digits.
- Count: 0 judgments, 16 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly. The static prompt says numerals take mẹ- and gives the date formula; the grammar rows give 21, 30, 40, 50, 70, 80, 200 and the ñyọ linker. The teens (no mẹ-) are nowhere, and 40 and 100 are contested between the rows (ọgbọ meji 40) and annotator_5 (Ọgbọ 40, Ógwúmẹ́lu 100).

  - ig_v42_gram_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `ẹ́gwẹ̀fà ochù Sẹptẹ́mba ọdọ́ ọ̀kẹ́gwá ñyọ ẹ́gwẹ̀jọ` -> speaker `ochu ẹkẹ’la nolu mi ẹgwẹlẹ efu ọdọ 2018`. "14th is ẹgwẹlẹ not ẹgwẹfa"

  - ig_v42_gram_012 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `che odo eta` -> speaker `chi ọdọ meta`. "I fixed spelling error and added some words"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Counting runs and ages: 11 to 20 one per line; mixed counts in one sentence (forty women and eighty men); ages (fifty, seventy, ninety, a hundred); one more date. Each is a closed list so disagreement between speakers is visible token by token.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Count aloud in Igala from eleven to twenty, one number per line.
  - Say in one Igala sentence that forty women and eighty men came to the meeting.
  - Say in Igala that your grandfather is ninety years old and your father is fifty.
  - Write one hundred, two hundred and one thousand in Igala, one per line.
  - A clerk asks your mother's date of birth: 3 March 1961. Write what you would say, as one spoken Igala line.

### edit-diffs 13. Pronoun person and number. 1sg u written where 3sg i is meant (gram_013 'U de' -> 'Í dẹ'; auth_010 u -> i), singular wẹ where plural wa (auth_001), singular ẹ where plural mẹ for a mother calling children (auth_014 'ẹ wá' -> 'mẹ wa'; lex_014 mẹ inserted), a where ma (gram_029 'a chira' -> 'ma chiya', twice by two annotators), full wẹ where the clitic ẹ (gram_030), clitic wẹ where emphatic úwẹ (reg_014), plural prefix am' where abo (gram_006 'Am'Ajaokuta' -> 'Abo Ajaokuta'; reg_016 'amon'Ígáláà' -> 'abo igala'), redundant plural dropped ('abo adọkita' -> 'dọkita'), possessive mi where wẹ/wa ('ọmaye mi' -> [child] wẹ'; 'ọ́mami' -> [child] wa'). 31 hunks any-tag, 25 edits, 20 prompts, evenly split across arms.

- Rule a speaker would state: Subject clitics: u = I, i = he/she, ẹ = you (sg), mẹ = you (pl), a = we, ma = they; full forms (úwẹ, awa, ama) only for emphasis or a one-word answer. Reported third-person speech must not slip into first person. Plural of a people word is abo/abọ + noun (abo Ajaokuta), not am'.
- Count: 0 judgments, 25 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes, the pronoun table is in the prompt ('Preverbal u = I ... he/she i'). The failures are usage, not knowledge: third-person reports and plural addressees. The am' vs abo plural is not in the prompt.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_013 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `U de` -> speaker `Í dẹ`. "Í dẹ he was there  Not U dẹ meaning i was there"
  - ig_bank_auth_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `wẹ` -> speaker `wa`. "Because wa is more than one person and wẹ s use for one person"
  - ig_bank_auth_014 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `ẹ wá jẹ [king]` -> speaker `mẹ wa jẹwñu`. "I made the corrections to change the tone and spelling"
  - ig_v42_gram_029 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `a ch'ẹyẹ kpaí` -> speaker `ma ch'íya kpa`. "I corrected the spellings"
  - ig_v42_gram_006 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Am'Ajaokuta kà kíni` -> speaker `Abo Ajaokuta kakini`. "The output was not well constructed"
  - ig_v42_reg_014 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `wẹ` -> speaker `úwẹ`. "I correted the word, spelling and sentence"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Prompts where the grammatical person is forced by the scene: a third person being reported on; several addressees; your (sg) vs your (pl); a contrast sentence that needs the emphatic full pronoun.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your friend asks about your uncle. Say in one Igala sentence that he lived in Lagos for ten years and came back last year.
  - You call your three children to come and eat. Say it in one Igala sentence.
  - Say in Igala: 'your mother is calling you' twice, once to one child and once to several children.
  - Say in Igala: 'I did not take it, HE took it', with the stress on he.

### edit-diffs 14. Announcement frame. Where the model wrote 'u kà' / 'u á kà' (I say), speakers rewrote the opening as na tẹnẹ ku kà ... ka ki ni (annotators 5, 6, 7 independently on lex_009, lex_010, idiom_001, idiom_002, auth_003). 11 hunks any-tag, 9 edits, 7 prompts. Three annotators on lex_009 converged on the frame.

- Rule a speaker would state: When the speaker announces, informs, requests or promises, the opener is na tẹnẹ (ku kà ... ka ki ni ...), first person na, not u. Vocative first ([mother], ọ́yà, Agba abọ ọgijọ), then na tẹnẹ.
- Count: 0 judgments, 9 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: In the grammar rows (two rows: the na speaker row and the polite volitive row), and v4.4 output uses na tẹnẹ in 12 answers. Not in the static prompt, whose pronoun table says I = u with no exception, so the two sources conflict.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_lex_009 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[mother], u kà nw'ẹ, abọ asibiti` -> speaker `[mother] [child], na tẹnẹ ku kà nw'ẹ, ka ki ni abọ asibiti`. "Mother i want to tell you that Health workers are coming to the village next week to give the children a vaccine."
  - ig_v42_lex_009 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[mother] u kà nw'ẹ` -> speaker `[mother] na tene ku ka ñwẹ kakiki`. "It makes sense but need a little corrections to be well constructed"
  - ig_v42_lex_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `u á kà ukọla òókáà` -> speaker `na tene ku kọla káá`. "Dọkitọ aná chí ukọlọ ọlá [money] wẹ meaning Doctor is going to do an operation on your husband body"
  - ig_v42_idiom_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `u` -> speaker `Na tẹnẹ ku ... ka ki ni`. "I restructured the sentence to be appropriate"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: 'Tell X that ...' prompts with an audience named (a mother, a group, an elder) where the natural answer is an announcement or a request.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell the women's group in Igala that you would like to inform them that the meeting has moved to Saturday.
  - Tell your father in Igala that you want to tell him something about the school fees.
  - Announce in Igala to the compound that the well will be cleaned tomorrow morning.

### edit-diffs 15. Handling verbs. The model's du/gba/chẹ where speakers want di 'give' (auth_013 'I ti lọ' -> 'di [market] ñwu'; gram_026 'gbà kẹkẹ' -> 'di añya'; lex_010 'du [goat] -> 'di’wọ'; gram_034 'dù òkìtì' -> 'di òkpẹti', 'dẹ àtè' -> 'di ọkọlọ'; lex_009 'chẹ allura' -> 'di ogwu'; auth_001 'chẹnyọ' -> 'd'ẹnyọ'), and du where the plural object needs the plural second verb (gram_035 'kó [yam] lẹ du t'ane [tree] -> 'kó [yam] lẹ jọ ofe [tree], exactly the write-up's kó ... jọ agreement; annotator_7 on the same prompt only inserted ọfẹ 'under'). 21 hunks any-tag, 17 edits, 15 prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: di = give / hand over; du = take or carry one; kó = take or carry many; after kó the placing verb is plural (jọ), after du singular (tẹ). gba is 'take/receive' and not a general 'take X somewhere'.
- Count: 0 judgments, 17 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: 'du = take one, kó = take many' is in the prompt and the grammar rows have du X tẹ / kó X jọ. di 'give' is absent, so the model keeps reaching for du/gba.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_035 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `du t'ane` -> speaker `jọ ofe`. "I. Corrected the word"
  - ig_v42_gram_035 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `du t'ane [tree]` -> speaker `du t'ane ọfẹ [tree]`. "I added that to complete the sentence"
  - ig_v42_gram_026 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `gbà kẹkẹ` -> speaker `di añya`. "Can I take your bicycle to the market"
  - ig_v42_lex_009 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `chẹ allura` -> speaker `di ogwu`. "Di ogwu ñwi ami ọmọtọ - igala Meaning to give drug to children"
  - ig_v42_gram_034 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `rọ̀ dù òkìtì ... dẹ àtè` -> speaker `lọ̀ di òkpẹti ... di ọkọlọ`. "I corrected the sentense"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Imperatives that chain give, take-one, take-many and put, with the object count fixed by the scene, so the verb choice and the plural second verb are both forced.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell your son in Igala: give the goat water, then carry the yams and put them in the barn.
  - In Igala: take one plate from the shelf and put it on the table; then take the cups and put them in the basin.
  - Tell your sister in Igala to bring the pot and the plates, and to give the baby to your mother.

### edit-diffs 16. Yoruba, Igbo and Hausa words and the s letter. ra 'buy' -> lẹ/la (6 hunks, 4 annotators), [hand] -> [money], nla deleted or replaced (3 annotators), ọ́ja -> [market], ọ́sẹ -> aladi, Nnọ 'sorry' -> Nago or deleted (3 annotators), Tí/tí/tèmi removed (annotator_3), ṣe/ṣ'uṣe/ùṣe 'work' -> chi ukọlọ / chukọlọ (annotators 5 and 3), sáyíkọ́lọ́jì -> cháyíkọ́lọ́jì, oke -> Ọjuwọ, ohimini -> aji, ode 'outside' -> oda/[name]/nya ti (three annotators, three forms), allura -> ogwu, kẹkẹ -> añya, ẹgbẹ deleted, fi removed, san -> rá. 40 hunks any-tag, 31 edits, 24 prompts; 22 on the bare arm, 18 on rag-v3. One counter-case: annotator_5 kept the lafia shape ('aláfíya ma wà' -> 'chọ’lafía bá').

- Rule a speaker would state: Extend the NEVER WRITE list with Nnọ (sorry is nago), oke (hill is ọjuwọ), ohimini (river is aji), ode (outside is oda/[name]), kẹkẹ (bicycle is añya), allura (medicine/injection is ogwu), san (pay), fi, tèmi. s and ṣ are not Igala letters: ch.
- Count: 27 judgments, 31 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Largely yes: ra, Yoruba money, nla, ọja, ọsẹ, tí, ẹgbẹ, ṣe/se, alaafia shapes are banned and show 0 hits in v4.4 output. Not banned: Nnọ, oke, ohimini, ode, kẹkẹ, allura, san, fi, tèmi.

- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Say in Igala that you bought yams and pepper at the market and paid two thousand naira.
  - Say 'sorry, I am late' to a friend in Igala.
  - Describe your village in two Igala sentences: there are many hills and a big river runs past it.

### edit-diffs 17. Modern loanwords. Where the model keeps the English word, speakers respell it into Igala orthography (economics -> ẹkẹnọmiks / ẹkọñọmiks, password -> paswọdú, university -> yuñivasítí, computer science -> komputa chienci, record -> recordi, bokiti -> bokẹti, Baturi -> Batiri, driver -> dírává) or replace it with an Igala word (allura -> ogwu, iticha -> akukọ / ẹnẹ ukọche / ak'onẹ ẹñwu, yunifasiti -> chegbulu Atẹ, opereshọn -> ukọlọ, English -> Enẹfu (4 annotators), East -> apa baki [sun] [morning], password -> igede). One speaker went the other way ([money]-erí -> ágriki). Speakers disagree on the same word: on gram_005 annotator_4 wrote 'komputa chienci' while annotators 6 and 7 kept 'computer science'. LOAN_NATIVIZE 17 hunks any-tag, 15 edits, 11 prompts; LOAN_TO_IGALA 11 hunks, 11 edits, 10 prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: A borrowed common noun is written in Igala letters (no s: ch; dotted vowels; final vowel), e.g. paswọdú, bokẹti, batiri, yunivasiti, dọkita; a proper name is not respelled. Teacher, medicine, work/operation, school/university and English have Igala words (ẹnẹ ukọchẹ or akukọ, ogwu, ukọlọ, ichegbulu, Enẹfu). Whether to keep the English form at all is a live disagreement among speakers.
- Count: 2 judgments, 24 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: No, and v4.4 points the other way: rule 10 says 'keep the English word in your sentence' and rule 4 'keep the everyday loanword the community uses'. The speakers' edits show the community form is a respelled loan or an Igala word, not the English spelling.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_lex_004 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `economics` -> speaker `ẹkẹnọmiks`. "Ecnomics is English not igala"
  - ig_v42_lex_008 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `password` -> speaker `paswọdú`. "Paswọdú is igala meaning your password"
  - ig_v42_lex_008 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `password` -> speaker `igede`. "Because it was not exactly correct"
  - ig_v42_gram_002 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ch'ítíchà` -> speaker `chi akukọ`. "Akukọ is the real meaning of teacher in Igala"
  - ig_v42_gram_002 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `akọchẹ` -> speaker `ẹnẹ ukọche`. "Ẹnẹ ukọchẹ not iticha"
  - ig_v42_gram_005 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `computer science ugbo` -> speaker `komputa chienci gwi [house] ukochẹ utọgb`. "Vivian studied computer science at Kogi State University and now works in Abuja"
  - ig_v42_orth_005 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `English U á kà emimi kpai ogo kpai Ígáláà ǹ` -> speaker `Enẹfu`. "English in igala is enẹfu"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Spoken-register prompts with two or three modern nouns (battery, password, bucket, torch, mattress, economics, vaccine) and the instruction to write each borrowed word the way an Igala speaker writes it. Measurable (English-like token count) and the gold records the nativized spellings.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell your aunt in Igala to charge her phone battery and never to give anyone her password. Write each borrowed word the way an Igala speaker writes it.
  - Say in Igala that your niece studies economics at the university in Anyigba.
  - Read the school list to your husband in Igala: a bucket, a torch, a mattress and a padlock.

### edit-diffs 18. Meta-commentary and the English layer. The model answers 'say whether you would ...' with Igala sentences about what it is doing ('U é-kà ki u dabi ẹñwu du ǹ', 'kwotejugede efu utokown', 'Ma kwotejugede ...') and speakers delete them wholesale or reduce them to the bare answer ('alu enẹfu a dabi kọ', 'Alu ku ma', 'Enẹfu'). 29 hunks any-tag, 18 edits, but only 10 prompts, 7 of them the orth_ 'say whether' series (orth_001, 002, 005, 006, 007, 008) which accounts for 21 of 27 primary META hunks. In lex_027 the speaker edited the model's English note instead ('retained' -> 'handled the subject name by adapting ... into Igala orthography').

- Rule a speaker would state: Answer only; no sentence about the answer. For the bank: do not ask 'say whether you would change the spelling'; ask for the sentence that contains the name.
- Count: 2 judgments, 18 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes ('Give the answer only. No preamble, no meta-commentary'); 0 'U kà' frames in v4.4 output. Fixed, and the prompt shape that produced it should be retired.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_orth_001 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[name] [goat] lẹ kwotejugede ... efu utokown Ígáláà U é-kà ki u dabi ẹñwu du ǹ` -> speaker `(deleted)`. "We just write it as Lagos"
  - ig_v42_orth_008 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `chi state méèjì Ma kwotejugede efu utokown Ígáláà U é-kà kì u` -> speaker `Alu ku ma`. "We write it as Bayelsa and Osun we dont change the name name of a state when writing them on Igala Wikipedia"
  - ig_v42_orth_005 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `kpai English U á kà emimi kpai ogo kpai Ígáláà ǹ` -> speaker `alu enefu`. "Made a perfect words correction"
  - ig_v42_orth_006 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `ọ U n̄ kọ ẹkpa [name] ñwu n̄` -> speaker `(deleted)`. "I deleted words that are not neccesary"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: None; this is a prompt-shape artifact. Replace the 'say whether' prompts with plain sentences carrying the name.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Write one Igala sentence saying that Grace Egbuson lives in Lagos and works in Bayelsa.
  - Write one Igala sentence saying that the novel Things Fall Apart was written by Chinua Achebe.

### edit-diffs 19. Proper names. Idah written without h by the model (Ida, Ìdá, Ídá) and restored with h by four annotators (Idah, Ìdáh, Ídáh); Añkpa -> Ankpa (annotators 3 and 6); [father] -> [father] (annotator_6, the title spelling); the institution class word inserted before the name (annotator_4 'Ichegbulu ... baki [house] Ukoche'). Two speakers dropped the foreign title or name altogether (annotator_4 deleted 'Things Fall Apart' and 'School'; annotator_6 deleted 'Green Valley International School'). 13 hunks any-tag, 10 edits, 6 prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: Names keep their letters (Idah with h, Ankpa with plain n, [father]). An institution may be led by its Igala class word (ichegbulu, banki) with the rest copied. Dropping the name is a speaker shortcut, not a target.
- Count: 0 judgments, 10 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes (rule 9), and the regression holds: 0 Ida-without-h, 14 Idah in v4.4 output. Keep two regression prompts only.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_orth_002 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ida kpai Añkpa` -> speaker `Idah Kpai Anpka`. "Idah and Ankpa"
  - ig_v42_orth_002 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Ìdá` -> speaker `Ìdáh`. "I just spelt it correctly"
  - ig_v42_gram_004 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[father]` -> speaker `[father]`. "Output is almost perfect"
  - ig_v42_orth_007 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Things Fall Apart` -> speaker `(deleted)`. "I just wrote the name of the book in Igala"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One sentence with two or three names (a town, a title, a school) in a plain statement, no meta question.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Write one Igala sentence saying that the [father] of Igala lives in Idah and his son studies at Kogi State University in Anyigba.
  - Say in Igala that your daughter attends Green Spring Montessori in Ankpa.

### edit-diffs 20. Determiner lẹ vs yí and clause linkers. lẹ where 'this' is meant (gram_025 [name] lẹ' -> 'yí' this road; auth_003 lẹ -> yi), yí where 'the' is meant (lex_026 yí -> lẹ), 'this afternoon' oroka yi, 'this one' ọ̀mọ yí; 10 hunks, 10 edits, 10 prompts. Linkers: oñ 'and' rewritten ya by three annotators on gram_005 and tákí by annotator_5 on gram_011; tọdu and the question word chi clash (auth_027); kakini / kini / kà kíni unsettled (annotator_3 kakini -> kini; annotator_6 kà kíni -> kakini; annotator_5 kakini -> todu). 12 hunks, 11 edits, 8 prompts. Yes-no questions closed with ba / bá / á / a by speakers (gram_028 n -> ba; gram_027 -> bá; reg_007 -> á; lex_006 -> a): 5 hunks, 5 prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: lẹ = the (after the noun, closes relative clauses), yí = this; 'now / then' sequencing is ya, not oñ; tọdu = because and is not combined with the question word chi; yes-no questions close with a final particle (ba/bá or a/á) rather than a nasal.
- Count: 0 judgments, 24 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: lẹ = the and 'never yí for the' are in the prompt; 'yí = this' is only in the open-questions grammar row. oñ is banned (0 hits) but no replacement is given, so the model has no 'and then'. The question particle is listed as unsettled in the grammar rows and absent from the prompt.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_025 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `lẹ` -> speaker `yí`. "Wrong words and spellings"
  - ig_v42_lex_026 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `yí` -> speaker `lẹ`. "I correted the sentence"
  - ig_v42_gram_005 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `oñ i` -> speaker `ya`. "I wrote the correct sentence (annotator_4 and annotator_6 made the same oñ -> ya change on this prompt)"
  - ig_bank_auth_027 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `tọdu ... (no chi)` -> speaker `(tọdu deleted) ... chi`. "We can’t use todu and chi together because they both ask questions."
  - ig_v42_gram_028 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `kí n ... n` -> speaker `ku ... ba`. "I did spelling correction"
  - ig_v42_gram_027 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `aláfíya ma wà` -> speaker `chọ’lafía bá`. "I corrected the words and spelling"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: this/that/the triplets in one answer (the sandals prompt worked); two-event narratives with 'then / now'; 'because' and 'if' sentences; 'ask him whether' prompts with three yes-no questions so the particle appears three times.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - A child asks where his sandals are. Say in Igala: this one is here, that one is over there, the other one is lost.
  - Ask a man in Igala whether this road goes to Ankpa and whether the road on the left goes to Idah.
  - Say in Igala: she married in 2015, then moved to Lokoja, and now she sells cloth.
  - Explain in Igala why you did not come yesterday: because it rained; and say that if it rains tomorrow you will not go to the farm.
  - Ask your brother in Igala whether he has eaten, whether he washed his hands, and whether the children are asleep.

### edit-diffs 21. Idle words and missing words. Speakers delete tokens the model added for nothing (úgbo 'Just deleted the extra word'; uloji; kpa 'all' twice; tó, Tí, a; three redundant wẹ; whole padding sentences) and insert words the model omitted: 'ago' (gram_012: annotator_7 inserted la + 'gudu i', annotator_5 inserted la + 'gwudu yi' for the same gap), 'under' (ọfẹ, gram_035), 'still' (to, gram_027), 'how long ago' (otítálá), the copula chi (lex_014, cult_037), the particle ya (auth_016, gram_037), vocatives (Amíye mi, Yaya mi, [mother] [child]). DEL_PAD 35 hunks any-tag, 30 edits, 26 prompts; INS_WORD 33 hunks, 25 edits, 22 prompts. v4.4 answers run 21.8 words vs 17.75 in gold.

- Rule a speaker would state: Time and place adverbials have fixed Igala words that the model drops: 'ago' = ... la gwudu/gudu yi; 'under' = ọfẹ/ofe; 'still' = to; 'next week' = aladi ki ya wa. Every small word must earn its place (kpa 'all' and úgbo are the usual idle ones).
- Count: 0 judgments, 55 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: The idle-word rule is in the prompt ('Every small word must have a job'); the missing adverbials (ago, under, still) are not, and v4.4 answers are still 23% longer than gold.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_012 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `t'Idah che odo eta` -> speaker `ti idah chi ọdọ mẹta ... la ... gwudu yi`. "Ki le is not a complete sentence"
  - ig_v42_gram_012 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `che odo eta` -> speaker `chi ọdọ meta ... la ... gudu i`. "I fixed spelling error and added some words"
  - ig_v42_gram_015 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `úgbo` -> speaker `(deleted)`. "Just deleted the extra word"
  - ig_v42_gram_041 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `lo t'[house] kpa` -> speaker `le t’[house]`. "Wrong tone marks"
  - ig_v42_gram_027 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `(absent)` -> speaker `to`. "I added words to complete the sentense"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Sentences anchored on a time or place adverbial (three years ago, next week, still, already, under, beside) so the omission is a detectable gap and the insertion teaches the word.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Say in Igala that you went to Idah three years ago, that you will go again next week, and that your brother has not yet gone.
  - Tell the boys in Igala to put the yams under the tree, not beside the house.
  - Say in Igala that the children are still playing and the food is already cold.

**Shapes that produced decisive, explained judgments**

- One-sentence translation of a plain English sentence with one named grammatical trap (destination, 'in' a town, 'and now', reported speech), judged and edited by 3 to 5 annotators.: Convergence is visible token by token: on gram_006 five annotators replaced 'àwéhì tì' with lo + ti; on gram_005 four annotators fixed efu Abuja -> efẹwọ and oñ -> ya; on lex_009 three annotators produced the na tẹnẹ ... ka ki ni frame and aladi. Edit ratios stay high (0.74 to 0.93) so hunks are small and attributable. (ig_v42_gram_006, ig_v42_gram_005, ig_v42_lex_009, ig_v42_auth_002, ig_v42_gram_001)
- Minimal-pair prompts where one wrong token flips the meaning (person, number, kill vs finish, 14th vs 16th).: They are the only prompts whose rationales state a rule: 'Í dẹ he was there Not U dẹ meaning i was there' (gram_013), 'Ta’kpa means finish in Igala not kpa Kpa means kill' (lex_006), '14th is ẹgwẹlẹ not ẹgwẹfa' (gram_014), 'wa is more than one person and wẹ s use for one person' (auth_001). Annotator_5 wrote 29 such 'X not Y / X means Y' rationales; annotator_7 wrote 43 generic 'I corrected the spelling' ones, so the prompt must do the work of naming the contrast. (ig_v42_gram_013, ig_v42_lex_006, ig_v42_gram_014, ig_bank_auth_001, ig_bank_auth_008)
- Closed lists: counting runs, specific numbers in a sentence, dates, ages.: Two annotators corrected the same forms nearly identically (18/19 without mẹ-, 85 as ọgbọmẹlẹ ñyọ mẹlu, 200 as Ọ̀gwọ́kọ́) and their disagreements (40, 100, tone marks) are one token each, so both the rule and the open question come out of one prompt. (ig_v42_gram_020, ig_v42_gram_021, ig_v42_orth_011, ig_v42_orth_012, ig_v42_gram_014, ig_v42_gram_012)
- Imperatives where the scene fixes the object count and the handling verb (carry the yams and put them under the tree; give the children the medicine).: gram_035 produced the only direct evidence for the write-up's verb-object number agreement (du t'ane -> jọ ofe) and lex_009 the di 'give' correction; the diff is exactly the verb. (ig_v42_gram_035, ig_v42_lex_009, ig_v42_gram_034, ig_v42_gram_026)
- 'Ask him whether ...' and 'this one / that one' prompts.: They elicited the yes-no particle (ba/bá/á) and the yí vs lẹ contrast that the model gets backwards, both absent or unsettled in v4.4; each edit is a one-token diff. (ig_v42_gram_028, ig_v42_gram_027, ig_v42_gram_025, ig_v42_lex_017, ig_v42_gram_030)
- Spoken-register prompts with two or three modern nouns (battery, password, bucket, torch, economics).: They surface the loan-handling disagreement (English kept vs paswọdú / ẹkẹnọmiks / komputa chienci vs Igala igede / ogwu) that v4.4 rule 10 currently decides against the speakers, and each loan is one token. (ig_v42_lex_008, ig_v42_lex_006, ig_v42_lex_004, ig_v42_lex_014, ig_v42_gram_005)

**Shapes that produced ties and "both inadequate"**

- 'Write the name as it should appear and say whether you would change / leave it in English' (the orth_ series).: The model answers with meta-sentences about itself and the speakers delete them: 21 of 27 primary META hunks and 7 of 10 META prompts come from six such prompts. The lesson is one bit ('We just write it as Lagos'), already encoded in v4.4 rule 9 and confirmed by 0 regressions. Two speakers also dropped the name entirely, so even the name lesson is noisy. (ig_v42_orth_001, ig_v42_orth_002, ig_v42_orth_005, ig_v42_orth_006, ig_v42_orth_007, ig_v42_orth_008)
- Open-ended register pieces ('a formal request to a council of elders', 'a blessing as a grandmother might say it', 'thank God on hearing good news').: Speakers replace the whole text instead of correcting it: 17 of 213 edits have similarity 0.2 or lower and 6 are at 0.0 (reg_033, auth_005, auth_012, auth_017 among them). A single rewrite hunk cannot be classified or counted, and the rationale ('i Made the corrections so the sentence can sound appriopraite') carries nothing. Gold is the right instrument for these, not edits. (ig_bank_reg_033, ig_bank_auth_005, ig_bank_auth_012, ig_bank_auth_017, ig_bank_dial_013)
- Prompts asking for two versions or for an English explanation alongside the Igala (code-switched plus fully-Igala; 'explain in English the image').: dial_013 collapsed to a single word ('Elu'eju') replacing both versions; in lex_027 the speaker spent three hunks editing the model's English note ('retained' -> 'handled the subject name by adapting ... into Igala orthography') and taught nothing about Igala. The English layer absorbs the edit. (ig_bank_dial_013, ig_v42_lex_027, ig_v42_lex_025, ig_v42_idiom_001)
- Prompts whose answer needs several unrelated lexical items at once (health insurance on the radio; land and inheritance; age-grades).: They produce 7 to 10 hunks per edit, mostly WORD_SUBST and INS/DEL with rationales like 'I wrote the sentence well', so no single rule is isolated. WORD_SUBST is the largest primary category (72 hunks) precisely because of these. (ig_v42_lex_012, ig_bank_cult_037, ig_bank_cult_038, ig_v42_reg_014)

**What the speakers' own writing shows**

- tone marks: Speakers REMOVE tone marks by a wide margin: 35 strip hunks (29 edits, 25 prompts) vs 11 add hunks (8 edits, 7 prompts), and 8 of the 11 additions are numerals in prompts that asked for full tone marks or counting. Per annotator (strips / adds): annotator_6 16 / 0, annotator_4 8 / 0, annotator_3 3 / 0 ('Just a normal n without tone marks', 'The tone mark was not required'), annotator_7 6 / 4, annotator_5 2 / 7 (adds on numbers and on the Í dẹ person contrast). Gold toned-word share: annotator_6 0.00, annotator_4 0.00, annotator_5 0.05, annotator_7 0.05, annotator_3 0.25, annotator_8 0.39. The write-up's 'tone marks always' describes annotators 3 and 8, not the five who edit. On the same full-tone prompt (orth_011) annotator_7 toned and annotator_5 did not.
- contractions: Mixed and purposeful. Speakers CONTRACT common verb-plus-object idioms (28 hunks any-tag: kọ’la, Gbọ’la, gw’[hand], l’ẹñwu, dọ’tákàdá, dọ́wọ́, k'eli, Am'[child], k'ùbí, di’wọ, ta’kpa) and EXPAND the model's wrong contractions (18 hunks: k'u á -> u neke, ch'ọ́w'àwọ́tọ -> chí [hand] awọtọ, nw'ẹ -> ñwẹ, k'ma -> ku ma, amon' -> abo, k'ọ́la -> ukọla, ef'ádṣa -> efu [market]). Per annotator (contract / expand): annotator_5 12 / 8, annotator_6 6 / 5, annotator_3 5 / 1, annotator_7 3 / 4, annotator_4 2 / 0. Gold apostrophes per word: annotator_6 0.135, annotator_3 0.087, annotator_4 0.079, annotator_7 0.068, annotator_5 0.044, annotator_8 0.04. Nobody writes fully expanded text as the write-up asks; the model (v4.4: 1.31 apostrophes per answer vs 0.75 gold) over-contracts.
- ki usage: Speakers change ki -> ku before plural ma (11 hunks: annotator_5 5, annotator_7 4, annotator_3 1, annotator_6 1), ki/k'ẹ -> kẹ between chained verbs (5 hunks: annotator_5 4, annotator_3 1), INSERT ki as a clause opener in announcement frames 'ka ki ni / ka ki de / do ki' (4 hunks, one each from annotators 3, 5, 6, 7), and DELETE idle kí/k' (3 hunks, annotators 5 and 3). They never retone kí to kì or kì to kí; when the model's kí carries a tone they strip it (annotator_6 'nyọ́nyọ́ kí' -> 'nyọnyọ ki'; annotator_4 'kí ch'ẹ́ñwu' -> 'ki ch'ẹñyo'). Net: ki count goes slightly down in edits (v4.4 0.98 standalone ki per answer vs 0.76 in gold), the write-up's over-use diagnosis holds, but the fix speakers apply is number agreement (ku) and verb chaining (kẹ), not the COMP/REL tone contrast.
- dialect: All five editing annotators write the Central/Idah l-forms (onobulẹ, onokẹlẹ, la, lọ, chiya) and reject every r-form; gold self-declarations: annotator_7 general_idah 118, annotator_6 general_idah 64, annotator_5 general_idah 17, annotator_4 general_idah 9 plus one ibaji, annotator_3 'other' 43, annotator_8 general_idah 117 plus ankpa 45 (annotator_8 does not appear in the edits). Variety shows in small choices: copula chi (annotators 3, 5, 7) vs che (annotator_6); negation ñ (annotators 5, 7) vs hyphenated -n (annotator_6: du-n, Igala-n, ojoji-n); 'outside' t'òda (annotator_5), t'[name] (annotator_7), nya ti (annotator_3); 'woman' Ónobulẹ (3, 6, 7) vs Ónobilẹ once (5); 'in a town' efẹwọ (4, 5, 7) vs efewo (3, 6) vs efu kept (6 on gram_005). Annotator_5 kept the lafia shape ('chọ’lafía bá') that v4.4 bans.
- other: Rationale quality differs sharply: annotator_5 states rules ('X not Y', 'X means Y') in 29 of 65 edits; annotator_7 writes generic 'I corrected the spelling' in 43 of 71; annotator_6 writes 'not well constructed' or nothing (9 of 28 empty). Speakers add vocatives the model omits ([mother] [child], Amíye mi, Yaya mi, Agba abọ ọgijọ, ọ́yà) and the hail Agba oo; they use full pronouns only for emphasis (úwẹ, Uwẹ, awa, ama). They nativize loans into Igala letters (paswọdú, bokẹti, batiri, yuñivasítí, ẹkẹnọmiks) and prefer an Igala word where one exists (akukọ, ẹnẹ ukọchẹ, ogwu, ukọlọ, Enẹfu, ichegbulu). They write Idah with h and Ankpa with plain n. Their answers are shorter than the model's (gold 17.75 words vs v4.4 21.8) and they delete 'all' (kpa) and similar fillers.

**Open questions (speakers disagree)**

- Ígáláà: v4.4 emits it in 46 of 249 answers and no speaker accepts it; is the fix a prompt line (write Igala / Ígálá) or a retrieval filter, since all 22 hunks are on the retrieval arm?
- Tone policy: the write-up says always mark tone, the five editing annotators strip tone and v4.4 tells the model not to mark it. Which is the target for gold, and should annotators 3 and 8 (who tone 25% to 39% of gold words) be the reference or the exception?
- Contraction policy: the write-up wants expanded forms, the speakers contract idioms and expand only wrong contractions. Should the bank ask for both forms side by side (the furigana idea as two lines) rather than choose?
- Negation spelling: ñ (annotators 5, 7, and v4.4) vs hyphenated -n (annotator_6 in edits and gold). Is -n a variety marker or a personal convention?
- Copula: chi (annotators 3, 5, 7) vs che (annotator_6) vs the model's chẹ; is this dialectal, and should v4.4 stop offering chẹ?
- Numerals: 40 is Ọ̀gbọ̀méji (annotator_7) or Ọgbọ (annotator_5)? 100 is Ógwúmẹ́lu (annotator_5) or Ọgbọ mẹ́lu (annotator_7 kept it)? The grammar row says ọgbọ meji = 40; a ruling is needed before these go into the GRAMMAR block.
- Loanwords: v4.4 rule 10 says keep the English word; speakers respell (paswọdú, ẹkẹnọmiks) or replace (igede, ogwu, akukọ). Which does the project want as gold, and does the answer differ between spoken-register and Wikipedia-register prompts?
- The á incompletive: v4.4 says standalone á; annotators 5 and 6 fuse it (afu, abi, akọ, a’mẹ́rú). Has anyone checked whether v4.4's standalone á gets accepted in blind judgment?
- efu before a town: the rule is in v4.4 yet 9 answers still write efu + town and 5 write ef' before a consonant. Is the repair round catching it, or is retrieval overriding the prompt?
- kí (COMP) vs kì (REL): the write-up's central syntax point never appears in an edit; speakers write toneless ki in both slots. Should the bank test it at all, or test the number agreement (ki/ku) and verb chaining (kẹ) that speakers actually enforce?
- 'Outside' has three speaker forms (t'òda, t'[name], nya ti) and 'left for' five spellings (loti, lotí, lọti, lotì, lo ti, leti): are these variety differences or free spelling, and should prompts ask speakers to name their variety every time?
- annotators 1, 2 and 8 have no edits in this file: is that because they only judge and write gold, and should the next bank route edits to them to widen the dialect sample (annotator_8 declares ankpa in 45 gold answers)?

## The unjudged v4.4 answers against the gold on the same prompts (249)

Data read: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-halim-Documents-wikitongues--claude-worktrees-fervent-jemison-b6bb05/4e7b27ad-9966-41d9-a2d6-dec139f0636b/scratchpad/review-split/v44-vs-gold.json (249 rows, read in full as a text dump, every row, plus mechanical counts); scratchpad/prompt-v4-4.txt (served system prompt, read in full); scratchpad/grammar-rows-oct8.json (30 rows, topics and content read); scratchpad/writeup-oct8.txt (Salem and Lydia, read in full); review-split/gold-*.json (8 files, 636 gold answers on slice prompts with englishGloss and dialect, used for convergence, glosses and per-annotator norms); review-split/comparisons-*.json (637 bare-vs-v3 judgments on 178 slice prompts: winners, failure tags, explanations, both arms' texts); review-split/edits-diffs.json (213 edits: original, corrected, hunks; token-level removed/inserted mining); review-split/prompt-informativeness.json (178 rows, decisive/tie/both_inadequate per prompt, grouped by prompt shape); review-split/bank-index.json (589 prompts, used to avoid duplicating existing prompts). Analysis scripts and outputs are in the scratchpad: analyze_v44.py, analyze2.py to analyze6.py, analysis-v44.txt, analysis2.txt to analysis5.txt, v44-gold-dump.txt.

### v44-vs-gold 1. Tone marks sprinkled on a handful of grammar-block particles and on the word Igala, in otherwise unmarked text. 172 of 249 v4.4 answers carry at least one mark but the median toned share is 0.09; five tokens account for most marks: Ígáláà 89 times in 46 rows, kì 75, kà 32, á 25, yí 25. These are exactly the forms the served GRAMMAR block writes with accents (kì, á, yí, kó, kẹ), so the model copies the block's spelling into prose that the REGISTER line says should be unmarked. Speakers never write Ígáláà (0 of 636 gold; Igala 96 times); in edits 'Ígáláà' was removed 22 times and never inserted, 'Igala' inserted 12 times. Speaker marking is bimodal, not partial: annotators 4, 5, 6, 7 mark 0 to 16% of answers; annotator_8 marks 89% of answers and annotator_3 32%, and when they mark they mark most words (share 0.5 to 1.0). When a prompt asks for full marks v4.4 still marks selectively and sometimes wrongly.

- Rule a speaker would state: An answer is either fully unmarked (community default) or marked on every syllable when the prompt asks. Never mark only particles. Write Igala, never Ígáláà. The GRAMMAR block itself should be served without accents on kì, á, yí, kó, kẹ if REGISTER wants unmarked output, or the model will keep copying them.
- Count: 119 judgments, 22 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes and contradicted: REGISTER says 'no tone marks unless the question asks for them', but CLOSED-CLASS GRAMMAR writes kì, á, yí, kó, kẹ with accents and the model copies those spellings. Salem's write-up asks for marks to be always present and consistent, which is the opposite default; neither is what v4.4 does.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_cult_006 [annotator_7] (v4.4): model `[father] Ígáláà chi [king] ami [king] kpai [king] abọ Ígáláà chakadu. I chi agbọji ọjanẹ kì ya di abọ Ígáláà chakadu dama.` -> speaker `[father] Igala chi [king] Ogwuchẹkpọ kpai abọji abọ Igala chakadu`. "annotator_7 gold, no marks anywhere; v4.4 marks only Ígáláà and kì"
  - ig_bank_cult_036 [annotator_7] (v4.4): model `[father] Igala chi abọji amọnẹ chakadu ọjanẹ Ígáláà` -> speaker `[father] Igala: chi agbọji abọ Igala chakadu`. "v4.4 writes Igala and Ígáláà in the same answer; annotator_7 writes Igala throughout"
  - ig_bank_auth_009 [annotator_6] (v4.4): model `[child] mi, [god] kì lo kpai uwẹ. [god] kì ch'[hand] wẹ. [god] kì fu wẹ de gw'ef'ẹñwu ẹbiẹnẹ. [god] kì d'ẹnyọ ñwu wẹ.` -> speaker `[frozen answer]`. "annotator_6 and annotator_3 ([frozen answer]) both unmarked, one sentence; v4.4 marks kì four times and [child] once"
  - ig_v42_orth_011 [annotator_8] (v4.4): model `Ógwú (twenty) / Ógwú ẹ́gwá (thirty) / Ọ́gbọmẹjì (forty)` -> speaker `ógwù , ógwù-égwa, ọgbómẹjì`. "prompt asked for full tone marks; annotator_8 marks every syllable with low tone on ógwù, v4.4 marks high and leaves gbọ unmarked"
  - ig_v42_auth_015 [annotator_8] (v4.4): model `Ichi Igala chi ichi ki ami onẹ Igala a kọ.` -> speaker `Igala chì ìchì kì amì ìgáláà ā ka ì chẹ`. "the only gold with a marked Igala is annotator_8's lower-case ìgáláà inside a fully marked answer; v4.4's capitalised Ígáláà in unmarked prose appears in 46 rows"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Paired prompts on the same sentence: one says 'write it without tone marks, the way you would text it', the other 'mark the tone on every word'. Add a third shape where the word Igala must appear in the answer (the Igala language, the Igala people, Igala land). The pair makes partial marking visible as a failure on both sides, and the speaker's fully marked version is the teaching signal.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - In one Igala sentence, tell a visitor that the Igala people live on both sides of the River Niger. Write it without any tone marks, the way you would type it in a WhatsApp message.
  - Now write the same sentence again with tone marks on every word, as a schoolbook would print it.
  - Write the Igala name of the Igala language and the Igala name of an Igala person, first as you would type them, then with full tone marks.
  - Your niece asks you to check her homework. The sentence is 'The children are not at home.' Write it in Igala twice: once with no tone marks, once with every tone marked.
  - A proofreader says a line in an Igala article is half-marked: some words have accents and most do not. Write the line 'The elder is sitting under the tree' in Igala the way the proofreader wants it, and say whether you chose marks everywhere or nowhere.

### v44-vs-gold 2. Negation written as a standalone ñ after an inserted incompletive á, and ñ reused as a question particle. v4.4 writes the negator only as a separate 'ñ' (32) or 'n' (7), never hyphenated, apostrophised or as nọ. Speakers write it six ways: attached to the verb (31), standalone ñ (26), hyphen -n/-ñ (24, annotators 6 and 8), apostrophe 'ñ (20, annotators 4, 5, 7), standalone n (15), and the syllable nọ/no/nóò (20, annotators 4, 7, 8). On 'They are not eating' all seven speakers write the bare verb (Ma jẹñwu + nasal); v4.4 adds á and contracts ('Ma á j'ẹñwu ñ'). In 'Abọ [house] wẹ ń?' v4.4 uses the nasal as a yes/no question particle, which its own grammar forbids; speakers use a, ha, ba, fá, mẹ.

- Rule a speaker would state: Negative present or habitual takes the bare verb plus the nasal; do not add á. The nasal attaches to the verb (ma jẹñwu-n, jẹñwu'ñ) or stands as nọ; a question is closed by a, ba, ha or fá, never by ñ. Which written shape of the nasal to standardise is an open question for the linguists, but 'verb + á + separate ñ' is nobody's.
- Count: 21 judgments, 20 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly, and the served wording is the source of the standalone spelling: 'Negation: ONLY a clause-final nasal, written ñ'. The speakers' attached, hyphenated and nọ spellings are not mentioned. The ban on using the nasal for anything else is told and the model still used it as a question particle once.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_gram_019 [annotator_6] (v4.4): model `Ma á j'ẹñwu ñ.` -> speaker `Ma jẹñwu-n (annotator_6), Ma jẹñwu’ñ (annotator_5), Mà jẹñwu ǹ (annotator_3), Ma jẹnwu nọ (annotator_7), Mà jẹnwu nóò (annotator_8), Ma jẹ'ñwu no (annotator_4)`. "seven gold answers, none with á, none with the verb contracted to j'ẹñwu"
  - ig_bank_gram_026 [annotator_5] (v4.4): model `Ẹ kì lo t'ọmọ ñ.` -> speaker `Ki lo t'ọmọ n (annotator_6), Ẹ ki loti ọmọñ (annotator_5), Ẹ lò tọmọ ń (annotator_3)`. "three speakers converge on 'Ẹ ki ...'; the nasal is attached (ọmọñ) or bare n, never marked ñ as a separate word by all"
  - ig_bank_auth_011 [annotator_7] (v4.4): model `Abọ [house] wẹ ń?` -> speaker `Abọ akwora [house] wẹ a? (annotator_7), Abọ akwọra [house] ha (annotator_7), Abo akwọra [house] wẹ ch'ọlafiya ba? (annotator_6), Abọ [house] wẹ fá (annotator_8)`. "annotator_5 judging bare and v3 on this prompt: 'Aba - wrong Agba- right'; no speaker closes a question with the nasal"
  - ig_v42_gram_029 [annotator_8] (v4.4): model `Ẹñwu ki che ki wẹ a j'ẹfẹ kpai ma ñ?` -> speaker `[child] kẹkẹ en̄wu a'chẹ ku wà ma chìya kpì aḿo'nuku wá n?`. "annotator_8 writes the negator as a bare n at the end; annotator_5 on the v3 pair: 'Play in igala is íya not ch’ẹyẹ'"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Negation minimal pairs in a fixed scene: the same clause as a statement, a negative, and a yes/no question (three lines), across persons (I, you, she, we, they) and tenses. Ask the speaker to write it as spoken and to say where the nasal attaches. Convergent gold on gram_019 and gram_026 shows speakers agree on the verb and disagree only on the spelling of the nasal, which is exactly the datum the linguists need.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your mother asks whether the children have eaten. Write three Igala lines as you would say them: 'They have eaten', 'They have not eaten', and the question 'Have they eaten?'
  - A neighbour asks about your father. Write in Igala: 'He is at the farm', 'He is not at the farm', and 'Is he at the farm?'
  - Write in Igala, as you would text a friend: 'I am not going to the market today.' Then write the same with 'we' instead of 'I'.
  - Your younger brother says he washed the plates. You did not see him do it. Write in Igala what you say to deny it: 'You did not wash the plates.'
  - Tell a child in Igala not to touch the pot, then tell a group of children the same thing. Write both commands.

### v44-vs-gold 3. The incompletive a/á written as a separate word and a 'Ma a + verb' list template. v4.4 writes 'a chi', 'a di', 'a lo', 'a du' as two words 168 times and fuses only 37 times; speakers fuse 135 times (achi, adi, awa, agwa, aluchẹ, akpẹ) and separate 30 (annotator_6 46 fused to 1 separate, annotator_8 60 to 9, annotator_7 23 to 19). The habitual opener 'Ma a ...' appears 63 times in v4.4 and 0 times in 636 gold answers; it drives the six-line cultural answers.

- Rule a speaker would state: Write the incompletive fused to the verb as the community does (achi, adi, alo, awa, agwa) unless the subject pronoun itself is a (we), in which case keep one a. Do not open consecutive sentences with 'Ma a'. If the served rule 'the incompletive is the standalone word á' stays, expect speakers to keep writing it fused and to read the separate a as 'we'.
- Count: 0 judgments, 18 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes, and the prompt teaches the divergent form: 'The incompletive is the standalone word á; a noun keeps its own first vowel inside the word' and 'Igala has no hyphenated prefixes - never é-'. The anti-é- rule was needed against v3's 'é-du', 'é-gbítì' (speaker edits removed 'e' 18 times), but the fix overshot: the community writes a+verb as one word.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_gram_017 [annotator_6] (v4.4): model `Ogijo lẹ a n'ọla am'[child] lẹ.` -> speaker `Ogijo lẹ agwa abimọtọ lẹ (annotator_6), Ogijo a qwi ami imọtọ ugwa (annotator_5), Ògìjò à gwì àmì ìmọtọ (annotator_3)`. "two of three speakers fuse a to the verb (agwa) or write the verb gwa for greet; v4.4 separates a and uses n'ọla"
  - ig_bank_gram_023 [annotator_6] (v4.4): model `Àmì ataja a wa kw'ef'[market] lẹ.` -> speaker `Ami añyaja kw'[market] awa (annotator_6), Ami ateñwu awa qwefu [market] (annotator_5)`. "both speakers write awa as one word and put the source before the verb"
  - ig_bank_cult_001 [annotator_7] (v4.4): model `Ma a du obi, [water], kpai ekpo wa tọdu ku ma ch'ẹbo ñwi [child] lẹ. Ọgijọ akwọra [house] a di [name] ñwi [child] lẹ. Ma a ch'ẹbo ka ki [child] lẹ ki kpe` -> speaker `Alẹju Akpa ichọlọ [name] ẹdu nwọ ma chi [god] ẹkẹbiẹ, Imam adi [name] ñwi [child]`. "annotator_7 writes adi fused; v4.4 opens three sentences with 'Ma a'"
  - ig_bank_cult_005 [annotator_7] (v4.4): model `Ma a chi ichọlọ [name] ẹdu ñwi [child] ẹkpa lẹ [god] ẹkẹbiẹ kpai [god] ẹkẹjọ. Ami ọgijọ akwọra [house] a k'amọnẹ dama tọdu ku ma ch'ẹbọ ñwi [child] lẹ. Ma a ch'ẹbọ tọdu ki ... Akwọra [house] a ch'[food]. Amọnẹ chakadu a j'ẹñwu dama.` -> speaker `Chẹ ñwu ki ọnẹ fi [child] bi ẹtitọ alẹ'ju ọgwuchekpo ichọlọ [name] ẹdu ñwi [child] chi [god] ẹkẹgwẹlẹ ma di [name] ñwi [child] ẹtito, ma di [child] ẹtitọ lọti ọgba ẹbọ na dọ [name] ñwu`. "annotator_7 writes one comma-chained sentence with 'ma di' (bare completive); v4.4 writes six lines, four opening 'Ma a' or 'a'"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Habitual and progressive sentences with varied third-person subjects (a named person, a plural noun, 'they'), one or two sentences each, no essay. Ask the speaker to write it as they would type it and to say whether the a belongs to the verb. Convergence on gram_017 and gram_023 shows the spelling is stable per speaker.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Your sister sells pepper. In one Igala sentence, tell a visitor what she sells and where she sits in the market.
  - In one Igala sentence, say that the women of your compound fetch water from the stream every morning.
  - Your father is weeding right now. In one Igala sentence, tell the caller that he is weeding and will come when he finishes.
  - In two Igala sentences, describe what the children do after school in your compound: they eat, then they play.
  - Say in Igala that traders come from Ankpa and Idah to this market, in one sentence, as you would say it to a stranger.

### v44-vs-gold 4. Contractions the community does not write: ef' (22 in v4.4, 0 in 636 gold; efu written in full 34 times by speakers), ñw' (20 vs 0; speakers write ñwu 107, ñwi 60), k'a for ki + a (4 vs 0; speakers fuse to ka), ch' before a vowel (66 vs 23 on fewer words), t' (41 vs 13). v4.4's apostrophe rate per word is 0.074; speakers range 0.04 (annotator_8) to 0.135 (annotator_6), and their contractions are d', k', ch', t', am' before nouns, never ef' or ñw'. Salem's write-up asks for expanded forms because contraction with no tone marks makes homographs unreadable.

- Rule a speaker would state: Write efu, ñwu and ñwi in full; ki + a is written ka; contract only where the community does (d'ẹnyọ, ch'ọlafiya, t'[money], k'ọla, am'[child]) and when asked for a readable form give the expanded version. When a word is contracted the vowel dropped is the first one, as the prompt says.
- Count: 0 judgments, 5 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: The prompt teaches the divergent forms explicitly: 'to/for is ñwu before a consonant, ñw' before a vowel' and 'in is efu, clipped to ef' only before a vowel (ef'ọdọ 2021)'. Bare wrote ef' in 4 of 178 answers, v3 in 6, v4.4 in 17: the rule created the habit. The write-up's request for expanded forms is not in the prompt.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_004 [annotator_6] (v4.4): model `[father] Amina á ta [yam] ef'[market] Idah.` -> speaker `[father] Amina at'[yam] efu [market] Idah (annotator_6), [father] Amina at’[yam] efu [market] efewo Idah (annotator_3)`. "both speakers write efu [market]; both fuse a to the verb (at'[yam]); annotator_5 on a bare answer to a sibling prompt: 'IN in igala is efu'"
  - ig_bank_gram_031 [annotator_6] (v4.4): model `Íchẹñwu k'a ma kakini ogijo lẹ a wa, a ma nẹdẹ.` -> speaker `I chẹñwu ka ma kakini abo ogijo awa, a ma dago (annotator_6), Chẹ ñwu ka ma ka ki ni ọgijọ a wa a ma dẽdẹ (annotator_7), I’jeñwu kama kaki ogijo lẹ a’ñawa a’ma dede (annotator_5)`. "ki + a is 'ka' or 'kama' for every speaker; only v4.4 writes k'a"
  - ig_v42_lex_002 [annotator_8] (v4.4): model `[mother], na tẹnẹ ku kà ñw'ẹ ka ki ni ma kọ [child] mi mathematics, enẹfu, social studies, kpai agricultural science efu ìchèkpúlù eñíni.` -> speaker `Ukocha Imatimatiki, ukocha ìgìlìshì, Ùkọ̀chẹ́ amẹ̀nwú kpaí ọ̀da [house] kpaí ányí, Ùkọ̀chẹ́ [money] kpaí amẹla`. "ñw'ẹ appears in 20 v4.4 answers and in no gold answer; annotator_8 writes ñwu or nwu in full"
