  - Give one Igala sentence in which two vowels meet across a word boundary, show the contracted form and the full form, and say which one you would use in a text message and which in a school book.

### rules-to-elicit 5. s/ch and the name/loan exception. Names are now mostly kept (the v4.2 rule took: Zainab, Vivian, Idah kept in gold and edits; annotator_4 on ig_v42_orth_001: 'In Wikipedia we dont change the name of a place'). The open hole is LOANWORDS: v4.4 keeps English academic nouns inside Igala sentences (mathematics, social studies, agricultural science, biology, economics, psychology, password, secondary) and the repair round catches its s-respellings (yuñivasítí ig_v42_lex_005, yunifásítì ig_v42_lex_027, shọpu ig_v42_idiom_002). Speakers split three ways on the same words: annotator_5 respells with Igala letters (ẹkẹnọmiks, paswọdú, ẹkọñọmiks, yuñivasítí, komputa chienci), annotator_6 substitutes an Igala word (igede for password), annotators 4 and 7 keep the English (computer science, economics). Old loans keep s in gold (asibiti hospital, státì state) while the same annotator_5 wrote 'Hospital in igala is achíbítí' (s to ch) on ig_v42_lex_012. No prompt has asked speakers which it should be.

- Rule a speaker would state: Igala words have no s (ch instead); a personal or place name keeps its letters; a borrowed common noun is written the way the community writes it, which for established loans may already be an Igala spelling with ch and for new ones is the English word unchanged; never invent a half-respelling.
- Count: 7 judgments, 25 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes for names and the letter inventory: L9 'Igala has no s, but a name is not an Igala word - never respell one'; L33 allowlist 'a name or borrowed word copied from the question keeps its own letters'; L10 'keep the English word in your sentence - an omission is worse than a borrowing'. Grade A for the s/ch rule and for names (scholarship + corpus names Iso, Sara + community). Loanwords are X: community split, scholarship says keep the written s, corpus respells Bible names. Missing: a community majority per loan class. Speakers' answers add a per-word verdict (keep / respell / replace) and, if asked, the reason, which is the only way to turn row 3b5fbaa6's open question into a rule.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_lex_004 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `I ch'ukọchẹ economics.` -> speaker `I ch'ukọchẹ ẹkẹnọmiks`. "edit respells the loan with Igala letters (kẹ, ọ, no c)"
  - ig_v42_lex_008 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ẹ kì du password wẹ ñw'ẹnẹ duu ǹ.` -> speaker `Ẹ kì du igede wẹ ñw'ẹnẹ du-n`. "edit replaces the loan with the Igala word for padlock; annotator_5 on the same output wrote paswọdú, annotator_7 kept password"
  - ig_v42_gram_005 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Vivian koche computer science ugbo Kogi State University oñ i chukolo efu Abuja abajoyi` -> speaker `Vivian koche komputa chienci gwi [house] ukochẹ utọgb Kogi State University ya chukolo efẹwọ Abuja abajoyi`. "edit: the subject respelled with ch (chienci) but the university's name kept letter for letter; annotator_7's edit kept computer science in English"
  - ig_v42_lex_012 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `uny'ogwu lẹ` -> speaker `achíbítí`. "Hospital in igala is achíbítí (yet the same annotator kept asibiti in her edit of ig_v42_lex_009: [mother] [child], àbó asibiti a wá ...)"
  - ig_v42_gram_002 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Zainab ch'ítíchà.` -> speaker `Zainab chi akukọ`. "edit: the name kept, the respelled loan (teacher) replaced by an Igala word"
  - ig_v42_orth_002 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ida kpai Añkpa` -> speaker `Idah kpai Ankpa`. "It should be written as 'Idah kpai Ankpa' not Ida kpai Añkpa"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Three-way forced choice embedded in a natural sentence: a sentence that needs a modern loan (password, bank, phone charger, university course), the speaker writes it and then says in one line which of the three they did and why. Pair old loans (hospital, school, bicycle, church) with new ones (psychology, password) to find the age split. Controls: Igala-area names that need no change and an English name with s inside an Igala sentence.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell your younger sister in one Igala sentence that she must not give her phone password to anyone. Then say in one line whether you kept 'password' as English, spelled it with Igala letters, or used an Igala word, and why.
  - In one Igala sentence say that Sunday Suleiman studies economics at Kogi State University. Keep or change the spelling of each English word as you really would, and underline any word you respelled.
  - Write how an Igala speaker says 'hospital', 'school', 'church' and 'bicycle' today. For each, say whether it is an Igala word, an old borrowing written the Igala way, or an English word kept as it is.
  - A new ATM has opened in Ankpa. In two Igala sentences tell a friend where it is and what it is for. Do not avoid the word if Igala has none.
  - Write the sentence 'Mrs Esther Sule teaches science at Idah' in Igala, keeping every name as it is written here.

### rules-to-elicit 6. Ogwugwu r for Central l: no data at all. The gold dialect field has ogugu on 2 of 1,446 rows (annotator_5) and no l/r pair appears anywhere; the only speaker contact with the alternation is annotator_5 rejecting ra 'buy' as wrong ('Ẹnẹ ki a lẹ ñwu not ẹnẹ ki a rẹ ñwu', ig_bank_auth_013) and editing the model's 'ra' to 'lẹ', which is what the served NEVER WRITE line also does: a dialect-blind ban that would mark an Ogwugwu speaker wrong. Annotator_7 names Ogugu as a variety with its own 'come here' (ig_bank_dial_004: abo ọgugu a ka ki ni 'wẹ mi').

- Rule a speaker would state: Only the Ogwugwu variety replaces Central l with r (li / ri see, kpali / kpari roll, ukpakẹlẹ / ukpankẹrẹ ladle, ikelekwu / ikerenku rat); an r in those words is Ogwugwu, not Yoruba; 'buy' in Ogwugwu may be ra.
- Count: 1 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: No. L11 says never assert which town uses a form unless the reference says so; NEVER WRITE L36 bans 'ra for buy' as Yoruba; row 003f68fc 'buy = la (never ra)'. Grade C (write-up only; the authors' own arrow /r/ to /l/ runs against their examples). Missing: corpus (the Bible is Central) and community. Speakers' answers add the first community leg: whether any annotator knows the Ogwugwu forms, whether 'ogugu' in dialects.ts is the write-up's Ogwugwu, and whether ra 'buy' is Ogwugwu or Yoruba to them.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_auth_013 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Ẹnẹ ki a ra eñwu: Ẹnẹ ádṣa, eñwu yi elia?` -> speaker `Ẹnẹ ki a lẹ eñwu: Ẹnẹ ádṣa, eñwu yi elia?`. "Ẹnẹ ki a lẹ ñwu not ẹnẹ ki a rẹ ñwu"
  - ig_bank_dial_004 [annotator_7] (gold): model `Wa mímí = come here (Idah area) / Wa efí = come here (Ankpa area)` -> speaker `Abọ Igala okpakpa a ka ki ni 'liya ẹmi' abo ọgugu a ka ki ni 'wẹ mi'`. "gold: the speaker names Ogugu as a variety and gives its form; the model's two area forms were judged both_inadequate by annotators 6, 7 and 8"
  - ig_bank_dial_014 [annotator_7] (gold): model `` -> speaker `Idah - Omaye / Ankpa, Ogugu -Omada / Okwọ, omẹnyi`. "gold: three area forms for 'sibling', Ogugu grouped with Ankpa"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Ask the speaker to name their own variety first, then give the Central form and any other variety's form for the write-up's four l/r pairs (see, roll, ladle, rat) and for buy; ask whether they have HEARD the r-forms and from where. The write-up's pairs may be quoted as data (cited to Ejeba and Wiernik 2026-09-25); the model is measured on whether it asserts an area it cannot source.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Which Igala do you speak (name your town or area)? Then write the word for 'to see' as you say it, and as speakers from the Ogugu area say it if you know it.
  - Give the Igala for 'rat', 'ladle' and 'to roll' as you say them. Have you heard people from any other Igala area say these words with a different consonant? Name the area.
  - Some Igala speakers say ri for 'see' where others say li. Where have you heard ri, if anywhere? Write a short sentence with the form you use.
  - Translate 'She bought a ladle at Ankpa market' into Igala as you say it, and say which area your way of saying 'buy' belongs to.
  - Is there a word in your Igala that neighbours from another Igala town pronounce with r where you pronounce l, or the other way round? Write both forms and name both areas.

### rules-to-elicit 7. One variety per answer and naming the variety. The model INVENTS area attributions and the speakers reject every one: in the dialectal_fidelity bucket the pooled arms got 12 decisive judgments, 11 ties and 33 both_inadequate out of 56, with 0 explanations containing Igala and 3 edits; retrieval loses to bare 5 to 12 there (the only bucket where it loses apart from orthography 14 to 17). Five of 356 distinct pooled outputs assert an area form when the prompt named no area. Speakers only produced variants when a prompt asked for ONE concrete word or phrase (dial_004 come here, dial_014 sibling, dial_017 regional pair), and then they named the areas themselves (Idah, Ankpa, Ogugu, Ibaji). The gold dialect field is unset on 688 of 1,446 rows; general_idah 478, ankpa 170 (annotator_8 only), other 107 (annotator_3 only), ogugu 2, ibaji 1: variety is confounded with annotator, so 'the model mixes dialects' cannot be tested against gold until each gold names its variety.

- Rule a speaker would state: Write one variety per answer, Central (Idah) unless the question names another; never attribute a form to an area without a source; when asked, name the variety you write in.
- Count: 56 judgments, 3 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Only L11 ('Never assert which town or area uses a form unless your reference material says so'). No one-variety line, no default variety, no instruction to name the variety. Grade C (write-up only; the gold itself mixes general_idah and ankpa by annotator). Missing: community (variety-tagged gold on the same sentence from speakers of different areas) and corpus (Bible is one variety). Speakers' answers add: variety-labelled forms for the same sentence, which is the only way a dialect-mixing detector can be built, and a test of whether 'Central' means Idah to them.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_dial_005 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Amí Ídá à dọ́ kí 'Ọla àgbo?'. / Amí Ankpa à dọ́ kí 'Àgbo?'. / Amí Dekina à dọ́ kí 'Àgbo wé?'.` -> speaker `Uchẹ ka aka kanini 'abẹ le' ama IBAJI aka ka kini 'ka bu ẹle'`. "They are both incorrect (4 of 4 judgments both_inadequate; annotator_8: 'Abu wele', or 'Agbebo'; annotator_3: Ibo ka cha ka kini 'abẹle' / Ibo omunẹ chi 'aidẹ')"
  - ig_bank_dial_004 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Wá mímí. / Idah: Wá mímí. / Ankpa: Wá málí.` -> speaker `Lìya k'ego abekì lẹwa'gọ . / Liy'emì`. "Both output are not correct (annotator_6: Wa emi, lia emi; annotator_7: liya ẹmi, Ogugu wẹ mi)"
  - ig_bank_dial_007 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Amí Idah a ka kí, 'Ù dọmọ lo tí ọja.' Amí Ankpa kpaí Dekina a ka kí, 'Ù a lo tí ọja.'` -> speaker ``. "Both are inadequate (3 of 4 both_inadequate)"
  - ig_bank_dial_014 [annotator_8] (gold): model `` -> speaker `1: idah Igala: [child] -[mother] mì'. / 2: Ankpa Igala:[child]-ìya mì'`. "gold: the speaker names both areas and gives one form each (a vowel change, [mother] / ìya)"
  - ig_bank_dial_017 [annotator_8] (gold): model `` -> speaker `Agba or awa / Gwugwu ane or gwu ane / Olodudu or [frozen answer] / [sun] ukọlọ or wola ukọlọ / Oka or Enye / Nawa or Liya / Alọ or nalo`. "gold: seven regional pairs from one speaker, none with the area named: the prompt did not ask for it"
  - ig_v42_gram_003 [annotator_3] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `` -> speaker `Ma bi Onoja kpai Felix efewo Ankpa`. "explanation gives the correct sentence with the town kept and efewo before it"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Never 'note any variation' (that clause produced 33 both_inadequate and 0 Igala explanations). Instead: one concrete word or short sentence, the speaker names their own town or area in the answer and gives their form; a second type gives the Idah form of a common phrase in English description only and asks whether the speaker's area says it the same way; a third type asks for a short paragraph (3 sentences) in the speaker's variety with the variety named in the first line, so mixing inside one text becomes checkable against a labelled gold.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Name the Igala town or area you speak for, then write how you say 'sit down' to an elder and 'come here' to a child. If you know another area says either differently, write that form and name the area.
  - In three Igala sentences, tell a visitor what people in your area eat in the morning. Begin the answer with the name of your area, and keep every word in the way your area says it.
  - Translate 'My elder sister is coming from Lokoja tomorrow' into Igala as spoken in your own town, and name the town. Is there any word in your sentence that an Igala speaker from a different area would say differently? Which one?
  - Write the Igala for 'something' and for 'what?' as you say them, and name your area. Have you heard a shorter one-syllable form of either word from another Igala area? Name that area.
  - A radio station in Idah and one in Ankpa both announce that the market is closed on Friday. Write the one-sentence announcement in your own variety and say which of the two stations it would suit.
  - Give the everyday word for 'thank you' as said in your area, name the area, and say whether you have heard a different word for it from another Igala area.

### rules-to-elicit 8. Restricted subject agreement (gwùgwú / tẹ́ singular vs jọ plural for sit / be located) and the adverb èmí 'here'. The project treated gwùgwú as unattested (0 Bible hits, jwal E-94-12 not seeded), but the community uses it: 11 gold rows carry gwugwu, two annotators chose it for 'ask an elder to sit down' and annotator_8 lists 'Gwugwu ane or gwu ane' as a regional pair; one gold has the plural verb with the write-up's adverb (jọ emi, 'you all come and sit here'). èmí 'here' is in 6 gold rows from three annotators (6, 7, 8), while the model wrote mímí / efí / málí for 'here' and every speaker who saw it marked both arms inadequate. No prompt has ever asked for a singular/plural pair of sit or of 'here is / here are'; the presentative yì has no community token (gold yi is 'this').

- Rule a speaker would state: Sit / be located agrees with the subject: gwùgwú (or tẹ́ in the presentative) with one, jọ with many; 'here' is èmí; no other verb changes for subject number and none changes for person.
- Count: 4 judgments, 1 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: No prompt line. Row 42657b2c (du X tẹ / kó X jọ 'keep', external_sourced, served when retrieved) carries tẹ/jọ as second verbs; gwùgwú and èmí are nowhere (inventory W-3.1-4, W-3.1-17); rows 61bc4bc6 and 92d5f938 state the principle as scholarship_note (not served); row 4c7b83ae says never make an r-form of another verb. Evidence now: gwùgwú lifts from C to B (scholarship + community 11 rows, 4 annotators); èmí lifts to B (scholarship + community 6 rows, 3 annotators); jọ sit.PL has one community token; tẹ́ presentative and yì stay C (yi in gold is 'this'); the 'number, never person' guard is B (scholarship + corpus). Missing everywhere: corpus (0 gwugwu in 30,907 verses). Speakers' answers add singular/plural minimal pairs of sit and of 'here is / here are' from several annotators, the presentative frame they actually use (de vs tẹ́ yì), and the first check that the model does not inflect other verbs.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_reg_014 [annotator_8] (gold): model `` -> speaker `Ogìjo Agba, gwugwu anẹ`. "gold for 'Ask an elder to sit down': annotator_6 Gwugwu anẹ nago; annotator_3 Gwa’nẹ ko’cho"
  - ig_bank_dial_017 [annotator_8] (gold): model `` -> speaker `Gwugwu ane or gwu ane`. "gold lists sit-down as a regional pair (long and short form)"
  - ig_bank_reg_031 [annotator_6] (gold): model `` -> speaker `Uch'ugbede mẹ todu ku mẹ wa jeju jọ emi yi ẹñwini`. "gold: plural addressees (mẹ) with jọ 'sit.PL' and emi 'here', the write-up's (3) frame in community spelling"
  - ig_bank_dial_004 [annotator_6] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Wa mímí = come here (Idah area) / Wa efí = come here (Ankpa area)` -> speaker `Wa emi, lia emi`. "Both Output are wrong (annotator_7 gold liya ẹmi; annotator_8 gold Liy'emì; annotator_6 gold on ig_bank_reg_026 'wait here': Dago emi)"
  - ig_bank_reg_005 [annotator_5] (gold): model `` -> speaker `Agba ooo wọla ulẹ, ugwugwu dẹ / Ku do’mi wa kẹ mọ`. "gold: welcoming one visitor, the singular sit verb with the locative de"
  - ig_v42_gram_035 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Mẹ kó [yam] lẹ du t'ane [tree].` -> speaker `Mẹ kó [yam] lẹ jọ ofe [tree].`. "edit: after plural kó the second verb rewritten jọ (the plural set-down), the model's du removed: community leg for verb-verb concord (v4.4 wrote Mẹ kó [yam] lẹ jọ anẹ [tree] lẹ)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Minimal pairs with the noun held constant and only number changing (one child sits / the children sit; the pot is here / the pots are here; one goat is missing / the goats are missing), always with an inanimate pair so the verb is the only sign of number; a control sentence with a verb that must NOT change (one child eats / the children eat); commands to one person and to a group (sit down, wait here, come here). Avoid the frozen gold words child and pot in the prompt text if the Scope-A gate flags them; use elder, visitor, goat, basket.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Translate 'The visitor is sitting here' and 'The visitors are sitting here' into Igala. Does the verb change? Write both.
  - Tell one elder to sit down, then tell a group of elders to sit down, in Igala.
  - Translate 'Here is the basket' and 'Here are the baskets' into Igala, keeping the noun the same in both.
  - Translate 'One goat is missing' and 'Three goats are missing' into Igala, and 'The goat eats grass' and 'The goats eat grass'. Which verbs changed and which did not?
  - Tell a child to wait here, then tell three children to wait here, in Igala. Write the word for 'here' as you say it.
  - Translate 'The elder is seated under the tree, and the children are seated beside him' into one Igala sentence.

### rules-to-elicit 9. Object agreement du / kó and the agreeing second verb (tẽ/jọ, tọ/nyu/ru, tinyo/rinyo). The rule is served (L22 'du = take one, kó = take many'; rows 2890da97, 4c7b83ae, 42657b2c, 63fa3897) but no prompt has ever put a singular and a plural inanimate object through the same verb, so the speakers have never judged it: gold has du 154 times and kó with a plural object only in a handful of rows (ig_v42_lex_014 annotator_8 'ám'ẹnwu ku ma ko kì [child] wa ... ' for four school items), tinyo / rinyo 0 times in gold, and the one community edit on the pair is ig_v42_gram_035 (kó ... du rewritten kó ... jọ). Meanwhile a competing verb di appears in 8 speaker edits where the model or the prompt table had du (give, hand, take), which row 3b5fbaa6 lists as open.

- Rule a speaker would state: du takes one thing, kó several, the choice following the object; the second verb agrees with the first (du ... tẹ / kó ... jọ; du ... tinyo / kó ... rinyo; du ... tọ / kó ... nyu one place / kó ... ru several places); plural commands put mẹ before the verb; di is a different verb (open: give or hand over).
- Count: 0 judgments, 8 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes: L22 'du = take one, kó = take many'; rows 2890da97 (du/kó ... wa), 4c7b83ae (tinyo/rinyo, 'never make an r- form of any other verb'), 42657b2c (tẹ/jọ, tọ/nyu/ru), 63fa3897 (the verb carries the number of a bare noun), all served when retrieved. Grades: du/kó A (scholarship + corpus 137/77 + community thin); tinyo/rinyo B (scholarship + corpus 356/761, community 0); tẹ/jọ and tọ/nyu/ru B weak (scholarship + weak corpus). Missing: community for every pair but du/kó, and the di/du question has no scholarship. Speakers' answers add the community leg on inanimate plural objects (the only case where the verb alone carries number) and a verdict on di.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_035 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Mẹ kó [yam] lẹ du t'ane [tree].` -> speaker `Mẹ kó [yam] lẹ jọ ofe [tree].`. "edit: the second verb after plural kó made plural (jọ), the model's du removed"
  - ig_v42_lex_014 [annotator_8] (gold): model `[money] mi, ìchèkpúlù kà ki ọ́mami ónobùlẹ du ẹñwu ẹ̀lẹ̀ wá: fọmu, bokiti, tọchi kpai eku. (retrieval)` -> speaker `[money] mi, ám'ẹnwu ku ma ko kì [child] wa kì anyị kpaí kì ya gba nẹ: ọ'tìtìmù , Ibokiti, Tọ́chì, kpaí Ìgèdè`. "gold: four items, plural ko; the model's du with four objects was edited by annotator_3 to ... kẹ̀ la wá (du removed)"
  - ig_v42_gram_034 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `dù òkìtì kí dẹ àtè wá tẹ́fù [house].` -> speaker `di òkpẹti kí di ọkọlọ wá tẹ́fù [house].`. "edit: du replaced by di for 'take the stool and bring it inside' (one object)"
  - ig_bank_auth_018 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Nnọ, [god] k'i ch'ẹñwu unyẹdọ nwi wẹ.` -> speaker `[god] k'i di unyẹdọ nwi wẹ.`. "edit: 'may God give you consolation' with di; annotator_5 made the same edit (Nago, [god] k'i di unyẹdọ ñwu wẹ)"
  - ig_v42_gram_026 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `U gbà kẹkẹ wẹ lò t'ádṣa?` -> speaker `U di añya wẹ lò t'[market]?`. "edit: 'take your bicycle to the market' with di, not du (annotator_6: Agba, u neke du añya wẹ lo t'[market]?)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Same object noun, one vs several, same verb, in commands and statements: bring the plate / bring the plates; throw this one away / throw them all away; put the yams in one basket / in several baskets; and a mass noun (water, sand) to see which form the speakers use. Add 'take', 'give' and 'hand over' sentences to separate du from di. Every prompt yields two short sentences, so the pair is one edit away from a rule.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Translate 'Bring the plate' and 'Bring the plates' into Igala. Then 'Bring him' and 'Bring them'.
  - Translate 'Throw that bottle away' and 'Throw all those bottles away' into Igala, and then 'Pour the water away'.
  - Tell your brother in Igala to put the yams in one basket, then tell him to put them in several baskets.
  - Translate 'One coin is missing from the bag' and 'Several coins are missing from the bag' into Igala.
  - Translate 'Take this letter to the chief' and 'Give the children their food' into Igala. Is the verb for take the same in both? Write the verb you used for each.
  - Tell a group of children, all together, to carry the firewood home, in one Igala sentence.

### rules-to-elicit 10. The negator and the yes-no particle. The model writes the clause-final nasal as ǹ, n̄, or a detached n and sprinkles idle nasals; speakers rewrite it as ñ, -n or 'ñ (three community spellings of one morpheme) and delete the idle ones. 31 edits change the count of final nasals (29 remove or respell, 2 add). The yes-no question now has a community particle with three annotators: ba / bá (annotators 5, 6, 7) and a (annotator_7), with 0 corpus and 0 scholarship legs, and twice a speaker replaced the model's question-final nasal with ba.

- Rule a speaker would state: Negation is a single clause-final nasal written ñ (community also -n, 'ñ); an idle nasal changes the meaning; a yes-no question may close with ba (open: a / á), never with a nasal unless it is negative.
- Count: 2 judgments, 31 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Yes for the negator: L19 'Negation: ONLY a clause-final nasal, written ñ ... A nasal added for any other reason makes a different word'; REGISTER 'negative nasal written ñ'. The bare arm has no such line and is the main offender. Not told: the yes-no particle (row 3b5fbaa6 lists it as open, not served). Grades: negator B (corpus + community; the write-up's ń and SCL.NEG i̋ are a third spelling and a new claim, C); yes-no particle C (community only, now 3 annotators). Missing: scholarship for both, corpus for the particle (Bible yes-no questions have none). Speakers' answers add the particle's distribution (which questions, which speakers) and whether a nasal inside a question is ever interrogative rather than negative.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_gram_028 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Wẹ gbo ẹnwù kí n ka n?` -> speaker `Wẹ gbo ẹnwù ku ka ba?`. "edit: two idle nasals removed, the question closed with ba"
  - ig_v42_gram_027 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Amọ́ma kí wẹ nọ́ t'[house], aláfíya ma wà?` -> speaker `Amọ́ma kẹ tọnọ t'[house], chọ’lafía bá?`. "edit: yes-no question closed with bá (gold ig_bank_reg_006 annotator_7: [frozen answer], e dachi nyoyo ba?; ig_bank_auth_011 annotator_6: Abo akwọra [house] wẹ ch'ọlafiya ba?; annotator_7: Abọ akwora [house] wẹ a?)"
  - ig_v42_orth_006 [annotator_7] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `NYSC a kọ ọ. U n̄ kọ ẹkpa [name] ñwu n̄, todu amonẹ chakadu mọ ọ nyan̄ NYSC.` -> speaker `NYSC a kọ todu amonẹ chakadu mọ NYSC.`. "edit: both n̄ deleted as idle"
  - ig_bank_gram_019 [annotator_5] (gold): model `Ma á j'ẹñwu ñ. (v4.4)` -> speaker `Ma jẹñwu’ñ`. "gold for 'They are not eating': annotator_3 Mà jẹñwu ǹ, annotator_6 Ma jẹñwu-n, annotator_5 Ma jẹñwu’ñ: one morpheme, three spellings; v4.4 adds an incompletive á the speakers do not"
  - ig_v42_auth_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `u ma ọ́dọ́ ku ma bi mi ǹ.` -> speaker `i ma ọ́dọ́ ku ma bi mi ñ`. "edit: ǹ to ñ (annotator_7 on the bare arm: n̄ to ñ)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Yes-no questions and their negative answers in one task (Did the children eat? No, they did not eat), so the question particle and the negator appear side by side and cannot be confused; a negative question ('Are you not coming?'); and statements with a nasal-final word that is NOT a negator (to catch the model's idle nasal). Keep sentences short so the final particle is the only thing to judge.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Ask your sister whether the children have eaten, then write her answer: 'No, they have not eaten.' Both in Igala.
  - Translate 'Are you coming to the farm tomorrow?' and 'Are you not coming to the farm tomorrow?' into Igala.
  - Ask an elder politely whether he slept well, in one Igala question, as you would really say it.
  - Translate 'The goat did not die' and 'Did the goat die?' into Igala.
  - Translate 'I did not see him at the market, and I did not see his wife either' into Igala, in one or two sentences.

### rules-to-elicit 11. Clitic vs full pronoun, and the 3sg possessive. v4.4 writes the full or possessive 2sg form as subject ('Ugbo wẹ alo?') where all seven gold answers use the clitic ẹ ('Ugbo ẹ lo'); the Ejeba rule that clitics are the default and full pronouns are for emphasis, coordination or one-word answers is grade B and missing everywhere (jwal E-92-6). For 'her mother' v4.4 writes [mother] wn (the served -wn) while five of seven gold answers write a free word after the noun ([mother] ñwu annotators 3, 5, 7, 4; [mother] un annotator_8) and one writes [mother] wñ (annotator_6): the write-up's nwu as a free genitive word (W-3.2-8) has a community majority against the served suffix.

- Rule a speaker would state: Subject and object pronouns are short clitics (u, ẹ, i, a, mẹ, ma); the full forms (uwẹ, onwu, awa ...) appear only for emphasis, in 'X and I', or alone as an answer; 'his / her' after a noun is written as its own word ñwu (open: ñwu vs wñ vs -wn).
- Count: 1 judgments, 2 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Partly: L17 gives the clitic table (and 'he/she i|u|-wn') and the u rule; row 95132451 (community_verified, served when retrieved) states the clitic-default rule and the full forms. No prompt line says 'never a full pronoun before a verb'. Possessive: L17 '-wn' and Bible -wñ against the community's ñwu and the write-up's nwu. Grades: clitic default B (scholarship + community), the possessive spelling X (three forms). Missing: corpus for the clitic-vs-full distribution (Bible rarely has 1sg/2sg speech), community majority on the possessive written form. Speakers' answers add the possessive form from every annotator on one sentence and a clean test of emphasis ('me, I went' vs 'I went').
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_gram_021 [annotator_6] (gold): model `Ugbo wẹ alo? (v4.4)` -> speaker `Ugbo ẹlo`. "seven gold answers for 'Where are you going?': Úgbò ẹ lò (3), Ugbo ẹlo (6, 8), Ugbo ẹ loyi (5), Ugbọ ẹlọ? (7), ugbọ ẹlọ? (4): the clitic ẹ every time, never wẹ"
  - ig_bank_auth_001 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `[god] ki chẹnyọ ñwu wẹ` -> speaker ``. "Wẹ in igala is used for one person"
  - ig_bank_gram_018 [annotator_3] (gold): model `I du ùjẹu lẹ ñw'[mother] wn. (v4.4)` -> speaker `Ìmì [food] dù ñwì [mother] ñwù`. "gold for 'She gave the food to her mother': [mother] ñwu (annotators 3, 5, 7, 4), [mother] un (8), [mother] wñ (6): the possessive is a free word for five of six annotators"
  - ig_v42_lex_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ọ́yà, u á kà ukọla òókáà ñwẹ.` -> speaker `ọ́yà, na tene ku kọla káá ñwẹ.`. "Ú á ká ukọla óókaa - wrong / Na tene kọlá káá - right (the announcing first person is na, not u á)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Pairs where the same person is subject once plainly and once with emphasis or in coordination ('I went' vs 'Me, I went' vs 'You and I went'); a one-word answer ('Who broke it? Me.'); and three possessive sentences with the same possessor (his farm, her mother, their house) so the written form of 'his/her' is elicited repeatedly from every annotator.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Translate 'Where are you going?' and 'And you, where are YOU going?' into Igala.
  - Someone asks 'Who broke the pot?' Answer in Igala with one word: 'Me.' Then answer with a full sentence: 'I broke it.'
  - Translate 'You and I will go to Idah tomorrow' into Igala.
  - Translate 'She gave the money to her husband' and 'He gave the money to his wife' into Igala. Write the word for 'her' and 'his' exactly as you would write it.
  - Translate 'Their house is near the river, and his farm is behind it' into one Igala sentence.

### rules-to-elicit 12. The completive 'has finished V-ing' is V kpa mẹ (mé / ma) in the community: all seven gold answers for 'He has finished eating' end in jẹ kpa mẹ / jẹkpá mé / jẹ kpa ma / jẹ gba mẹ; v4.4 wrote 'I chẹ jẹñwu kpa.' (chẹ + verb + kpa, the perfect frame it uses elsewhere), the shape no speaker produced. Deduced R2.5 graded this C [thin] on 3 attestations; it now has 7 from 6 annotators and still no corpus or scholarship leg, and no prompt family targets aspect beyond two old translate prompts.

- Rule a speaker would state: Completed-and-finished action: verb + kpa + mẹ (also written mé, ma); the incompletive is preverbal á; the bare verb is simple past; chẹ before the verb is not the completive.
- Count: 0 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: No. L18 gives 'bare verb = completed; preverbal á = not yet complete' and nothing for 'finished'. Grade C, community only (now 7 gold, 6 annotators), corpus method blind to verb-final compounds, no scholarship. Speakers' answers on the designed prompts add the frame on other verbs (finished cooking, finished the work, finished talking) so a row can be seeded with more than 'eat', and test the past-progressive d'ọmọ frame a second time.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_gram_024 [annotator_8] (gold): model `I chẹ jẹñwu kpa. (v4.4)` -> speaker `Oñwù [food] jẹkpá mé`. "gold: annotator_6 I jẹ kpa ma; annotator_3 Ì fùjẹñwù jẹ kpà mẹ; annotator_5 i fi [food] jẹkpa mẹ; annotator_7 E fu [food] jẹ kpa mẹ; annotator_4 E fi ẹñwu jẹ gba mẹ"
  - ig_bank_gram_023 [annotator_6] (gold): model `Àmì ataja a wa kw'ef'[market] lẹ. (v4.4)` -> speaker `Ami añyaja kw'[market] awa`. "gold for 'The traders are coming from the market': the incompletive a before wa in all four gold (à wá, awa); the model's a is right, its noun and elision are not"
  - ig_bank_gram_025 [annotator_6] (gold): model `A dẹ a luchẹ egba kì [water] chanẽ a lọ. (v4.4)` -> speaker `A d'ọmọ aluchẹ ẹgba k'[water] chanẹ elọ`. "gold for 'We were farming when the rain started': d'ọmọ + a + verb for the past progressive (annotator_3 Á jì [money] à lùchẹ tàkòmì chànẹ)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Three-way aspect sets on one verb (she is cooking / she cooked / she has finished cooking) and 'finished' with an object (the work, the farm, the speech); one past progressive with a when-clause. One sentence each, translate shape, since gram_024 (translate) produced 7 of 7 agreeing gold.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Translate 'She is cooking', 'She cooked yesterday' and 'She has finished cooking' into Igala, one line each.
  - Translate 'The men have finished the work on the farm' into Igala.
  - Translate 'Have you finished eating? Yes, I have finished.' into Igala.
  - Translate 'The elder had finished speaking when the rain started' into Igala.
  - Translate 'The children were playing when their mother called them' into Igala.

### rules-to-elicit 13. Register strength (words too heavy or too light for the occasion, children's phrases in adult contexts). The write-up supplies no failing outputs and the data holds no explanation naming heaviness; the community leg is the addressee pairs in the old corrections (deduced R9.3: [child] na che yẹ' to a child vs 'Atai ẹ na che yẹ yẹ' to an elder) and the vocatives row c1cc5591. The nearest judgments: annotator_5 rejected the model's bare 'u á kà' announcement for the humble 'Na tene' frame (ig_v42_lex_010); annotator_8 on consolation wrote her own [god] kì danyedo-we ...' and marked both arms inadequate (ig_bank_auth_018), where the model opened with the Igbo Nnọ (annotator_5 elsewhere: 'Nnọ is igbo not igala', ig_v42_reg_012). Register prompts give 20 decisive of 40 pairs and only 3 informative explanations, so the weight dimension has never been elicited as such.

- Rule a speaker would state: Match word weight to the occasion and the addressee: formal announcements open with the humble na tẽnẹ frame and a vocative; consolation and condolence use the optative kì blessing, never a borrowed interjection; no children's phrasing in adult speech and no heavy words in light speech.
- Count: 40 judgments, 10 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: No weight line. Register is served through L30 (community voice), rows c1cc5591 (vocatives by addressee), 86fcc4d0 (consolation), de97cf41 (na tẹnẹ) when retrieved. Grade B for the addressee rule (community corrections + the write-up as scholarship); C for 'too strong / too childish' with no examples. Missing: the failing outputs themselves (ask Salem and Lydia), corpus (the Bible has no register pairs). Speakers' answers add the first explicit weight judgments if the prompt asks them to mark the word that is too heavy or too light.
- Evidence:
  - ig_v42_lex_010 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `ọ́yà, u á kà ukọla òókáà ñwẹ.` -> speaker `Na tene kọlá káá`. "Ú á ká ukọla óókaa - wrong / Na tene kọlá káá - right"
  - ig_bank_auth_018 [annotator_8] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `Nnọ, [god] k'i ch'ẹñwu unyẹdọ nwi wẹ.` -> speaker `[god] kì danyedo-we, e kiwedon, unekwu [god] ì chẹ , [god] lẹ ā áche nenwu duu,efedojẹ.`. "In Igala, the word for how an Igala speaker would naturally comfort someone who is grieving is ... (both arms inadequate; annotators 4 and 5 edited Nnọ out)"
  - ig_v42_reg_012 [annotator_5] (gemini-3-1-pro): model `Nnọ` -> speaker ``. "Nnọ is igbo not igala"
  - ig_bank_auth_027 [annotator_4] (gemini-3-1-pro-rag-v3): model `` -> speaker ``. "My child why did you take so long to come (gentle scolding of a child, decisive: the speaker glosses the winning answer)"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: One message, three addressees in one task (a child, a friend of your age, an elder) so the same content shifts form; serious-context prompts (a death, a land dispute, a court of elders) with the instruction to use adult weight; light-context prompts (teasing a friend, calling a child in) where heavy words would be wrong; and a judging prompt: the speaker is asked, in the explanation, to name any word in the model's answer that is too strong or too childish for the scene.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Tell a child, then a friend your age, then your grandfather, that dinner is ready. Three short Igala sentences.
  - A neighbour has lost his wife. Write the two sentences you would say to him at the door, in Igala, with the weight an adult uses, and say in one line which word carries the comfort.
  - Two families are disputing a boundary before the elders. Write the one Igala sentence the head of one family uses to open his case respectfully.
  - Tease a friend your age in Igala for arriving late to the farm, in a way that is warm, not harsh.
  - Ask your younger brother to turn the radio down: first in words you would use with a child, then in the words you would use if an elder were in the room.

### rules-to-elicit 14. The audio ask (write-up section 5): a product feature, not a prompt rule; no audio field or recorder exists in web/ (inventory W-5-3). It bears on bank design because the tone and dialect rules above are the ones audio would settle (unmarked tone is UNKNOWN, not mid, per Adeniyi via row 3ce5d469), and because recordable gold must be short, single-sentence and spoken-register.

- Rule a speaker would state: Not a grammar rule. Design the tone and dialect prompts so that the gold is one short spoken sentence a speaker could record in under ten seconds, and tag those prompts so the recording feature, when built, starts on them.
- Count: 0 judgments, 0 corrections
- Already told to v4.4: Not applicable to the model. Evidence class: none possible until recordings exist. Speakers' answers on short, recordable prompts add nothing by themselves; the prompt bank should mark the 20 to 30 tone and dialect prompts as 'record-ready' so the first audio pass has targets.
- Evidence:
  - ig_bank_gram_022 [annotator_3] (gold): model `` -> speaker `[food] kì [mother] mì hyì à rìnyọ`. "fully toned gold from the one heavy toner; annotators 5 and 6 wrote the same sentence untoned ([food] ki [mother] mi hi ariyọ / [food] k'[mother] mi yi arinyọ): only audio can say whether the three agree on the tune"
  - ig_bank_orth_029 [annotator_7] (gold): model `` -> speaker ``. "the Aug corrections volunteered kọ́ build vs kọ̀ refuse and [money] aeroplane vs [money] money (deduced R8.2), with three conflicting diacritic schemes across annotators: the X that audio resolves"
- Prompt shape that elicits it: Single-sentence gold, 4 to 8 words, in the speaker's own voice (greetings, commands, the minimal pairs), with a one-line instruction 'as you would say it aloud'; no lists, no explanations in the answer field.
- Example prompts the analyst proposed:
  - Say, as you would aloud, 'Good evening, mother, I have come back from the farm' in Igala, in one sentence.
  - Say aloud the Igala for 'to build' and 'to refuse', each in a three-word sentence.
  - Say in one Igala sentence, as you would to a visitor at your door, 'Welcome, sit down, I will bring you water'.
  - Say the Igala for 'Here is the money' and 'Here are the coins', one short sentence each.

**Shapes that produced decisive, explained judgments**

- Translate one quoted English sentence that contains exactly one target construction (a that-clause after say, a whose-relative, a plural object, a negation), with no 'note' or 'explain' clause: The translate shape has the highest edit yield of any shape (17 edits on 23 pooled pairs, 0.74 per pair) and produces convergent gold: 7 of 7 annotators agreed on the completive frame for ig_bank_gram_024, 7 of 7 on the clitic ẹ for ig_bank_gram_021, 6 of 7 on kakini / ka ki ni for ig_bank_gram_032. The speaker's edit is a word-level diff on the construction, which is what a rule needs. (ig_v42_gram_006, ig_bank_gram_024, ig_bank_gram_032, ig_bank_gram_021, ig_bank_gram_019)
- Scenario with a named speaker and addressee ('Your uncle needs an operation ... say it to his wife in one or two sentences'): The v42 scenario shape produced 145 decisive judgments and 122 edits on 278 pooled pairs (0.44 edits per pair, the most edits in absolute terms) and the explanations most often take the form 'X is wrong, Y is right' with Igala forms (annotator_5: 'Nẹ gbo is wrong / Ki gbo is correct'; 'Abíne -wrong / Agíní - right'). The cost is ties (102 of 278): annotator_8 ties and writes the answer in the explanation, which is still gold. (ig_v42_lex_010, ig_v42_lex_011, ig_v42_orth_005, ig_v42_auth_002, ig_v42_lex_014)
- Perform a short speech act in a fixed situation ('Write a natural Igala way of telling someone to eat well and be strong'): Perform prompts gave 127 decisive of 280 pairs, 93 informative explanations and 56 edits, and their gold is the community formula itself; ig_bank_auth_003 is the single most informative prompt in the data (4 decisive, 4 edits, 5 gold, explanations with the form). Works for register and formulas, not for grammar rules the speaker is not thinking about. (ig_bank_auth_003, ig_bank_auth_005, ig_bank_auth_001, ig_bank_auth_010, ig_bank_auth_019)
- Minimal pair in one prompt: two sentences that differ in one feature (one vs many; belief vs fact; one addressee vs another), both to be written: Not yet tried as a shape, but every rule in this report is a contrast, and the data shows the speakers answer contrasts when asked for a concrete pair (ig_bank_dial_014: 'idah Igala: [child] -[mother] mì / Ankpa Igala: [child]-ìya mì'; ig_bank_dial_017: seven regional pairs). Scoring: the model fails measurably if its two sentences do not differ where the speaker's do. (ig_bank_dial_014, ig_bank_dial_017, ig_bank_orth_029)
- Ask the speaker to name their own town or variety inside the answer: The dialect field is unset on 688 of 1,446 gold and is confounded with annotator (ankpa = annotator_8 only, other = annotator_3 only). Where speakers named areas themselves the answer was usable (ig_bank_dial_004 annotator_7 named Ogugu; ig_bank_dial_005 annotator_6 named Ibaji); where the prompt said 'note any variation' the pair was marked inadequate and nothing was learned. (ig_bank_dial_004, ig_bank_dial_005, ig_v42_gram_003)

**Shapes that produced ties and "both inadequate"**

- 'Give the Igala word for X and note whether it varies between areas / communities': Fifteen dialectal_fidelity prompts of this shape produced 33 both_inadequate, 11 ties and 12 decisive out of 56 pooled pairs, 0 explanations containing Igala and 3 edits; the 'note any variation' clause makes the model invent area attributions (Idah: Wá mímí / Ankpa: Wá málí) which the speakers reject wholesale ('They are both incorrect', 'Both are inadequate', 'Both output are not correct') without saying what is wrong. The gold channel still yielded forms (dial_004 had 6 gold), so the word itself is fine; the meta-clause is the waste. (ig_bank_dial_005, ig_bank_dial_004, ig_bank_dial_007, ig_bank_dial_011, ig_bank_dial_017)
- 'Write the Igala word for X with correct spelling and tone marks' on a single common noun: Thirty-five orthography prompts of this shape exist; gold on tone-requesting prompts is toned 84 of 242 times (35%) and the toned answers come from one annotator, so the model's tone competence is never measured against a stable target and the comparisons degrade to dot checks ('Agba [god] vs 'Àgbá [god] decided on the dot). Also most single-noun answers are frozen gold words, which blocks any reuse. (ig_bank_orth_001, ig_bank_orth_006, ig_bank_orth_022, ig_bank_auth_012)
- Long open prose tasks ('Describe the customs surrounding ...', 'Explain the role of ...') and multi-turn dialogues: Both arms produce 60 to 120 word answers with many ki and invented words; the speaker marks both inadequate or writes a paragraph of their own in the explanation (annotator_8's 'In Igala the word for ... is ...' blocks). Edits are rare (ig_bank_cult_038 is the exception) because a wholesale rewrite is not an edit; the rule signal is lost in the volume. ig_bank_auth_039 (elder and young person over a meal) is the least informative prompt in the data: 4 pairs, 0 decisive, 0 informative explanations, 0 edits. (ig_bank_auth_039, ig_bank_cult_038, ig_bank_auth_013, ig_bank_auth_034)
- Prompts that ask the model to explain, note, or comment in English alongside the Igala: The English half triggers task narration and respelled loans in the Igala half (ig_v42_lex_005, ig_v42_lex_027 'English note: I retained ...'), and speakers then edit the English out or tie; the Oct 8 checker rework was needed precisely because of these prompts. The write-up's rules are all testable with Igala-only answers. (ig_v42_lex_027, ig_v42_lex_005, ig_v42_orth_007, ig_v42_orth_008)

**What the speakers' own writing shows**

- contractions: 35% of train gold (510/1,446) carries an apostrophe contraction, by annotator: annotator_6 48% (130/273), annotator_3 37%, annotator_5 32%, annotator_7 35%, annotator_8 30%, annotator_4 25%. Edits run both ways (30 expand, 29 contract), the same phrase appears contracted and expanded from the same writers (k'ọla 10 vs ki ọla 5; k'[water] vs kì [water] vs Komi on ig_bank_gram_016), and nobody has corrected a contraction as such. v4.4 contracts in 58% of its answers, above every annotator.
- dialect: The gold dialect field is unset on 688 of 1,446 rows; general_idah 478 (annotators 4, 5, 6, 7, 8), ankpa 170 (annotator_8 only), other 107 (annotator_3 only), ogugu 2 (annotator_5), ibaji 1 (annotator_4). Variety is confounded with annotator identity. Speakers name areas spontaneously only on concrete word prompts (Idah vs Ankpa vowel in 'sibling', Ogugu 'come here', Ibaji 'how are you'); none has identified as Ogwugwu or Ibaji.
- ki usage: Standalone ki-forms appear in 314 of 1,446 gold (22%), 3.50 per 100 words; written ki 461 times, kì 139 (annotator_3 and annotator_8 mostly), kí 22. So the low tone is written sometimes, the high tone almost never, and the write-up's kí/kì contrast is unobservable in gold today. The linker after say/tell/know is kakini or ka ki ni (6 of 7 annotators on ig_bank_gram_032; also 'had we known' on gram_031). Relativizers in gold: kì / ki, ku before plural ma and also before 2sg wẹ (annotator_5 twice), ka (annotators 3, 6), ka ki (annotator_7).
- tone marks: 28% of gold (404/1,446) carries any tone mark; annotator_8 81% (304/376), annotator_3 19%, annotator_5 14%, annotator_7 8%, annotator_6 2%, annotator_4 6%. On the 70 prompts that ask for tone, 35% of gold is toned. Edits remove tone marks in 119 of 213 and add them in 23; the tone_marks failure tag is applied to outputs that HAVE marks (112 of 119 tagged), so it means wrong marks. When a speaker tones her own correction it is to carry a contrast (annotator_5: 'Na tene kọlá káá - right'). Dots (ẹ, ọ) are decisive where tone is not.
- other: Negator spelled ñ, -n, 'ñ or ǹ by different annotators for the same sentence (ig_bank_gram_019); the model's ǹ / n̄ is rewritten ñ. Yes-no questions close with ba / bá (annotators 5, 6, 7) or a (annotator_7); 'Where are you going' takes the clitic ẹ in 7 of 7 gold. Completive is V kpa mẹ / mé / ma (7 of 7). 'her mother' is [mother] ñwu (4 annotators), [mother] un (1), [mother] wñ (1): the possessive is a free word for most. gwugwu 'sit' appears in 11 gold rows (annotators 3, 5, 6, 8), emi 'here' in 6 (annotators 6, 7, 8). Loanwords: annotator_5 respells with Igala letters (ẹkẹnọmiks, paswọdú), annotator_6 substitutes Igala words (igede), annotators 4 and 7 keep English; names are kept by all. Judging styles: annotator_8 ties 88 of 129 pairs and writes the correct answer in the explanation (27 explanations with Igala forms); annotator_5 decides 94 of 110 and writes 'X is wrong / Y is right' pairs (34 informative, 15 with Igala); annotator_7 (172 pairs) marks 54 both_inadequate and 43 ties with generic explanations; annotator_6 marks 38 of 78 both_inadequate; annotator_3 decides 69 of 104.

**Open questions (speakers disagree)**

- Is the relativizer ka (annotators 3, 6: 'Ẹnẹkẹlẹ ka lo t'[money] ñwu lekwu', [father] kà lò') the same item as kì, a tone variant, or the ka of ka ki ni? And what conditions ku before 2sg wẹ (annotator_5: 'kú wẹ rá', 'kú wẹ ma ch'íya')? The served 'ku before plural ma/me' is too narrow.
- Does the community distinguish the belief (kí) and fact (kì) readings of the write-up's 9a/9b and 10a/10b at all, and if so by tone, by a different word, or by nothing? No gold writes kí after a verb of saying; kakini covers that slot. Three annotators judging the pairs blind is the gate the inventory set.
- Is kakini / ka ki ni also the linker after know, hear and be glad (gram_031 says yes for know), or only after say and tell? And does a noun complement ('the news that') take kakini or kí?
- gwùgwú: the community uses it (11 gold rows, 4 annotators) against 0 Bible hits. Is it a spoken-register item the Bible avoided, and is 'gwu ane' (annotator_8's short regional variant) a different area's form?
- Presentative yì: is there any community sentence 'X lẹ tẹ yì / X lẹ jọ yì'? Gold yi is 'this' (iko yi, [food] yi); the locative in gold is de. If no annotator produces yì on the designed 'here is / here are' prompts, the row stays a note.
- The 3sg possessive: [mother] ñwu (4 annotators), [mother] un (1), [mother] wñ (1), served -wn, Bible -wñ, write-up nwu. Which written form should the prompt teach, and is the free word ñwu the same morpheme as the dative ñwu?
- di vs du: 8 edits put di where the model or the prompt table has du (give, hand, take the stool, take the bicycle). Is di a distinct verb (give / hand over) and does it agree for number like du / kó?
- Loanwords: three annotators, three strategies (respell with ch and Igala vowels; replace with an Igala word; keep English). Does the split follow the age of the loan (asibiti, státì kept with s; psychology respelled) or the annotator? The designed three-way prompts should decide before any prompt line is written.
- Yes-no particle ba / bá / a: three annotators, no corpus leg, the write-up silent. Is it the same particle as the Yoruba-looking bí the model sometimes writes, and is it obligatory?
- Which variety is 'Central'? Idah by gold volume (478 general_idah), Dekina by Ejeba's data, Ankpa by the heaviest toner. The one-variety line needs a default and a detector; neither can be built until gold rows carry a speaker-named variety.
- Ogwugwu: is dialects.ts 'ogugu' the write-up's Ogwugwu, does any current annotator speak it, and is ra 'buy' correct there? Until answered the NEVER WRITE ban on ra is dialect-blind.
- Tone standard: with one annotator producing 75% of all toned gold, should the tone prompts be scored per annotator, and is 'tone the meaning-bearing monosyllables' acceptable to Salem as the served compromise?
- Register strength: Salem and Lydia name no failing outputs. Which words were too heavy and which children's phrases broke adult contexts? Without examples only the three-addressee prompts can surface the dimension.
- Scope-A gate: the write-up's example sentences contain bracketed frozen gold words (child, pot). Every prompt text that quotes write-up Igala must pass scripts/static-leak-check before seeding; the English-only versions of the same contrasts are the safe default.
